# Known issues and defects

27 September 2026. Concrete things that **are** wrong, in our artifacts or in
the environment we depend on. Forward-looking risks live in
[12-RISKS.md](12-RISKS.md); the boundary is that an issue has already happened
and can in principle be reproduced.

Closed entries are kept deliberately. Several of them are retracted
conclusions, and a register that quietly drops its own errors is worth less than
one that carries them — three of the entries below are places where a confident
claim in this repository turned out to be wrong, and knowing that is part of
calibrating the rest.

**Status values.** `open` · `fix known` (root cause understood, not applied) ·
`fixed` (corrected in this repo) · `worked around` (living with it) ·
`documented` (environment behaviour, not ours to fix) · `closed` (a wrong claim,
retracted and corrected).

---

## Open

| ID | Title | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I-01** | CP's `MSG` macro fails under z390 *(z390 only)* | `AZ390E error 35, expression parsing error` on lines like `MSG 'STORAGE IS VIRTUAL=REAL'`. `MSG.MACRO` does substring and length arithmetic on a quoted operand (`'&ARG2'(1,1) NE ''''`, `K'&ARG2-2`) and z390's expression parser disagrees with HLASM. **One macro blocking ~20 modules, including `DMKPGS`, `DMKBLD` and `DMKPTR` — three of the five DAT modules.** **Reclassified 27 Sep: this is a z390 dialect artifact, not a CP defect.** `DMKPTR` and `DMKBLD` each carry an `MSG` call and both assemble clean under CE's Assembler XF, so HLASM parses `K'&ARG2-2` the way CP intends and z390 does not. No longer the highest-leverage item, because it blocks nothing on the native build. | **z390 only** |
| **I-02** | `DMKCPI` source encoding | `MZ390E error 138, invalid ascii source line 365` — the U+E000 private-use encoding the CE README describes. Applies only to some members; `DMKVAT` and `CORE` are clean. **Does not exist on the native build:** CE's disks hold native EBCDIC, so the private-use encoding is an artifact of the exported UTF-8 text tree and `DMKCPI` — which carries one such character — assembles clean on Assembler XF. It matters only to tools reading the text tree. | fix known |
| **I-03** | `DMKDSP` duplicate `USING` ranges *(z390 only)* | `MNOTE 4, 'Duplicate USING ranges found for - 2 and 0 using highest'`. z390 escalates what HLASM tolerates. Confirmed by execution 27 Sep: `DMKDSP` has 44 `USING` statements and assembles clean on CE's Assembler XF. | **z390 only** |
| **I-05** | 59 macro names never cross-checked against z390 directives | `TRACE` was found because it failed. Any CP macro name colliding with a z390 directive behaves identically but may not fail — producing a false clean assembly. The cross-check is mechanical and has not been run. Mitigation for R-13. **Value downgraded 27 Sep**: with all four z390 failure classes shown to be dialect artifacts ([16-NATIVE-BASELINE.md](16-NATIVE-BASELINE.md)), z390 is a convenience syntax checker rather than an authority, so a false clean result from it no longer misleads anything that matters. Worth doing if z390 is used again in anger. | open |
| **I-255** | IPL of a dedicated DASD: the first user CCW looked mistranslated | M7.10, 10 Oct. **Not a CP bug -- ours.** CP's own `TRACE CCW` showed the virtual CCW at X'08' as `60000090 08003000`: the IPL record was shifted by four bytes, because a CDL volume's records 1 and 2 carry a 4-byte key ('IPL1', 'IPL2') that `vmzipl` did not write (the dasd driver puts key and data from the front of the block). Fixed in `vmzipl`. Two real CP changes came out of it: DMKCCW keeps DEDDTBL while DMKVMI runs an ESA/390 IPL (OTHRTBL cannot follow its SEARCH-TIC loop), and DMKCFG puts DMKVMI at X'FFF000' for an ESA/390 IPL of 16 MB or more. `CP SET ESA ON`, `CP IPL 250` now boots Debian from its disk (run zp12). | **closed** |
| **I-256** | ESA/390 guest: zero fullwords at X'38', X'48' or X'50' of guest pages | M7.10, 10 Oct. Found by simulating the disk's IPL channel program offline (`dasdcopy` + a CKD reader): 2 to 4 blocks of a 7 MB file Linux wrote differed from the source by a zero fullword at page offset X'38', X'48' or X'50' -- the S/370 I/O old PSW, CAW and interval timer. Part fixed: an ESA/390 guest's SSCH planted its CAW at guest X'48' for DMKVSI; it now goes in DMKVCSCW (DMKVSI VSIO, DMKVCS). Still seen after that (zp12: X'50' and X'3B'), so another store remained. Found (zp16 memtest, 64 MB guest, 40 MB of X'A5': 2-3 pages a round, always X'50'): DMKPTR's page-in brings "virtual 80 up to date" when the page is page 0, tested with XPAGNUM = X'00FFF000' -- 24 bits -- so every page at a multiple of 16 MB counted as page 0 and got VMTIMER at its X'50'. This hit 31-bit CMS users above 16 MB too. Fixed (XA0054DK): a 31-bit mask, and both page-0 location-80 updates (PAGEINC, SGXNS) skip for an ESA/390 guest, which has no location-80 timer. Verified zp17: 8 rounds of 40 MB, 0 bad bytes; the Asterisk packages that failed in zp16 (libcurl3) then installed cleanly. | **closed** |

| **I-257** | The REXX full-screen package (FSEWRITE ...) and FULIST fail under the EC CMS (`IPL 290`): `DMSSVT121S UNSUPPORTED SVC 201` | 10 Oct. The package (RXFNCMGR on 19E) waits for the 3270 with its own S/370 **BC** PSWs: it puts a BC I/O new PSW at X'78', loads a BC wait PSW, and resumes with `LPSW X'38'`. Its next SVC 202 then comes from BC mode: the SVC old PSW is BC, the code is in it, and X'8A', which the EC CMS reads (XA0051DK), still held 201 from DMSITS's own CMSRET SVC. DMSITS took the SVC for an OS SVC 201. Found with CP TRACE ALL PRINTER: the return PSW `FF040000 60E7C62C` (byte 1 = 04: BC). Fixed (DMSITS XA0051DK, SVCINT): a BC SVC old PSW is made an EC PSW at entry -- code and ILC to X'8A'/X'89', CC and program mask to byte 2, channel masks to the EC I/O mask -- and the caller is resumed in EC mode. Verified fx3: FULIST under 290 (list, PF5, PF8, browse, edit); IPL CMS unchanged; CHAT under 290. | **closed** |
| **I-258** | MECAFF (EE, FSVIEW, FSLIST) under the EC CMS: specification exception | 10 Oct. MECAFF (C, fsio.c) and the full-screen package enable with `SSM =X'FF'`; in an EC PSW bits 0 and 2-4 must be zero and bit 5 is DAT, so CP reflected a specification exception (EE$S at 0547E0). Fixed in CP (DMKPRV XA0054DK, SETMASK): for an EC-mode System/370 machine (not ESA/390), a system mask with any of bits 0, 2-4 set is taken as a BC mask -- channel masks 0-6 become the EC I/O mask, bit 7 stays external; a valid EC mask is unchanged. A deviation from S/370, where such an SSM is a specification exception, kept to the case no EC program uses. Verified fx3: FSVIEW and EE under 290 and under IPL CMS. | **closed** |
| **I-265** | **Linux from the reader (`LINUX READER`, `CP IPL 00C` of the kernel deck) ends in a PROGRAM INTERRUPT LOOP** | 11 Oct. Also with a nucleus from before any of 11 October's changes, and with a 15 MB machine (so not M7.10's DMKVMI placement). After the IPL, guest location 0 holds `02000140 800003C0` -- a CCW of the second card, not the IPL PSW `00080000 800003C0` -- and the CCW at X'08' shows X'FF' in its zero byte. The `INVALID OPTION - KEEP` that comes first appears to be the Linux IPL code's own DIAG 8 (a z/VM spool option) and harmless. Disk IPL (`CP IPL LINUX`, the standard since M7.10) is not affected. | **fixed** 11 Oct — the cause: DMKVSP's page-crossing move took the next page's address from `L R1,VSPCCW` (the CCW word, op code in byte 0) and `LA R1,1(R15,R1)` kept that byte in AMODE 31; TRANS masks to 24 bits only for a 24-bit guest, so for Linux the part of every card past a 4 KB boundary went 32 MB too high and the kernel image had holes (operation exception at X'10000', then the loop through an unset program new PSW). Found by comparing guest storage after the IPL loader with the kernel image. XA0055DK: `N R1,XRIGHT24`. Verified: `LINUX READER` boots to the login prompt. (`INVALID OPTION - KEEP` is the kernel's own `CHANGE RDR ALL KEEP NOHOLD`; harmless) |
| **I-266** | **`CP IPL LINUX` gives every user the same IP address** | 11 Oct. The address is in the disk's own kernel parameters (vmzipl), so a second user's copy comes up as 10.1.1.2 on a CTC whose PC side is 10.1.2.1. LXPARM sets it per user only on the reader path (I-265). Needs a per-user parameter for the disk IPL (IPL PARM, or the initramfs asking CP for the userid). | **fixed** 11 Oct (9.12a) — the address follows the CTC pair: attached as 62x/63x (x its offset in the 06xx block) or at its real address, pair 06xx gives 10.1.n.2 with n = xx/2 + 1, which is what the PC end of that pair (Hercules CTCI) is set to. `/usr/local/sbin/vm370plus-net` on the Debian disks (run from rc.local; also in vm370plus-post.sh) and the same rule in the initramfs for the next kernel build |

## Environment — documented, not ours to fix

| ID | Title | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I-13** | Hercules 3.13 will not link with modern gcc | `softfloat` declares `float_exception_flags` and `float_rounding_mode` `__thread`; libtool's generated symbol table references them as non-TLS, so `ld` fails with `TLS definition … mismatches non-TLS reference` — reported misleadingly as `libsoftfloat.a: error adding symbols: bad value`. Dropping `__thread` links cleanly. Lab-only; see R-21. | worked around |
| **I-14** | `--disable-shared` breaks the device modules | Statically linking every device module collides, because they all define `hdl_depc`, `hdl_init` and `hdl_ddev`. Configure with the default shared modules. | documented |
| **I-15** | Device modules must be installed, not just built | Running `hercules` from the build tree gives `HHCCF042E Device type 3215-C not recognized`, because `hdt1052c` is not in `/usr/local/lib/hercules`. With no device attached there is no subchannel 0000, so `SSCH` correctly returns cc=3 and test 6 reports `000C03` — which looks exactly like a real SSCH failure. `make install` first. | documented |
| **I-16** | OPERATOR cannot IPL CMS under CE | Its directory entry gives it 2 MB, while CE's CMS saved system has shared segments at 15.5 MB, so the segments fall outside the machine. `cp define storage 16m` first, or log on as MAINT (`15M 16M`). | documented |

## Closed

| ID | Title | Description | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I-17** | `TRACE` collides with a z390 directive | z390's built-in `TRACE` shadowed CP's macro and z390 tried to resolve `CODE=` as a symbol. Fixed by renaming to `TRACZ` — and the rename **must be the same length**: the first attempt, `CPTRACE`, pushed each line's `@V40759` change marker into column 72 and the macro began failing with "continuation line < 16 characters". This is where R-04 comes from. | fixed |
| **I-18** | First `XAOPS` validation proved nothing | z390's built-in instruction definitions shadowed the macros under test, so the validation exercised z390 rather than `XAOPS`. Fixed by X-prefix renaming throughout `macros/validate/`. | fixed |
| **I-19** | `snt-collisions.py` assumed `SYSHRSG` starts where `SYSPGNM` starts | `CMSOLD` broke the assumption. Fixing it is what revealed the segment-0 finding, so the bug was productive. | fixed |
| **I-20** | Storage-key negative control broke the guard, not the guarantee | The first negative control for `11-storage-keys.rc` patched `ISKE` to read the wrong frame, which tests the guard rather than bypassing it — giving `001201` instead of the `001203` a real bypass would produce. | fixed |
| **I-21** | `RSCH` opcode "dispute" | Recorded as a genuine conflict between Hercules (`B238`) and a reading of the 370-XA Reference Summary (`B23B`). There was no dispute: z390's table gives `B238 RSCH, B239 STCRW, B23A STCPS, B23B RCHP`. `B23B` is RESET CHANNEL PATH, a different instruction. My error, not the sources'. | closed |
| **I-22** | "Hercules cannot prototype the `SIO` gap" | Claimed that `ORB5_I` was ignored, so the `SIO` condition-code contract could not be tested before M1. Wrong: initial-status interruption has been in Hercules since **version 1.39, 24 November 1999**. `06-initial-status.rc` tests it and passes. Retracted in three documents. | closed |
| **I-23** | "Segment 15 is the false alarm" | `04-SHARED-SEGMENTS.md` concluded that the segment-15 collision was benign, resting on saved-page analysis, with one caveat it could not check. Reading the real `USER DIRECT` closed that caveat **against** the conclusion: eight machines default to 15 MB, two to 14 MB, `XNET` to 16 MB, and 21 can be defined to 16 MB, so private storage routinely occupies exactly where CMS's shared segments sit. Corrected in `04`, `05` and `11`. | closed |
| **I-04** | `XATEST` listing never checked | Seven of twelve `XAOPS` encodings came from z390's opcode table rather than from execution, and the listing that would confirm them had never been produced. Now produced by CE's own Assembler XF and saved as `macros/validate/XATEST-CE-XF.LISTING`; all twelve object codes read off it and correct. See [14-M0-CLOSED.md](14-M0-CLOSED.md). | fixed |
| **I-26** | Duplicate spool file from `START` plus `devinit` | `CP START 00C` un-drains the real reader *and* triggers a read; a following `devinit` of the same device read the same file again, queueing two identical spool files. The second `READCARD` took the duplicate, so `XATEST ASSEMBLE` landed on disk as a 194-record copy of `XAOPS MACRO` and failed with `IFO047 UNEXPECTED END OF FILE ON SYSTEM INPUT` — a plausible-looking macro failure that was nothing of the kind. Fix: `devinit` once per new deck, and always check `LISTFILE`'s record count against the deck size. | fixed |
| **I-08** | M1's exit criterion is satisfiable by a hung CP | M1 is defined as "CP IPLs in ESA/390 mode and writes one console message". Test 7 proved that a CP which gets `DMKIOS` right and omits CR6 **prints exactly that message and then hangs forever**, with nothing in any log. The milestone as worded passes on a dead system. Needs strengthening to require progress *past* the message — a second message, or a line of console input, which puts `DMKCNSIN` on the path.  **Fixed 27 Sep: M1 now requires CP to write its message **and then accept a command typed at the console**, which puts `DMKCNSIN` on the path so the interruption must genuinely have been delivered.** | fixed |
| **I-09** | M2 names no observable | "DAT on with ESA/390 tables, no guests" may not describe a runnable state: CP's own execution is largely real-mode, and the tables it builds are for virtual machines. With nothing to dispatch, nothing translates, so M2 either has no observable or collapses into M3. Needs an explicit one — e.g. `DMKBLD` builds a table set and `TRANS` returns a correct above-the-line real address for a virtual address in it, self-checked inside CP.  **Fixed 27 Sep: M2’s criterion is now that `DMKBLD` builds a table set and `TRANS` returns the correct above-the-line real address for a virtual address in it, self-checked inside CP — testable with no guest, and it fails loudly if `LRA` is being asked a truncated question.** | fixed |
| **I-10** | M3 bundles two independent risks | M3 contains both "a guest logs on and runs CMS" and "frame-level shared segments in `DMKATS`", so a failure has two candidate sources — the thing the milestone ladder exists to prevent. The split is already available in the machinery: **`IPL 190` by device address needs no `DMKSNT`, no `DMKATS` and no shared segments**, while `IPL CMS` by saved-system name needs all three. Proposed M3a / M3b.  **Fixed 27 Sep: split three ways. **M3a** CP IPLs from DASD, **M3b** a guest runs CMS via `IPL 190` by device address, **M3c** `IPL CMS` by name with frame-level sharing.** | fixed |
| **I-11** | IPL-from-DASD is owned by no milestone | M1 explicitly defers `DMKCKP`, `DMKSAV` and the whole IPL path under strategy B, and no later milestone picks them up. A CP that can only be started by poking storage from the Hercules panel is a lab artifact, not a system. Those three modules also carry 70 of the 82 DAT references M1 avoided. See R-09.  **Fixed 27 Sep: it is **M3a**, with `R-09` mapped to it.** | fixed |
| **I-12** | CMS stage 2 has no milestone number | The stated goal — an application with an 18 MB heap — is unreachable through M0–M4, all of which leave every guest at 16 MB. The CMS work is surveyed and measured but unnumbered. See R-10.  **Fixed 27 Sep: it is **M5**, with `R-10` mapped to it, and it is marked as the only milestone that delivers the stated goal.** | fixed |
| **I-06** | `dat4b.py` fills unused PTEs with `X'20'` | `INVALID = 0x20` was used for **both** segment-table and page-table entries in `dat4a.py` and `dat4b.py`. Worse than the cosmetic issue first recorded: a PTE with `X'20'` set has bit `X'400'` **clear**, so the hardware reads it as a *valid* entry with page-frame real address zero — every unmapped page in the segment quietly aliased real page 0. Split into `STE_INVALID` (`X'20'`) and `PTE_INVALID` (`X'400'`) in both scripts, with the reasoning in the source. Tests 4a and 4b re-run and still pass `006004` and `006005`. | fixed |
| **I-07** | `herc.conf` carries an invalid codepage name | `herc.conf` now carries `CODEPAGE 819/1047`, which both 3.07 and 3.13 accept. The old `819-1047` was rejected non-fatally by 3.13, so the run continued on the default and the error was easy to miss entirely. | fixed |
| **I-37** | `XAOPS` in its own `XALIB` is invisible to CP's build | The first `DMKIOS` assembly failed with `IFO078 UNDEFINED OP CODE` for `SSCH` and `TSCH` while every other new symbol resolved. `DMKLCL.CNTRL`'s `MACS` record is `DMKLCL DMKHRC DMKMAC DMSLCL CMSHRC CMSLIB OSMACRO` and `VMFASM` globals exactly that list, so a macro library CP's control file does not name is never searched however well it was built — M0 built `XALIB`, which CP cannot see. Fixed by putting `XAOPS` into `DMKLCL MACLIB`, which is what that library is for. | fixed |
| **I-38** | Killing Hercules while CP is quiescent does not force a cold start | `docs/11-RUNNING-CE.md` says a killed run always leaves the warm-start area inconsistent and the next IPL must cold-start. Too strong. After a `SIGTERM` delivered while Hercules sat idle in a `pause`, the next IPL **did not prompt at all** — straight from banner to `DMKCPI966I Initialization complete` in one second, with `FILES: 005 RDR` showing the spool intact — so the `/cold drain` in the `.rc` arrived afterwards and drew `?CP: COLD`. The observable rule: **CP prompts when it needs an answer.** An unconditional `/warm` or `/cold` in an `.rc` is at best ignored, so the log must be checked rather than the answer assumed. Mechanism not fully established from one observation. | open |
| **I-36** | `PSA` serves both CP's lowcore and a guest's page 0 | Every `X-PSA(,R2)` site uses a `PSA` field name as a **displacement into a virtual machine's page 0**, not into CP's real lowcore — 300+ such sites across 41 modules, 47 in `DMKDSP` alone. Guests stay S/370-mode through M4, so those displacements must keep their S/370 meaning while CP's own lowcore becomes ESA/390. Of the renamed fields exactly **three** are used that way: `DMKDSP` `INTTIO-PSA-1(R2)`, `DMKPRV` `CHANID-PSA(0,R2)`, `DMKCCH` `ECSWLOG-PSA(4,R2)`. Two are already correct — `S370CHID` and `S370ECSW` are the right names for guest lowcore — and the third gets `G370TIO`. Not anticipated by any earlier document. | fixed |
| **I-39** | ~~The analysis tree is a snapshot of unknown vintage~~ | Closed by reading the tree's own `UPSTREAM.md`, which I had not opened. `/home/claude/vmce` **is** `systems/vmce` copied out of the monorepo, and it is dated and hashed: imported **20 September 2026** from `VM370CE.V1.R1.2.zip`, archive SHA-256 `7772e914…`, with a 630-member native-`UPDATE` comparison against CE's own disks and per-member `bootstrap_sha256`. The absent `.git` is an artefact of copying a subdirectory, not evidence of a stray extraction. It is better provenance than a bare checkout would have given. | closed |
| **I-40** | `RCH370` is now set on every channel, permanently | `DMKIOG`'s survey only ever resets `RCHTYPE`'s `RCH370` flag on the 2860/2870/2880 paths, and `XA0009DK` makes those unreachable, so the flag stays set for every `RCHBLOK`. Its only two consumers are the `HDV` sites in `DMKIOS` (`seq 00302000`, `01284000`), which therefore both take the "let the channel do the work" branch statically. That is the branch `HSCH` wants, so this **simplifies** the outstanding M1 step 4 work rather than complicating it — but it is a static truth now, not a runtime choice, and the dead alternatives should not be converted. | documented |
| **I-41** | A deck read through CP's card reader needs an ID card | Two ten-minute runs were lost to `READER EMPTY OR NOT READY`, which reads like a device fault and is not one. An `UPDATE` deck spooled through the real reader must begin with `ID MAINT NAME <fn> <ft>` or CP has no user to spool the file to: `CP START 00C` reports `RDR 00C STARTED SYSTEM` and `Q RDR ALL` still says `NO RDR FILES`. The working run19 card files had the ID card; I restaged run20 and run21 by copying the raw decks and dropped it. Two diagnostic misreads followed — first the printer purge, then the `START`/`devinit` ordering — neither of which was the cause. Fixed permanently by `tools/mkrun.py`, which generates the card files and the rc together and cannot omit the ID card. | fixed |
| **I-42** | `READCARD` takes the first entry of `Q RDR ALL` | Mike's correction, and it retro-weakens a claim of mine: run19's four `READCARD`s succeeded with 6 stale spool files already queued, so the decks it read were the right ones by ordering luck, not by construction. The assemblies it verified are still sound — `DMKDSP` and `DMKPRV` would have flagged the old symbols had the wrong deck landed — but the method was unsound. Every run now purges the reader first. | fixed |
| **I-43** | `DMKCCH` has a fourteenth site I never catalogued | CE found it, not me: `STH R2,INTTIO` at seq 00524000, flagged after `XA0010DK` took the other thirteen. I had listed DMKCCH's sites by grepping for the logout symbols only, and read straight past it while printing the region that contains it. It is reachable — DMKCCH returns to DMKIOS even with `TERMSYS` set — and R2 holds `SIOADDR`, a device address, being stored into what is now `IOSCHNO`, a subchannel number. That is the exact substitution the no-alias rename was designed to make impossible, so the deck drops the store rather than translating it; a correct implementation restores the SSID from `RDEVSSID`, which is M1 step 5 work in `DMKIOT`. **The lesson is the method, not the site**: a per-module site list built by grepping for the symbols I expected will miss the ones I did not. `status.py` scans for all five renamed symbols and had DMKCCH's count right; my hand list did not. | fixed |
| **I-44** | "Storage keys are the cheap win" — wrong | I told Mike the 67 storage-key sites were the one low-risk win available, on the strength of `ISKE`/`SSKE`/`RRBE` being valid in all three architectures. That part holds and is genuinely valuable — the work can be tested on CE as it runs today. But the sites are not mechanical: CP keys a 4 KB page as **two 2 KB halves** (`ISK R1,R14  STORAGE KEY FOR 1ST HALF OF PAGE` / `INSERT 2ND KEY`, `RRB 0(R6)` / `RRB 2048(R6)`), so the pairs must collapse rather than translate; `RRB`→`RRBE` moves the address from a base-displacement operand into a register, needing a spare register at 13 sites; and a third of the family is in `DMKPTR`, the page manager. See [23-STORAGE-KEYS.md](23-STORAGE-KEYS.md). I then overstated the compensating find as well, and Mike caught it: the 24-bit mask applies to a **real** address, because keys belong to real frames, so it does not block a 31-bit CMS at all — DAT creates the guest's address space and its frames can all sit below the line. The family is a prerequisite for CP using real storage above 16 MB, which is a different and later goal. Answering the question also turned up work I had not counted: guest keys live in `SWPKEY1`/`SWPKEY2` in the swap table, and `DMKPRV` simulates only X'08', X'09' and X'B213' — a 31-bit guest issuing `ISKE`/`SSKE`/`RRBE` finds no case there. | closed |
| **I-45** | Update control cards must be in ascending anchor order | `XA0010DK` failed with `SEQUENCE NUMBER '00524000' NOT FOUND` for a record that is plainly there — line 538 of CE's own `394/DMKCCH.ASSEMBLE`, sequence `00524000`, and no applied PTF deck touches the region. The cause is the deck, not the file: `UPDATE` reads the source once, forward, so a control card whose anchor precedes the previous card's is reported as missing rather than as out of order. I had appended the new 00524000 edit after the 00780400 and 00801000 ones. The message sends you hunting through the base file and ten PTF decks for a record sitting in front of you; it cost a ten-minute run and a wrong hypothesis about the snapshot padding sequence numbers. `mkdeck.Deck` now refuses a non-ascending anchor and says why. | fixed |
| **I-46** | `DMKPRV` cannot simulate the extended key instructions | Its entry points are `DMKPRVEK` for X'08' `SSK` and `DMKPRVIK` for X'09' `ISK`, plus `CLI VMINST,X'B2'` for `RRB`. A guest that issues `ISKE` (B229), `RRBE` (B22A) or `SSKE` (B22B) has no case in the simulator, so **M5 requires DMKPRV work that none of the 67-site count covers**. Guest keys are software state in `SWPKEY1`/`SWPKEY2` — "VIRTUAL STORAGE KEY, 1ST/2ND 2048 BYTES" — so the 2 KB pairing is in the data structure as well as the instruction stream, and collapsing to a single 4 KB key changes `SWPTABLE`. | open |
| **I-47** | CP copies the IPL device address into hardware-defined storage | Four of the eighteen `INTTIO` sites are not hardware reads at all: `DMKVMI`, `DMKCKP` and `DMKDMP` write the IPL device address into X'BA' to mimic what a real I/O interruption would have left there — `DMKVMI`'s BC-mode sibling one instruction earlier is `STH R13,IPLPSW+2`, the same value into the BC-mode PSW. CP already has the right field: `SYSIPLDV`, in the PSA, set by `DMKCPI` at seq 00484000 and read in six places. `DMKCKP` 00274360/70 reads `SYSIPLDV` and then copies it into `INTTIO` on the next line. So three sites become `SYSIPLDV` and one is deleted — no translation needed. Under ESA/390 X'BA' is inside the subsystem-identification word, so this was a corruption waiting for the rename to expose it. | fixed |
| **I-48** | `DMKCKP` and `DMKDMP`'s `INTTIO` sites cannot be done before their channel I/O | Both modules do standalone I/O and would set their own interruption parameter in the ORB of their own `SSCH`, so the `INTTIO` read and the `SIO`/`TIO` conversion are the same change. Splitting them means two decks per module and an intermediate state where the module reads a parameter its own code never sets. Revises the agreed order: `DMKVMI` and `DMKIOT` are INTTIO work, `DMKCKP` and `DMKDMP` belong to the bootstrap chain and get one deck each there. See [24-INTTIO.md](24-INTTIO.md). | documented |
| **I-49** | S/370-only instructions hand-coded as `DC` constants | `tools/s370only.py` scans the opcode column, so it cannot see an instruction written as data. Six live sites were invisible to it: `CONCS` (`DC X'B2001000'`) in `DMKCPI`, `DMKCPP` and `DMKMCT`; `DISCS` (`B201`) in `DMKCPP` and `DMKMCT`; and `CLRCH` (`DC X'9F01'` followed by `DC S(0(1))`) in `DMKVSJ`. Each carries the mnemonic in a commented-out line above it, which is how they were found. One further match, `DMKVSI`'s `TCHOPER DC X'9F00'`, is a **comparison operand** (`CLC VMINST(2),TCHOPER`) and not executed — nothing mechanical distinguishes the two, so the tool now reports DC matches with a hand-verified verdict rather than counting them silently. `CONCS`/`DISCS` are channel-**set** instructions with no ESA/390 counterpart at all: the channel subsystem replaced channel sets, so they must be removed, not translated. | fixed |
| **I-50** | CE generates and enables Attached Processor support | `OPTIONS.COPY` line 56 has `&AP SETB 1` applied by `HRC035DK`, and `DMKSYS`'s `AIF (NOT &AP).E1` therefore assembles `SYSCOR RMSIZE=16384K,AP=YES`. So `DMKCPI`'s runtime gate — `CLI N0(R2),YES  IS SYSTEM GEN'D FOR ATTACHED PROCESSOR SUPPORT` — **passes**, and the block below it executes `CONCS 0(R1)` in a loop over 64 channel-set addresses. That is an operation exception on ESA/390: a second `STIDC`-class M3a blocker, and one that assembles perfectly. Two consequences. The nucleus load list is an open choice — no EXEC in the tree invokes `VMFLOAD`, and CE ships four lists (`CPLOAD` 173, `APLOAD` 181, `VRLOAD` 175, `APVRLOAD` 182); every count in `docs/` assumes `CPLOAD`, which is consistent with one Hercules CPU but not with `AP=YES`. Settled by `XA0014DK`: `DMKSYS` forced to `AP=NO`, which is one character — `DMKSYSAP DC CL1'&AP1'` — in one module, so `DMKCPI`'s gate fails and the `CONCS` loop never runs. `&AP` in `OPTIONS.COPY` is deliberately untouched, because seven macros declare `GBLB &AP` and changing it would re-expand every module. Eight modules already branch on `DMKSYSAP`, and Hercules is `NUMCPU 1`, so this is a supported configuration rather than a workaround; reverting is dropping one line from `DMKSYS AUXLCL`. `RMSIZE=16384K` also confirms CE's real storage is 16 MB, the `R-25` gate's starting point. Load list settled as `CPLOAD`, consistent with every count in `docs/`. | fixed |
| **I-51** | A `DS` length modifier cannot be a forward reference | `CPISCHIB DS XL(SCHIBSIZ)` gave `IFO231 SYMBOL NOT PREVIOUSLY DEFINED` because `XABLOKS` is copied near the end of DMKCPI, after the work area. Moving the COPY earlier is not an option — it defines DSECTs, so everything after it would land in the last one instead of the CSECT. Written as `DS 13F` instead, which is the same 52 bytes and also documents the fullword alignment `STSCH` and `MSCH` both require. | fixed |
| **I-52** | A hand-quoted `limit` defeats the sequence check | `mkdeck` refuses generated numbers that reach the next surviving record — `R-22`'s whole purpose — but it can only check the limit it is given, and I read one off the screen from a nearby literal. `XA0013DK` numbered 01930100 to 01931100 against a quoted limit of 01951000 and walked over a real record at **01931000**. `UPDATE` said `SEQUENCE ERROR INTRODUCED IN OUTPUT FILE` and DMKCPI's own `ISEQ 73,80` then gave `IFO025 STATEMENT OUT OF SEQUENCE`, so the deck failed in exactly the way the generator exists to prevent. Fixed at the cause: `mkdeck.next_seq(source, anchor)` derives the limit from the file, and the DMKCPI deck now passes derived limits everywhere. The guard was sound; the input to it was a guess. | fixed |
| **I-53** | Lowcore referenced by absolute address, not by symbol | Found by reading DMKCKP, not by any tool: `MVC SAVEDEV(4),184` stores from absolute 184 = X'B8', which the PSA rename turned into the subsystem-identification word. A scan for renamed symbols cannot see it, because it names nothing. Sweeping for the pattern then found **three more in `DMKLD00E`** — `ST 2,72`, which is the **CAW** at X'48', a word ESA/390 does not have at all — in a module that uses bare register numbers throughout and so matches none of the usual shapes. A third DMKCKP site, `MVC SAVECODE(8),136`, grabs eight bytes of the interruption-code area. I called it "very likely wrong" on the assumption ESA/390 lays that area out differently; **it does not, and the site is correct**. Hercules's `PSA_3XX` gives X'88' `svcint` and X'8C' `pgmint`, the same pair S/370 has, so those eight bytes mean what DMKCKP thinks. Left alone, and the caution retracted. `tools/s370only.py` now sweeps for this class. `MVI` and `CLI` are excluded because their second operand is an immediate byte — including them gave nine false positives (pages per cylinder, `RECMAX`, byte counts) against two real sites. | documented |
| **I-54** | I set the wrong CCW format, in three places | `DMKIOS`'s `ORBTMPL` carried `AL1(ORB5F)`, `XAIO` did `MVI XAIOORB+5,X'80'`, and `XABLOKS.COPY` documented it as "CCW FORMAT 1 -- CP ALWAYS SETS THIS". All three are wrong: CP's CCWs are format 0. Found while mapping DMKCKP's self-relative branches, because three of them turned out to be **CCWs** — `CCW X'08',*-8-DMKCKP+X'800',0,0` — which raised the question of what a CCW even looks like now. `DMKIOS` had been reported as converted and clean since run19 with this bug in it. The lesson is the one this project keeps relearning from a different direction: `NO STATEMENTS FLAGGED` is a statement about the assembler, not about the machine. See `R-27`. | fixed |
| **I-55** | I put executable code inside a region CP zeroes at startup | `XAIOWORK` generates work areas **and** the `XAIOFIND` subroutine, and my first placement put it beside `SAVEDEV` — which sits inside `CLR1`, the region `00541000` clears with `XC CLR1(CLR1SIZE),CLR1`. CE rejected it with `IFO224 LENGTH ERROR`, because 250-odd bytes took `CLR1SIZE` past the `XC` length limit of 256. **The length error was the lucky part.** Had the region been smaller the assembly would have succeeded and CP would have zeroed the lookup subroutine at startup — executable code erased at runtime, with no diagnostic anywhere and a wild branch as the only symptom. Moved to after `CLR1SIZE EQU *-CLR1` and before `ALLOCBUF`, which is outside both that clear and the `XC ACBUFF(SFBSIZE*8),ACBUFF` one. The general lesson for the remaining bootstrap modules: **a macro that emits code needs a home checked against every `XC` in the module**, not just against addressability. | fixed |
| **I-56** | Macro expansions overflowed a module's base registers | `DMKDMP` failed with `IFO209 ADDRESSABILITY ERROR` on two of its **own** pre-existing lines — `L R5,MAINPSA` and `L R12,=A(DMKDMPDK)` — not on anything the deck added. `USING DMKDMPDK,R12` plus `USING DMKDMP+4096,R13` covers 8 KB, and 22 sites expanding to about fifteen instructions each pushed the module past it, so the literal pool fell out of reach. `DMKCKP` had room and gave no hint of the problem. **Call-site size is a hard constraint in these modules, not a preference**, which the first design ignored. `XAIO` now expands to four instructions per site — `STM R14,R1` / `LR R1,dev` / `BAL R14,XAIOxxx` / `LM R14,R1` — with the bodies emitted once as subroutines by `XAIOWORK`. That is fourteen bytes a site against sixty, and it is register-transparent: `STM R14,R1` wraps to save R14, R15, R0 and R1, and `LM` restores them without disturbing the condition code the subroutine set. | fixed |
| **I-57** | "The CCW macro can emit either format" — there is no CCW macro | I wrote in `17-CARRY-FORWARD-64.md` that the 240 static CCW sites could be converted by changing one macro, on the assumption that `CCW` was a macro invocation. It is not. `CCW` is Assembler XF's own instruction, XF has no `CCW1` (Assembler H introduced it), and XF **refuses to define a macro of that name at all**: `CCWTEST` on CE gave `IFO043 MACRO PROTOTYPE STATEMENT HAS INVALID OP CODE`. `OPSYN` might work and CP uses it nowhere — untested. The correction that matters is better than the claim was, though: `ORB5_F` lives in the **ORB**, so the format is chosen per `SSCH`, not globally, and format-0 and format-1 channel programs can coexist. The paths that need storage above 16 MB can build format-1 CCWs with `DC` while everything else keeps its `CCW` statements untouched. | closed |
| **I-58** | A comment is not a parameter | Mike asked whether the CCW format was parameterised everywhere after I recommended exactly that. It was not: `build.py` had a literal `DC X'00'` and `XAIO` had a comment saying "left zero on purpose". Now `ORBCCWFM EQU 0` in `XABLOKS.COPY` is the only place the format is chosen, and both `DMKIOS`'s ORB template and `XAIO`'s subroutine take flag5 from it. That created a real dependency worth noting — `XAIO` now needs `XABLOKS`, which the bootstrap modules did not copy — and `XABLOKS` ends in DSECTs, so the `COPY` has to go immediately before `END` or everything after it lands in the last DSECT instead of the CSECT. | fixed |
| **I-59** | `DMKSAV` runs in two architectures, so only half of it converts | It is both a nucleus module (`CPLOAD.EXEC` line 99) and the `LDT DMKSAVNC` target on the last card. `DMKSAVNC` at seq 00380000 is entered by the standalone loader deck **while the nucleus is being built**, on the build machine in S/370 mode; the CSECT entry at 00129000 and `DMKSAVRS` at 00205000 run on the target machine at IPL, in ESA/390 mode. My first deck converted all eleven channel sites, which would have made the build IPL take an operation exception while writing the very nucleus it was building. Revised to the six on the IPL side; `DMKSAVNC`'s five stay `SIO`/`TIO`, and 00430000's `BC 7,*-4` stays with them because the instruction it spans is unchanged. A module holding both forms is not a contradiction — the two paths never execute in one IPL. | fixed |
| **I-60** | `DMKLD00E` needs no conversion, and the reason is CMS | I had it queued as the last and hardest bootstrap module: 19 sites, no PSA symbols, and base registers shifting through `USING RELDR,15,9`, `RELDR,12,9` and `RELDR,4,9`, so a module over 8 KB with no single location addressable from every site. Mike's question about building CMS gave the decisive argument instead. `DMKLD00E LOADER` is line 2 of **both** `094/CPLOAD.EXEC` and `093/CMSLOAD.EXEC` with the same SHA-256, so converting it would break the CMS nucleus build as well as CP's — and CMS must keep being built in S/370 form, because it is an S/370 guest through M4. The loader runs only in the build IPL and never on the converted machine. **19 channel sites leave the scope.** [26-BUILDING-CMS.md](26-BUILDING-CMS.md). | closed |
| **I-62** | `CLRCH` is the one site where the answer is to delete the function | `DMKVSJ` simulates the guest's `CLCH`, and when the virtual channel is **dedicated** (`VCHSTAT` has `VCHDED`, set only by `DMKVCH`'s `ATTACH CHANNEL` at seq 00570000) it passed the guest's clear straight to hardware as a hand-assembled `DC X'9F01'` / `DC S(0(1))`. `RCHP` (X'B23B') looks like the substitution — it is `GENx___x390x900`, existing exactly where `CLRCH` does not — but it resets a *channel path*, a shared resource the channel subsystem owns, not a channel a guest can be given; VM/XA dropped dedicated channels for this reason. Two further checks made it conclusive. `RCHP` takes its CHPID in R1 bits 24-31 and takes an **operand exception** if bits 0-23 are non-zero, and `VCHADD` is a channel address in device-number form (X'0300' for channel 3), so the obvious `LH R1,VCHADD` / `RCHP` program-checks every time. And a shift would make it *worse*, not better: Hercules assigns `chpid = devnum >> 8` (`config.c:724`) so it would appear to work, while `chp_reset` calls `device_reset` on every device on the path — and `device_reset` is the one thing that clears `PMCW5_E` and the interruption parameter, which CP sets exactly once at IPL in `XA0013DK` and never again. **One guest `CLCH` would permanently disable every device on that channel**, with SSCH returning cc3 for the rest of the IPL and no diagnostic anywhere. `XA0018DK` replaces the whole block with `B CLCHEXIT`, so `CLCH` is always simulated as a `TCH` — which is what the module already did one instruction earlier for every channel that was not dedicated. `NO STATEMENTS FLAGGED`, run46. | fixed |
| **I-63** | Two Hercules instances were started on one pack | A `cd` into the CE directory followed by a backgrounded `hercules` lost its working directory to the tool wrapper before the following `tail` ran, so the log appeared not to exist and I started a second instance. Both IPLed, both auto-logged OPERATOR and MAINT, and both wrote for about thirty seconds before I killed them. The damage was far smaller than it should have been, for a reason worth recording: **CE ships every VM/370 pack `ro` with a Hercules shadow file**, so the distribution packs are physically unwritable and still carry their 2021-2022 mtimes after forty-six runs. Only `gccbrx.cckd` at 06A0 is mounted writable, and it was restored from the pristine zip. `cckdcdsk -f -3` found free-space-chain errors in three shadows and no track-level errors, and rebuilt them; `Q DISK` and `LISTFILE` then confirmed `MNT191` R/W with the MACLIB and all thirteen `TXTLCL` files intact at their original timestamps. Nothing was lost. Two habits from it: **never background Hercules without first asserting `pgrep -c hercules` is 0**, and `setsid ... < /dev/null` so the instance does not depend on the shell that launched it. | fixed |
| **I-64** | A blank line does not answer CP's start-type prompt | Consequence of `I-63`. A killed CP writes no warm-start record, so the next IPL says `DMKWRM920I NO WARM START DATA; CKPT START FOR RETRY` and prompts `Start ((Warm|Force|COLD|CKPT) ...)`. The rc's null line after `ipl 6A1` does **not** satisfy that prompt — CP re-prompts, and every later command in the run is swallowed by it with no error reported anywhere. A whole IPL ran to completion with `readcard`, `q disk`, `cpacc` and `vmfasm` all consumed by the prompt, and the only symptom was the prompt string repeating in the log. `mkrun.py`'s `BOOT` now sends `cold` after the null line: harmless when CP did start (it reaches OPERATOR as an unknown command) and answers the prompt when it did not, so the rc is self-healing after any interrupted run. | fixed |
| **I-65** | `mkrun.py` had no way to run an ordinary CMS command | Checking the pack after `I-63` needed `Q DISK` and `LISTFILE`, which no spec could express, and the only route was to hand-edit the generated rc — which is exactly how run33 was lost. Added a `cmd:<text>[:<secs>]` spec, emitted before the `VMFMAC`/`VMFASM` block. It is the general escape hatch that makes "never hand-edit the rc" an enforceable rule rather than an intention. | fixed |
| **I-66** | The nucleus load list I was measuring is not the one CE builds | There are two `CPLOAD.EXEC` files — `194/CPLOAD.EXEC` with 172 modules and `094/CPLOAD.EXEC` with 183 — and CMS's search order reaches F (094) before G (194), so 094's is the one `VMFLOAD` reads. Every count `status.py` and `s370only.py` reported was taken against 194's, leaving **eleven nucleus modules outside every figure**: `HDKCQA`, `HDKCQU`, `HDKD58`, `HDKD7C`, `HDKD8C`, `DMKGRU`, `DMKGRV`, `DMKGRX`, `DMKPEC`, `DMKPED`, `DMKPEQ`. All eleven are clean on both axes, so 133/67/200 are unchanged and no scope is added — the numbers were right by luck, not by method. The same investigation settled two pending items: assembly coverage is **three** EXECs (`194/ASMDMK` plus CE's own `094/HRCASM` and `094/LDFASM`), which is what assembles the `HDK*` modules, and together they cover every load-list module but `DMKBTS`. A second error in the same place: the load-list regex was unanchored, so `DMKSST` — commented out with a `*` in column 1 — counted as a module and made it 184. Both tools now anchor with `(?m)^`. | fixed |
| **I-67** | Three decks were committed with sites left inside the loops they converted | `DMKVSJ` was committed as complete with an `HDV` at seq 00324000 untouched; `DMKCKP` left a `TIO` at 00733000 and an `SIO` at 00742000 inside the very drain loop whose `HIO` it converted at 00726000; `DMKDMP` left `SIOIPL` at 00769000 whose own cc0 target `TIOIPL` it converted at 00775000, and `PRSIO` at 01161000 between two converted sites. Each would have taken an operation exception on the instruction after one that had just been converted. **Root cause: an ad-hoc `awk` one-liner used to clear a module instead of the tool built for it.** `substr($0,10,8)` on `         HDV   0(R5)` yields `HDV   0(`, and the pattern required an exact match, so every site whose operand followed the mnemonic closely enough went unseen — it reported zero for a module with one. The same one-liner is what cleared the eleven modules of `I-66`; rechecked with `s370only.py`, they are genuinely clean. Nothing caught this for three commits because every existing check answers a different question: `s370only.py` lists sites but not whether a deck covers them, `status.py` tracks the PSA axis, and CE only says the module assembles. Two tools now close the gap — `deckscan.py` reads the punched object deck, which is ground truth, and `coverage.py` diffs `s370only.py`'s site list against each deck's own `./ R` and `./ D` ranges, and cannot be fooled by a bad scan because it does not scan. | fixed |
| **I-68** | A byte pattern in an object deck is evidence, not proof | `deckscan.py --find B230` reports `CSCH` in `DMKVSP`, which has no `CSCH` and no XA mnemonic anywhere in its source: it is two adjacent bytes of some constant. So a positive in an unconverted module is noise until the source agrees. The two results that are hard to fake are the ones worth leaning on — a pattern **absent** from the whole deck (`9F01` after `XA0018DK`), and an ESA/390 opcode **present** in a module known to be converted (`B231`/`B233`/`B235` in `DMKIOS`), since no base `TEXT` deck could contain one. Recorded in the tool rather than only here, because the tool is what a later reader will use. | documented |
| **I-69** | `DMKCPI`'s twenty-first channel site is dead, not missed | The `SIO` at 00485480 sits inside the channel-set-switching probe, which `CLI N0(R2),YES` at 00483000 skips whenever `DMKSYSAP` is `N` -- and `XA0014DK` forces exactly that with `SYSCOR RMSIZE=16384K,AP=NO`. The `&AP` global is still 1, so the block is *assembled*; it is simply never *entered*. Converting it would mean half-converting an AP path whose unconvertible `CONCS` (`DC X'B2001000'`) sits four instructions earlier, which is worse than leaving the block whole and dead. Declared in `tools/coverage.py` with its reason rather than silently skipped, so the report still shows it and says why. This is the third disposition the conversion uses, alongside convert and delete: **leave it, because the configuration that reaches it is off.** | documented |
| **I-70** | Where a work area is placed decides which base register it assumes | `DMKCPI` has two addressability domains in one CSECT: `USING DMKCPINT,R12,R13` from 00421000, then `DROP R12,R13` at 01767000 and `USING DMKCPIEM,R12,R13`, with `BASEREGS DC A(DMKCPIEM,DMKCPIEM+4096)` loaded at entry. The obvious home for `XAIOWORK` was beside the work areas `XA0013DK` already inserts at 01930000 -- and that would have been wrong, in a way that assembles perfectly. A *reference* resolves against whichever `USING` is active where it is written, so `LA R4,CPISCHIB` at 00480000 is fine wherever `CPISCHIB` is defined; but `XAIOWORK` is not data alone, it emits **code**, and `BAL R14,XAIOFIND` and `MVC XAIOORB+8(4),CAW` inside it would have had their displacements computed off `DMKCPIEM` while every caller runs with R12/R13 holding the `DMKCPINT` base. No diagnostic, a correct-looking listing, garbage addresses at IPL. It goes at 01761000 instead, just after `TSTGRF`'s `BR R14` and before the `LTORG`, where the callers' own `USING` is in force and nothing falls into it. The general rule: **a macro that emits code must expand where its callers' base registers are the ones in effect**, which is a stronger constraint than `I-55`'s "not in a region something zeroes". | fixed |
| **I-71** | The dispatcher overwrites CR6 on every dispatch, undoing the I/O interruption mask | `XA0013DK` moved the I/O-interruption subclass mask into CR6 (`DC X'FF000000'` in `CTLREGS`) because CR6 was zero and no I/O interruption would ever be presented. That fix is correct and **insufficient**: CR6 is also S/370's virtual-machine-assist control register, and CP reloads it constantly. `DMKDSP` at seq 02542000 does `LCTL C6,C6,0(R6)` **unconditionally on the dispatch path**, with R6 defaulting to `CPCREG6` (`LA R6,CPCREG6` at 02452000) and becoming `VMMICRO` only when the assist is on for both system and user. With the assist off -- the CE default -- `CPCREG6` is zero, and the module's own comments say so: "NO - LEAVE CREG6 ZERO". So every dispatch sets CR6 to zero and silently disables all I/O interruptions. `DMKCPI` itself does it first, at 00698000: `OI CPCREG6,X'02'` then `LCTL C6,C6,CPCREG6`, overwriting `CTLREGS` a few instructions after loading it. `DMKCFO` at 00612200 resets it again, and `DMKAPI` twice more (AP-only, out of scope). **This is on the M1 critical path**: `DMKIOS` exits via `GOTO DMKDSPCH` to wait for the console interrupt, so the dispatcher runs before the interrupt that M1 is waiting for. The fix is small and turns the problem into its own solution -- make `CPCREG6` carry `X'FF000000'`, and the dispatcher's unconditional `LCTL` then re-asserts the correct mask on every dispatch instead of destroying it. The `VMMICRO` path needs the same treatment or the assist disabled, since ECPS:VM does not exist in ESA/390. Found by sweeping every `LCTL`/`STCTL` on CR2 and CR6 after noticing `DMKFRE`'s `STCTL C2 / NI ,X'7F' / LCTL C2` -- "disable channel zero" -- which is the same class on CR2 and is also broken, three times over -- CR2 is not fixed yet and is tracked separately. | fixed |
| **I-72** | `DMKMCH` restores control registers from a hardware-written area whose layout changed | `LCTL C0,C13,MCFXDLOG+216` restores CR0-CR13 from the machine-check fixed logout area -- which the **hardware** writes, and which ESA/390 lays out differently from S/370. The displacement is therefore probably wrong, and CR6 is inside the range, so a machine check would restore a wrong I/O-interruption subclass mask on top of everything else it got wrong. Left alone deliberately: it is reachable only on a machine check, which Hercules does not generate, and getting it right needs the ESA/390 machine-check save-area layout rather than a guess. Declared in `tools/creg.py` so the report keeps showing it. | documented |
| **I-73** | CR2 carries a channel mask CP still writes at runtime | The companion to `I-71`, on the register `XA0013DK` cleared rather than the one it filled. `CTLREGS` stopped putting `X'FFFFFFFF'` in CR2, but **sixteen `LCTL`s across nine nucleus modules put it back**, because on S/370 CR2 is the channel mask and CP masks channels constantly: `DMKFRE` three times to "disable channel zero while we are extending", `DMKIOS` and `DMKIOT` twice each around channel-check recovery, `DMKCKP` with `LCTL C2,C3,X8FF` — a **range**, so it sets CR3 wrong too — `DMKCPI` twice around an `LPSW` that waits for a 3277, and `DMKDMP` once to "re-enable channel 0". On ESA/390 CR2 is the dispatchable-unit-control-table origin. Eleven loads are removed and none is replaced, which is the part worth arguing. `DMKFRE` looks like a critical section, but `TS XTNDLOCK` two instructions earlier is the real lock and is unchanged; the CR2 masking covers channel 0 alone, so it was always partial. And the obvious substitute is worse than nothing: masking the I/O subclass in CR6 instead would mask **all** I/O, because every subchannel here is ISC 0 — and the extend calls `DMKPTRFR`, which does paging I/O and waits for it, so a faithful-looking conversion would deadlock the path it was protecting. Every `STCTL` stays: a store can never put a wrong value into a live register, so it is harmless by construction. Three sites are declared rather than converted: `DMKSAV` 00418600 is inside `DMKSAVNC` (`I-59`), `DMKLD00E` is out of scope (`I-60`), and `DMKMCH` is `I-72`. | fixed |
| **I-74** | Rebuilding a macro library without restaging its member list builds the old set | `XAIOB.MACRO` was added, read onto the A-disk, and `VMFMAC DMKLCL DMKLCL` run — and every `XABTIO`, `XABSIO` and `XAIOBWRK` came back `IFO078 UNDEFINED OP CODE`, with fourteen statements flagged. `VMFMAC` builds the library from `<lib> EXEC`, which **is** the member list, and the copy on the A-disk still named six members rather than seven; the run's own output says so plainly if you read it — `XAIO MACRO ADDED`, `XAOPS MACRO ADDED`, and no XAIOB. The symptom points at the macro and the cause is a stale list. `mkrun.py`'s `mac:` spec now stages `<lib>.EXEC` itself, so the combination cannot be assembled by hand again. Worth noting alongside: the failed assembly still reported `DMKIOT TXTLCL CREATED` after `*** ERROR ASMBLING DMKIOT ***`, so a **wrong** TXTLCL was left on the disk where `VMFLOAD` would find it — always re-assemble clean before building a deck. | fixed |
| **I-75** | The one site where reentrancy costs nothing to satisfy | `DMKPSA` is lowcore itself — `USING DMKPSA,R0`, `PSA EQU DMKPSA` — declares REENTRANT, and has no `USING IOBLOK`, so neither `XAIO`'s static work area nor `XAIOB`'s IOBLOK-resident one fits. Reading what its two sites actually need dissolved the problem rather than solving it. The `HIO` needs **no storage at all**: `HSCH` takes its subchannel in R1 and writes nothing. And the `TIO` at 00651000 is followed immediately by `BCT R15,HALTCON` — no CSW test, no condition-code test — so its only purpose is to consume pending status and the IRB that `TSCH` stores into is **write-only**. That is what makes a shared scratch area *correct* here rather than merely convenient: two paths racing on it would both be discarding what they wrote. The general lesson is worth keeping: the reentrancy question is not "is this module reentrant" but "does anything read what this writes", and the second question is often cheaper to answer and sometimes answers itself. | fixed |
| **I-76** | Enlarging a shared control block invalidates every module that was not reassembled | `IOBLOKS.XA0003DK` added the ORB, IRB and register save area to the IOBLOK so the shims could be reentrant — and anchored the insert at 00044000, **in the middle of the block**, immediately after `IOBCSW`. Two consequences, neither visible to the assembler. Every field below it — `IOBIOER`, `IOBSPEC`, `IOBFLAG`, `IOBTRQB` and the rest — shifts by 112 bytes, so **every module that reads an IOBLOK** has wrong offsets unless reassembled. And `IOBSIZE EQU (*-IOBLOK)/8` at 00052000 grows by 14 doublewords, so **every module that allocates one** asks `DMKFREE` for too little; `DMKIOS` then does `TSCH IOBIRB` and writes sixty-four bytes past the end into the free-storage chain. **Fifty-seven nucleus modules reference `IOBSIZE` and I had reassembled six.** `RBLOKS` has the same shape for `RDEVSIZE`, with five users and none reassembled. Two fixes. The insert moves to the end of the block, before the `IOBSIZE` EQU, which narrows the blast radius from "reads an IOBLOK" to "allocates one" — it does not remove the need for a rebuild, it changes what a missed module does. And the rebuild becomes routine rather than exceptional: `ASMDMK` assembles all 186 members in **ninety seconds** (`21-NUCLEUS-GAP.md`), so there was never a reason to be assembling modules one at a time and hoping the rest still matched. Any change to a member of `DMKLCL MACLIB` now means a full `ASMDMK DMKLCL` before a deck is built. | fixed |
| **I-77** | A macro that emits code inside a DSECT generates nothing, and says nothing | The first IPL of the converted nucleus took an operation exception at X'000208'. `DMKSAV`'s deck anchored `XAIOWORK` at `./ I 00685000` — and sequence 00685000 **is the `PSA` macro call**, so the insert landed *after* it, inside `PSA DSECT`. In a DSECT every `DS` and `DC` generates no storage and the subroutine bodies generate no object code at all, so `XAIOSIO`, `XAIOTIO` and `XAIOHIO` became **offsets into the PSA** rather than addresses in the CSECT. With `USING PSA,R0` in force, `BAL R14,XAIOSIO` branched to absolute low storage — landing in `TEMPSAVE`, on a stored TOD clock value, which is why the failing bytes decoded as neither an instruction nor anything this conversion emits. **The module assembled clean**: every symbol was defined, just in the wrong section. `NO STATEMENTS FLAGGED`, a `TXTLCL` produced, and 640 KB of nucleus with a branch into page zero. Three things caught it, none of them the assembler: the deck audit showed `DMKSAV` with zero ESA/390 opcodes while its S/370 count had dropped from 11 to 5; the load map showed it growing by 64 bytes where `XAIOWORK` alone is about 400; and the control deck — CE's unmodified nucleus through the identical build — reached wait state X'012', `NUCLEUS LOAD ON 'LABEL'`, which is success. This is `I-70` one step further: it is not enough to ask which base register is in force where a macro expands, one must also ask whether that place is a CSECT at all. Every other work-area anchor was checked against its record and all five are ordinary CSECT statements. | fixed |
| **I-78** | A spool file is queued at CP shutdown, not when the writer ends | Getting the load deck out of CP takes **two runs**, and nothing says so. `VMFLOAD` prints `SYSTEM LOAD DECK COMPLETE` and the punch spool file is *not yet queued* — CP queues it at shutdown — so `CP START 00D` in the same run finds an empty queue, reports `PUN 00D STARTED` and punches nothing. The deck then comes out during the **next** run, at a moment when no `herc:devinit` has run and CE's own config owns the device: `000D 3525 io/punch.txt ascii`. 18,894 records went into that ascii punch and were lost. Two plausible fixes both failed and the failures are the useful part. Pointing the config line at a binary file did nothing — the run reported `PUN 00D OUTPUT OF MAINT FILE = 0405 RECDS= 018894` and `START FOR OUTPUT` and produced no file at all. A `devinit` placed *ahead of* `BOOT` also produced no file, because the IPL's system reset undoes it. **Only a devinit issued after the IPL takes.** `mkrun.py` gained `--punch <file>`, emitted after `BOOT` and before the specs, with no `ascii` operand — an object deck is EBCDIC and the translation would mangle every TXT record while leaving the ESD card names readable, so a corrupted deck would still scan as if it were fine. | fixed |
| **I-79** | PSW format is a third conversion axis, and it was never enumerated | The channel-instruction axis (133 sites) and the storage-key axis (67) were both counted. **BC-mode PSW is a third**, and it is the one that gates everything: ESA/390 has no BC mode. Hercules refuses the IPL outright — `HHCCP030E ESA/390 mode IPL failed: Invalid IPL PSW: 00040000 00004470` — because bit 12 is zero. Forcing bit 12 on gets past the check and straight into `HHCCP016I Program interrupt loop`, since in EC mode the interruption code moves from the old PSW to X'8C' and the fixed-storage layout CP was built against no longer holds. This is not a site count to be swept for; it is every PSW CP builds, loads, stores and inspects, plus the low-storage map underneath them. It surfaced only because the I/O axis got far enough to attempt a real IPL — which is an argument for attempting the IPL earlier, not for having counted better. | open |
| **I-80** | The standalone loader is itself S/370 I/O, and it is not in the audit | `deckscan --audit` reads object modules and reports 43 S/370 sites left in the deck. `DMKLD00E` appears in none of them, because the loader's cards are not object modules — they are the bootstrap the audit prints as `other` and does not decode. It reads the card reader with `TIO`/`SIO`: at X'44C2', `9D00 8000` = `TIO 0(R8)`, and `9C00 8000` = `SIO` four bytes later. So an ESA/390 IPL of the converted deck cannot get as far as the nucleus, and `DMKSAVNC`'s own five sites — declared dead on the assumption the build IPL stays S/370 — come back to life the moment it does not. The measurement lesson is the sharper one: **an audit that only decodes what it understands reports the rest as absent.** | open |
| **I-81** | A macro change contaminates every module, so `VMFLOAD ... DMKHRC` is not a control | The control experiment — CE's unmodified nucleus through the identical build — was supposed to prove the build chain sound, and its nucleus did not boot. The reason is that **it was never a control.** `VMFLOAD CPLOAD DMKHRC` skips every `TXTLCL`, which isolates *module* changes; it does nothing about *macro* changes. `PSA`, `IOBLOKS` and `RBLOKS` are members of `DMKLCL MACLIB`, rebuilt by `VMFMAC`, and **every module that copies them compiles the change in regardless of its output filetype**. So `CPCREG6 DC X'FF000000'` — correct as an ESA/390 I/O-interruption subclass mask, garbage as an S/370 VM-assist control register — was in all 183 modules of the "unmodified" deck. The real baseline was run on a **pristine extraction** instead, where it passes cleanly: all 186 modules assemble (`T=27.36/51.02`, only I-34's pre-existing `DMKRIO` MNOTEs), `VMFLOAD` yields an 18,799-card deck, the standalone IPL reaches wait `X'012'` = `NUCLEUS LOAD ON 'LABEL'`, and IPL of 6A1 comes up stamped `09/28/26 18:44:12` against CE's shipped `07/19/22 13:20:46` — which is how one tells the rebuilt nucleus from the shipped one. The general rule: **a control has to be a separate tree, not a filetype filter on the same one.** Anything reached through a shared MACLIB is not isolated by the `CNTRL` hierarchy. | fixed |
| **I-82** | CP already runs in EC mode; M2 is two modules, not twenty-nine | I sized the BC-to-EC conversion by grep — 143 `LPSW`, 196 old/new PSW references, 44 `SSM`, 26 BC interruption-code fields, 409 sites across 29 modules — and called it "three times the I/O axis". **The measurement was of the touched surface, and the touched surface is almost entirely already correct.** `EQU.COPY` defines `EXTMODE EQU X'08'  BIT 12 - EXTENDED MODE`, and every one of CP's own new PSWs is built with it: `CPIPSWS DC A(MCHEKENB,DMKPSAEX)` for external, SVC, program, machine-check and I/O, plus `RESTPSW`, where `MCHEKENB EQU (MCHEK+EXTMODE)*X'10000'`. `PSA.MACRO` already declares the whole EC-mode fixed-storage map at the architected offsets — `INTEX` X'86', `INTSVCL`/`INTSVC` X'88'/X'8A', `INTPRL`/`INTPR` X'8C'/X'8E', `TREXADD` X'90', `PERCODE`/`PERADD` X'96'/X'98', `INTKFLIN`/`INTTIO` X'B8'/X'BA'. And the handlers read it: `DMKPRG` does `LH R6,INTPR` and `LH R2,INTPRL`, `DMKSVC` does `LH R14,INTSVC` — never extracting the code from the old PSW, which in BC mode would be the only place it existed. **IBM ran CP in EC mode from the start.** 18 modules reference `EXTMODE`; the four standalone programs reference it **zero** times, which is the clean line between the two worlds. So M2's real content is the standalone side: `DMKLD00E` (22 I/O, 26 `LPSW`, 10 `SSM`) and `DMKSAV` (15 I/O, 5 `LPSW`) — about 78 sites in two modules to reach an IPL, with `DMKFMT` and `DMKDDR` following later as utilities. The residual `PROPSW+2`/`IOOPSW+2` hits are byte 2, condition code and program mask, which is valid in both modes, or they are guest-PSW simulation that must stay dual-mode because guests may be BC. **The lesson is the one I keep relearning: a grep counts what a pattern touches, not what has to change, and the difference here was a factor of five.** | fixed |
| **I-83** | The card loader is not on the IPL path at all, and CP already boots in ESA/390 | I scoped `DMKLD00E` as M2's first job — 20 I/O sites, 26 `LPSW`, 10 `SSM` — and found while checking addressability that its sites sit under **five different `USING` regions** (R15, R3/R4, R11, R13, R12), so no single work area can be reached from all of them: `I-70` five times over. That check was worth more than the deck would have been, because it prompted the obvious question: **a disk IPL never runs the card loader.** `DMKLD00E` exists to build a nucleus from a card deck; `IPL 6A1` reads the IPL record `DMKSAV` wrote and goes straight into CP. Tested on the pristine baseline pack with `ARCHMODE ESA/390`: the IPL record reads `000C0000 00000000` — **bit 12 already on, EC mode** — Hercules accepts it without complaint, CP executes, and the machine stops in `PSW=000A0000 00000017`, a proper CP disabled wait, with the program interruption code at X'8C' reading `00040001`, an operation exception. X'017' is `DMKCKP`'s `WAIT17 DC X'000A0000'  ERROR LOADING CKP PAGE 2`, installed as the program new PSW before the checkpoint-page read — and `X'0A'` is EC plus Wait, so CP's own wait PSWs are EC-format too. **Unmodified CP boots in ESA/390 today and runs into checkpoint processing before its first S/370 I/O instruction.** Consequences: `DMKLD00E` needs no conversion and can stay S/370 indefinitely; `DMKSAV`'s conversion (`XA0017DK`) must be **reverted**, because the build IPL is a card IPL and has to stay S/370; and the entire remaining path to a 31-bit CP is the I/O axis on CP proper, which was already the work in hand. The lesson: **I sized a module before asking whether it was on the path.** | fixed |
| **I-84** | `cmd:` runs before the assemblies, so it cannot consume their output | `mkrun.py` emits every `cmd:` after `CPACC` and before the `VMFMAC`/`VMFASM` block, which is right for setting an assembly up and wrong for using what it produced. Reverting `DMKSAV` needed an erase, an assembly and a `VMFLOAD` **in that order**, and no ordering of `cmd:` specs can express it — a `VMFLOAD` issued as a `cmd:` punches the *previous* run's TEXT. Added `post:<text>[:<secs>]`, emitted after the assemblies. | fixed |
| **I-85** | `CP PURGE PUNCH ALL` does not purge a closed spool file, and the stale one is punched first (twice) | The rebuilt deck came out **1,511,680 bytes — byte-for-byte the same size as the previous one** — and `deckscan --audit` still showed `DMKSAV` carrying `HSCH SSCH STSCH TSCH` after the conversion had been reverted and `DMKSAV TXTHRC CREATED` confirmed. The deck was not the one just built. `CP PURGE PUNCH ALL` reports `NO FILES PURGED` against a file that VMFLOAD has already closed and queued to SYSTEM — it addresses the user's virtual punch, not the system output queue — so spool file 0412 from three hours earlier survived every purge, and `CP START 00D` punched the **oldest** file in the queue. This is `I-26` on the other unit: the reader takes the first entry of `Q RDR ALL`, and the punch queue behaves the same way. The purge is not the fix; **draining is** — punch the queue out to a scratch file and check `CP QUERY PUN ALL` says `NO PUN FILES` before the VMFLOAD that matters. The deeper point is that every check I had was consistent with success: the assembly was clean, the filetype was right, `SYSTEM LOAD DECK COMPLETE` appeared, the card count was plausible. Only comparing the artifact against the change caught it — the audit is worth more than all the progress messages combined. **It then happened a second time**, in the very next cycle, because the drain loop was in one build script and not in its successor: `DMKCKP TXTLCL CREATED` at 21:07:50, `VMFLOAD` at 21:09:04, `SYSTEM LOAD DECK COMPLETE` — correct order, correct inputs — and the punched deck was again byte-identical to the previous one once the spool header card was stripped, which is the check that settles it (`cmp` the *stripped* decks; the unstripped ones differ only by the timestamp in the header and look reassuringly unequal). Two consequences are now permanent in the build scripts: the configured punch is a **binary** file so the boot drain lands somewhere recoverable rather than in an ascii device, and every cycle **drains to `NO PUN FILES` before the VMFLOAD that matters** and then **gates on the artifact** — measuring the changed thing in the deck and refusing to IPL if it is absent. A build pipeline that cannot tell you whether your change is in the output is not a pipeline, it is a coincidence generator. | fixed |
| **I-86** | The IPL reads 4096 bytes, and the routine that reads the rest must be inside them | The converted nucleus IPLed in ESA/390 and stopped at `PSW=000A0000 00000017` with an operation exception whose old PSW pointed at X'1F54' — in a region that dumped as **all zeros**. Not an unconverted instruction: a branch into storage that was never loaded. The caller decoded as `STM R14,R1,X'614'(R13)` / `BAL R14,X'752'(R13)` / `LM R14,R1,X'614'(R13)`, which is XAIO's call macro, and `deckscan --find 45E0D752` put it in **`DMKCKP`**, not the `DMKMCH` my load map claimed — that tool attributes TXT by most-recent-SD rather than by ESDID and was wrong, as its own docstring warns. `DMKCKPT` is one CSECT addressed by two base registers, `USING DMKCKPT,R12,R13` with R12 = X'800' and R13 = X'1800', and **the IPL record reads only the first 4096 bytes**: `06000800 00001000`. The second 4096 arrive afterwards through `LDCCW  CCW 6,0+X'1800',CC+SILI,4096` — a read that XAIO itself drives. So XAIO above offset X'1000' cannot work, because the code that performs the read needs a subroutine that only exists once the read has happened. Measured in the punched deck the bodies sat at X'16F2'–X'179A' against a text length of X'17A0', and `BAL R14,X'752'(R13)` branched to X'1752', 1,618 bytes past anything ever loaded; zeros decode as operation exception. Moved to sequence 00517500, beside `WAIT16`, measured at offset X'05B0' in the deck, inside the constants block ending at the `LTORG` — nothing falls through it and it is far below X'1000'. **This is the third distinct wrong answer to "where does `XAIOWORK` go", after `I-55` (inside a region that is zeroed at runtime) and `I-77` (inside a DSECT, generating no code at all), and the first that no assembler or linker could have caught** — the module assembled clean, linked clean, and the address was correct; it was simply not in storage yet. The check that catches it is arithmetic on the artifact: find the opcode bytes in the deck and compare the CSECT offset against the IPL read count. | fixed |
| **I-87** | The converted nucleus runs in ESA/390: no program check, and cc=3 instead | With `XAIOWORK` below the IPL read boundary (I-86) the ESA/390 IPL of the converted nucleus stops being a crash and becomes a hang, which is progress of the best kind. `PSW=000C3000 00000A52`, and the decode matters: `cmwp=C` is EC plus machine-check with the **wait bit clear**, so the CPU was still executing when it was stopped, and X'8C' reads all zeros, so **no program interruption occurred at all**. The operation exception that had stopped every previous attempt is gone; `DMKCKP`'s converted I/O executes. What remains is condition code **3**, which from an I/O instruction means *not operational*, in a retry loop — `LA R14,100` at X'A40' sets the count and `GR14=00000064` confirms it is still 100. So the subchannel that `XAIO`'s `STSCH` scan resolves is not operational at the moment `DMKCKPT` runs, which is before `DMKCPI` has done any subchannel setup. That is an enablement question — a subchannel must be valid and enabled in its PMCW before `SSCH` will take it — and not an opcode question. Recorded as the next problem rather than solved, because it is the first failure in this conversion that is about ESA/390 *semantics* rather than about instructions, placement or build plumbing. **Root cause found 30 September, and this entry called it.** The hypothesis recorded here -- *"a subchannel must be valid and enabled in its PMCW before `SSCH` will take it"* -- is exactly right, and `SA22-7201-08` states it outright: *"**Enabled (E)**: Bit 8 of word 1... When the E bit is zero, status presented by the device is not made available to the program, and **I/O instructions other than MODIFY SUBCHANNEL and STORE SUBCHANNEL that are executed for the designated subchannel cause condition code 3 to be set**... initially, all subchannels are not enabled; **IPL causes the IPL I/O device to become enabled**."* That accounts for every observation without remainder: `STSCH` succeeds (129,814 times in one IPL, resolving device numbers correctly) because it is explicitly exempt; `SSCH`, `TSCH` and `HSCH` return cc3 for every device except **6A1**, which works only because the IPL enabled it -- which is why `DMKSAV` reads the whole nucleus and nothing else ever gets going. In `DMKCPI`'s `MOUNTALL` loop the result is `BC 1,MOUNTDVI` on every device, so no device is brought online, no volume label is read, SYSRES is never found, and CP ends at `ABEND CPI001`. `XAIOFIND` tests the **valid** bit at `XAIOSCHB+5,X'01'` and never the **enabled** bit, which is bit 8 of word 1 -- `X'80'` of the very same byte -- and nothing in the conversion issues `MSCH`. **The methodological lesson is the expensive one**: this was diagnosed here weeks ago and rediscovered from scratch on 30 September, by trace, over an afternoon. The register exists to be read before the search starts, not after. **Fixed 30 September.** Four lines in `XAIOFIND`, where the scan already had the SCHIB in hand: `TM XAIOSCHB+5,X'80'` / `BO` past it if already enabled / `OI XAIOSCHB+5,X'80'` / `MSCH XAIOSCHB`. The E bit is bit 8 of PMCW word 1 -- `X'80'` of the very byte whose `X'01'` the existing valid test already read -- and the sequence is the one `tests/hardware/05-channel-subsystem.rc` proved by execution months ago (`STSCH`, `OI SCHIB+5,X'80'`, `MSCH`, `SSCH`). `MSCH`'s own condition code is deliberately untested: if it refuses, the caller's `SSCH` still answers cc3 and the caller already handles that, so checking would add a second place to get the same answer wrong. `XAIOB` needed no change -- it takes its subchannel from `RDEVSSID`, which `DMKCPI` populates during the very scan this fixes. **Measured effect:** `MSCH` 0 -> **85** executions, label-read instructions 0 -> **480**, `MVC RDEVSER` 0 -> **16**. CP now reads volume labels from sixteen real devices under ESA/390, passes the `VOL1` check on each and records the serial. **The IPL's PSW was byte-identical before and after** -- same wait code, same registers -- so judging by the symptom alone would have said 'no change'. Checking the artifact is what showed the fix had landed completely and moved the failure to `I-108`. | closed |
| **I-88** | The IPL bootstrap cannot read `SYSIPLDV`, because nothing has set it yet | `I-87`'s cc=3 was not a disabled subchannel and not ESA/390 semantics. Reading XAIO's work area back out of storage settled it in one dump: `XAIOSSID = 00000000`, **`XAIODEV = 0000`** — `DMKCKP` asked `XAIOFIND` to locate **device zero**, the 256-subchannel scan found nothing, and `XAIONONE` returned cc=3 exactly as designed. The cause is my own `I-47` change. `DMKCKP` opened with `LH R0,INTTIO  GET SYS IPL ADDRESS`, and I replaced it with a read of `SYSIPLDV` on the grounds that `DMKVMI` and `DMKCPI` put the IPL device there — true for the nucleus-resident path and **false for `DMKCKPT`, which runs at IPL before `DMKCPI` exists**, so it reads zero. Meanwhile the architected field held the answer all along, and both architectures were dumped to prove it: `r BA.8` gives `06A1` in S/370 (device address at `INTTIO`) and `r B8.10` gives `00010056` in ESA/390 (subsystem-ID word). The **S/370 control is what made this cheap**: the same converted nucleus fails there too, `PSW=000C3000 00000F12`, cc=3, no program check — so the fault could not be architecture semantics, and the search narrowed to my code before any ESA/390 theorising. The fix is to split the two roles by path rather than by module: the bootstrap takes the subsystem ID from X'B8' directly and skips the lookup entirely, since it already has what `XAIOFIND` exists to compute; the resident path keeps `SYSIPLDV`. | fix known |
| **I-89** | Flipping `ARCHMODE` leaves the rest of the config contradicting it | Mike asked whether the Hercules configuration was correct, and two settings were not. `CPUMODEL 4381` is a S/370 4381, a processor that does not exist in ESA/390, so `STIDP` reports a S/370 machine to a CP that is meant to believe it is on ESA/390. `ECPSVM YES` declares the ECPS:VM assists, whose `E6xx` opcodes are `GENx370x` — S/370 only — and CP detects those assists by executing one and recovering from the program check, so declaring them under ESA/390 is a no-op at best and a confounder at worst. Neither caused the failure under investigation, which is the point worth keeping: they were found by review rather than by symptom, and fixing them mid-diagnosis would have muddied `I-88`. `mkrun.archmode()` now moves all three together, and **S/370 runs keep CE's shipped values so the control stays a control**. Still unfixed and noted rather than changed: `MAINSIZE 16` caps the machine at 16 MB, which the 31-bit goal will eventually outgrow. | fixed |
| **I-90** | Restoring a shadow set rewinds CP's spool queue, not just the disk | Three build cycles in a row punched a deck that did not contain the change just made, and I twice blamed the punch. The punch was innocent. CP's **spool files live on the VM50-x packs**, and those packs are exactly what the Hercules shadow files cover — so `cp -r shadows.cms shadows`, done to get a bootable CMS back after a build IPL overwrites the nucleus, also restores **the punch queue as it stood when the snapshot was taken**. An old `VMFLOAD` output then sits at the head of the queue and `CP START 00D` punches it first, because `CP PURGE PUNCH ALL` will not touch a closed file queued to SYSTEM (`I-85`). The tell was the byte count: 1,511,040 for the old placement against 1,511,120 for the new one, and stripped decks comparing equal (`cmp` the *stripped* files — unstripped they differ by the timestamp in the spool header, which is just enough to look reassuring). Two rules now: **after any shadow restore, drain the punch queue to `NO PUN FILES` before the VMFLOAD that matters**, and never trust a build whose artifact has not been measured for the change. The artifact gate caught this one before it reached an IPL, which is the first time one of these cost nothing. The deeper lesson is about snapshots: a restore rewinds *everything* on the restored volumes, including state that belongs to a different subsystem than the one being restored, and the thing most likely to be silently rewound is whatever you were not thinking about. | fixed |
| **I-91** | Wait X'007' is CP's own error exit, not an unexplained stop | With `I-88` fixed, `GR02 = GR10 = 000006A1`: `DMKCKP` addresses SYSRES instead of device 0000 and the cc=3 spin is gone. What remains is a real program check with the program old PSW at `000C2000 00000002` — execution reached absolute location 0, where the bytes are the IPL PSW (`000C0000`, opcode X'00'), an operation exception by construction. `MAINSIZE` is not involved: probed at 256 MB and at 16 MB the old PSW, interruption code and registers are byte-identical, which also clears `MAINSIZE 256` as safe (`I-89`). **The correction worth recording is what I first made of the surrounding evidence.** I read X'B8' = 0 as lowcore being mysteriously overwritten and flagged it as something to establish before chasing the branch. It is nothing of the kind. X'007' is `DMKCKP`'s `ERRWAIT`, reached from its own program-check handler `MSG900W  EQU *  HERE ON PROGRAM CHECK` by way of `CHKERR`, which does `MVC SAVECODE(8),136`, `MVC SAVEPSW(8),PROPSW` and `STM R0,R15,CKPSAVE`; `ERRWAIT` then does `LM R0,R15,CKPSAVE`, `MVC 184(4),SAVEDEV` — 184 is X'B8' — `MVC 136(8),SAVECODE`, and `LPSW WAIT7`. So the zeroed X'B8' is CP restoring `SAVEDEV`, through a statement **this conversion wrote** (`I-53`, which widened it to `MVC IOSSID(8),SAVEDEV` for the eight-byte ESA/390 field), and the interruption code at X'8C' is restored rather than live. The registers are `registers at time of trouble`, which is what makes `GR14=100` meaningful. Two lessons: a wait code in a module one has been editing all week deserves a grep before it is called unexplained, and **evidence read after an error handler has run is evidence about the handler**. The live datum is the program old PSW at X'28', which `CHKERR` saves but does not overwrite. **Closed 30 September.** This entry was a correction of an earlier misreading, not a defect of its own. Its content is preserved above; there is nothing to fix. | closed |
