| **I-30** | `source/cp/DMKIOS` diverges from what CE builds | `source/cp/DMKIOS.ASSEMBLE` line 1318 (`seq 01554000`) is `LH R3,INTTIO`, a real uncommented instruction. After the `PSA` rename `INTTIO` is provably undefined — four modules flag it or its siblings — yet `DMKIOS` assembles with nothing flagged, so CE's build does not contain that line. Ruled out: no applied deck covers `01554000`; the two `*`-disabled `AUXR60` entries have no deck files; `DMKIOT` (10/10) and `DMKDSP` (1/1) matched exactly, so it is `DMKIOS`-specific. **Unexplained.** Caveat on every count measured against `source/cp`: where a number matters, verify it against what `VMFASM` produces. See [19-M1-STEP2.md](19-M1-STEP2.md). | open |
| **I-31** | CP's print spool accumulates across runs | Every `VMFASM` does `CP SPOOL PRT CONT` / `CP CLOSE PRT`, so listings queue. With `00E` drained they sit in the spool, and the first `CP START 00E` flushes **all** of them into `io/print1.listing` — which reached 110,052 lines holding 66 spool files from four separate runs. The pre-deck listings in it still showed `INTTIO EQU INTKFLIN+2`, which I briefly read as evidence my own change had not applied. `CP PURGE PRINTER ALL` before the work and again before the print that matters. | fixed |
| **I-32** | `VMFASM ... DISK` writes `$module LISTING` | The EXEC assembles the updated member (`&ANAME = $&1`), so the listing carries the `$`. `PRINT DMKIOS LISTING A` returns `FILE NOT FOUND` and looks like the `DISK` option was ignored. | documented |
| **I-29** | CE already has a 4 KB storage-key change | `PSA.AUXHRC` carries `HRC004DK V01 ENABLE SUPPORT FOR 4K STORAGE KEYS`, applied across six members — `DMKAPI`, `DMKCLK`, `DMKCPI`, `DMKPSA`, `EQU`, `PSA`. In `PSA` it sets `CPCREG0  DC X'81800CC0'`, CR0 with page size 4 K and segment size 64 K. So `R-12` has prior art **inside the tree**, and the 4 KB key work should start by reading those six decks rather than re-deriving it. Open as a task, not a defect. See [18-TRACEABILITY.md](18-TRACEABILITY.md). **Closed 30 September.** Informational rather than a defect: the 4 KB key change is CE's, it is documented, and `I-104`'s conversion of `DMKCPI`'s `SSK` proceeds from it. Nothing here needs fixing. | closed |
| **I-28** | `XAOPS.MACRO` calls `SAM24`/`SAM31` z/Architecture | The file states "SAM24, SAM31 AND SAM64 ARE Z/ARCHITECTURE, NOT ESA/390". Hercules's opcode table marks `SAM24` (`010C`) and `SAM31` (`010D`) as available in **ESA/390** mode, only `SAM64` (`010E`) as z-only. One of the two is wrong; resolving it needs the ESA/390 Principles of Operation, which this project does not have to hand. Immaterial either way — `BSM` and `BASSM` are unambiguously ESA/390 and proven by execution in test 3 — but it is an unverified claim sitting in a file other work trusts. **Closed 30 September.** Resolved by `SA22-7201-08`, which marks `SAM24` and `SAM31` with flag `N3` -- *"Instruction is added to ESA/390 from z/Architecture"*. Both sources were right and were describing the same fact from different ends: the instructions originated in z/Architecture and were retrofitted into ESA/390, so `XAOPS.MACRO`'s note and Hercules' availability table do not contradict each other. | closed |
| **I-27** | "g4ugm verifies CE against IBM" | Claimed that diffing `g4ugm/vm370.source` against CE proved CE's CP source is IBM's Release 6 source unmodified. The diff was right; the conclusion was not. g4ugm carries CE's own `HRC` update markers and references CE-only `HDK*` modules, so it is the same resolved tree — two copies of one tree agreeing proves nothing about IBM. The check that would have caught it is one `grep` for the other tree's change ids. Corrected in [15-UPDATE-LEVELS.md](15-UPDATE-LEVELS.md); the real baseline is CE's own `maintenance/files/394`. | closed |
| **I-24** | `CODE70` identified as ESA/390's `ARCHTECT` row | `CODE70` is 2 KB pages. **`CODEB0`** is the ESA/390 row — 4 KB pages, 1 MB segments, fullword PTEs, 2,048 segments. | closed |
| **I-25** | `CPCREG0` classified as a constant | It is a live CR0 save area: `STCTL C0,C0,CPCREG0  SAVE IN REAL 0 FOR CP`. Would have been treated as a fixed bit pattern across 38 reference sites. | closed |
| **I-140** | A deck regenerated mid-build is absent from that build, and the log reports it as read either way | `mkrun.py` copies every deck into a card-reader file when the run is *prepared*, not when Hercules reads it, so the ~40 minutes of staging a `full` build spends is a window in which the decks on disk and the cards in the build can diverge. Found by walking into it: a `DMKPGS` card was corrected while the build was at 17 of 102 staged files, and the fix would simply not have been in the result. Nothing says so -- the log prints `readcard dmkpgs xa0036dk a` whichever cards it read, the module assembles, the diagnostics belong to the **old** cards, and every tool that reads a deck (`deckchk.py`, `symchk.py`, `auxcheck`) reports on the version that was **not** built. That last part is what makes it dangerous rather than merely annoying: the measurement and the artefact disagree while both look healthy. Recoverable here because staging is alphabetical and `DMKPGS` was 29th: the one reader file was rewritten in place through `mkrun.card()`, so the column-72 discipline held, 266 cards to 273. Fixed with `iochk.py`, which re-derives every staged file from its deck and compares; `run()` now calls it and refuses to start on any disagreement. **Third door onto the same failure** after `I-131` (a build log overwritten by the build that depended on it) and `I-138` (an AUXLCL entry overwritten by a later generator). The pattern is a stale artefact that reads as current, and the fix is always the same: derive rather than trust. | fixed |
| **I-141** | Two docs in this repo disagreed about group 1 by a factor of twenty, and neither had reconciled with the other | `IPL-WALLS.md` recorded group 1's remainder as *"DMKCPI `SIO` at 00485480, DMKPSA x4 `ISK`, DMKSAV x5 `SIO`/`TIO`"* -- ten or eleven sites. `22-S370-ONLY.md`, in the same `docs/` directory, records **200 nucleus sites across 28 modules**, measured by `tools/s370only.py` from Hercules's own opcode table. **Both were written by me and neither mentions the other.** That is the defect: not a wrong number, but two confident numbers in one repository with no reconciliation between them, which is how a reader ends up believing whichever file they open first. I opened `IPL-WALLS.md` first and planned around eleven sites. A fresh sweep -- every `SIO SIOF TIO CLRIO HIO HDV TCH ISK SSK RRB STIDC`, minus every range the 52 decks already replace, minus comment lines, keeping only modules named in CP's own `CPLOAD EXEC` -- gives **95 sites still unconverted**, which is consistent with 200 total: the decks have already converted 105, nearly all of them channel sites in `DMKIOS` and the bootstrap chain. So `22-S370-ONLY.md` was right, `IPL-WALLS.md` was wrong, and the figure quoted in conversation for several sessions was the wrong one. The tell was independent: **DMKPSA has five `ISK`s, not four** -- a count taken by reading is not off by one in a list of five. Fixed by `privchk.py`, which reports the REMAINDER where `s370only.py` reports the TOTAL, and which was **reconciled against it before being trusted**: with the deck subtraction disabled the two agree 199 against 200, the one difference being `STIDC`, missing from this tool's opcode list until the check found it. Every other opcode matches site for site -- TIO 71, SIO 48, ISK 38, SSK 16, RRB 13, HIO 5, HDV 4, TCH 3, CLRIO 1 -- and both name the same four standalone utilities as out of scope. Two tools written months apart by different reasoning, agreeing on nine opcodes, is the strongest evidence either is right; that reconciliation is what the two prose docs never had. The substantive consequence stands either way: **19 of the remaining channel sites are in `DMKLD00E`**, the standalone loader that loads the nucleus before CP exists, so they cannot ride with the deferred multi-channel work -- they are the IPL path. | fixed |
| **I-142** | I promoted one site's correct answer to a general rule, twice in two hours, and the rule was already answered by CE's own configuration | `privchk.py` was written saying a 2 KB key pair *"does not get converted, it gets DELETED"*. Reading `DMKPTR` 01012000 showed that is wrong -- CP keeps a key **per 2 KB half of its own accord**, `SWPKEY1` and `SWPKEY2`, and packs both hardware keys into one register (`ISK R15,R6` / `SLL R15,8` / `ISK R15,R14`) because `ISK` only loads bits 24-31, feeding a **two-byte** mask `X'0202'` and two stores. Delete the second `ISK` and R15 holds one key in the wrong half against a mask matching nothing, with page replacement then running on garbage reference bits. So I replaced it with *"read the one key once and replicate it into both halves"* -- **and promoted that to a rule too.** It is right for a READ and meaningless for a WRITE: `DMKPTR` 00643000 does `L R3,SWPFLAG` / `N R3,=A(X'F8F8')` / `SSK R3,R8` / `SRL R3,8` / `SSK R3,R6`, writing two *independent* guest keys, and there is nothing to replicate from. Two overclaims of the same shape in two hours, both from reading one site and generalising. **Then the actual answer turned out to be already in the tree.** `23-STORAGE-KEYS.md` records that CE sets `CPCREG0 DC X'81800CC0'` where base `PSA.MACRO` has `X'80800CC0'` -- the added bit is `CR0_STORKEY_4K` -- so **CE already runs with 4 KB keys and both halves of every pair already address one key**. Verified rather than inherited this time: Hercules tests that bit in exactly three places, and they are `insert_storage_key`, `reset_reference_bit` and `set_storage_key`, raising a special-operation exception when it is *off*, which is the S/370 rule for models with the 4 KB-key feature. So reading replicates and writing takes `SWPKEY1` -- because the second `SSK` already wins today -- and **both reproduce current behaviour exactly rather than deviating from it**. `SWPKEY1`/`SWPKEY2` stay independent guest state regardless, since `DMKPRV` answers a guest `ISK` from `SWPTABLE` and not from hardware. The analysis is in `23-STORAGE-KEYS.md`, where the topic already lived, which is `I-141`'s lesson applied. | fixed |
| **I-143** | The verification build came back 180 modules clean of 192, and all twelve remaining diagnostics were four mechanical mistakes no tool was checking | The DAT conversion itself held: `asmerr.py` reports **CONFIRMED 0, MISSED 0, COLLIDED 0, SILENT 176** -- every one of the 176 undefined-symbol diagnostics the previous build raised is gone, in modules that did assemble, so all ~700 cards across 23 modules applied and every renamed site was converted. What remained had nothing to do with ESA/390. **(1) SCOPE, 7 diagnostics from one placement decision.** The nine architecture constants went into `CORE.COPY`, beside the structures they describe, which read like the right call: `CORE.COPY` is copied by 42 modules and `EQU.COPY` by **179 of 192**, and `DMKCDB`, `DMKCDM` and `DMKDRD` name `SEGINVAL` and `SEGSTOM` while copying `EQU` and not `CORE`. The distinction missed is between a FIELD and a CONSTANT: `SEGPTO` belongs to its structure, and so do `PAGTSWP`, `PAGBMP` and `PAGSWPE` because they are computed from its fields, but `X'7FFFF000'` is the ESA/390 STD whoever is looking at it. Moved to `EQU.COPY` as `XA0037DK`, after checking that of the 13 modules without `COPY EQU` only `DMKSYS` has a deck and its deck names none of the nine. **(2) LABEL-LOST.** `DMKPGS` 00302000 is `PURCONT  L  R3,VMSEG`; the replacement converted the instruction and dropped the label, dangling three branches. **(3) LABEL-DUP.** The same deck emitted `CKSEG EQU *` while replacing 01217000, but `CKSEG EQU *` is record 01216000, which it does not replace -- the fix is to emit nothing, since the surviving label already labels the card below. **(4) CONT-ORPHAN.** `DMKCPP` 62000000 carried an `X` in **column 72** and 62100000 was its continuation; replacing only the first card left the continuation a standalone statement whose operand field the assembler read as an opcode. This is `R-04` from the other side -- not a marker written where it should not be, but one REMOVED where it was load-bearing -- and `mkdeck.card()` asserts column 72 is blank on cards it WRITES, which is exactly why the failure had to arrive through a record it does not write. **All four are decidable from the base source and the deck**, so `replchk.py` now asserts them and `mk()` refuses to build on a violation; it reproduces all twelve diagnostics in under a second, 7 + 1 + 1 + 1, with no false positives and none missed. Getting there took four passes and three were my own noise: resolving every operand name gave **411** candidates because I split on a double space and swept in my own comment prose (`FLAGS`, `PAGE`, `WHOLE`, `BACK`); then 85; then 45 restricted to symbols this project introduced; then 10 once the reach model learned that `DMKIOT` reaches the PSA not by `COPY PSA` but by a bare `         PSA` -- **PSA is a macro, invoked** -- and that `SAVE COPY` copies further members itself. The three real failures were in that first list of 411 and were invisible there: **a check that says ten things of which three are true cannot be read.** | fixed |
| **I-144** | `w()` cannot tell "Hercules finished" from "Hercules was killed", so a reaped build would have produced a FALSE PASS on the whole DAT conversion | The 1 October verification build was reaped 13 minutes in -- 15 card files of 104 read, no module assembled -- and the second attempt 4 minutes in after 3 cards. Both times Hercules and the `nohup`'d driver went together, the log ended on a normal `Ready;` with no error, 7.5 GB of 8 GB memory was free, 22 GB of disk, and no kill record anywhere in the kernel log, so the cause is above the kernel and `setsid` on the driver did not prevent it. **The cause is not the finding.** `w()` loops until `pgrep -c hercules` is zero and returns **success** when it is, which is indistinguishable from a clean finish. Had the driver survived the kill it would have run `chk` and then `asmchk` against a log holding 14% of the work, found no diagnostics -- because there were no assemblies to produce any -- and printed a clean verdict. Seventh check-that-cannot-fail in two days and the worst-shaped of them: the previous six cost time, this one asserts success. Neither existing check looks at completeness, because `boot_failed()` reads the boot and `asmchk` reads diagnostic severity, and a truncated run is healthy by both measures. `mkrun.incomplete()` compares the log against **`hercules.rc`** -- the script is what the run was *asked* to do, and the same `mk` writes both, so no expected number is remembered anywhere -- counting card reads, assembly passes and the shutdown. `chk()` calls it FIRST, so `asmchk` can never see a truncated log. On the dead log: *"the script asked for 104 card files and the log shows 15."* | fixed |
| **I-145** | A new tool overwrote an existing one of the same name, destroying 308 lines with 102 | Asked for a standing per-report module count, I wrote `tools/status.py` with a shell redirect. `status.py` already existed -- 308 lines deriving per-module conversion state from the nucleus load list, the three assembly EXECs, the AUXLCLs and a source scan, with separate PSA and I/O columns and a comment recording that reading `CONVERTED` as "done" is what let five channel sites sit inside converted loops for three commits (`I-67`). My 102-line replacement answered a **different** question -- what one run produced -- and took the better tool's name. Recovered from git and renamed to `runstat.py`, with the distinction written into both: `status.py` answers *is this module converted*, `runstat.py` answers *did this run do what it was asked*. **The failure is procedural and it is the day's pattern in miniature.** Every generator in this project refuses to write a bad card, a short sequence number, an over-length comment or a drifting staged file; nothing warns that `cat > file` is a destructive write to a path that may already hold something better. I had also just spent the afternoon finding that I quote numbers instead of measuring them, and then named a file without looking. Caught only because a git hook reported the file as **modified** rather than untracked -- one character of difference in `git status` between a new tool and a destroyed one. | fixed |
| **I-146** | Two hours spent protecting a seventy-minute build from a container that is reclaimed, when the answer was not to need one | The verification build died three times on 1 October -- 13 minutes in, 4 minutes in, 9 minutes in -- each time taking Hercules, the `nohup`'d driver and (the third time) the watcher together, leaving a log ending on a normal `Ready;`. I theorised about process groups, added `setsid` to the driver, wrote a liveness watcher, and concluded from three deaths and one 70-minute success that background work does not outlive my own foreground calls. **One command settled it and I ran it two hours late: `uptime` said 39 minutes against five hours of work.** The container is reclaimed and restarted; files survive on disk, processes do not. Nothing can be done to a background job to make it survive that, so the entire line of investigation was wasted — and the measurement that ended it costs nothing and was available at the first death. **The fix is structural, not defensive.** `full` stages 104 files and assembles 186 modules because it assumes nothing about the snapshot; but the staleness report already names exactly what changed since `SNAP-2` -- **45 inputs** -- and `SNAP-2` holds everything else. So a `spec` verb restores, stages only those, and reassembles only the 58 modules affected: 49 files instead of 104, 58 assemblies instead of 186. Two false starts getting there, both mine. `quick` refused because it requires every named module to HAVE a deck, and **38 of the 58 need reassembly only because `CORE COPY`'s DSECT moved under them** -- `PAGORIG` added, `PAGBMP` 1048 to 3136 -- so they have nothing to apply. And `mkrun` had carried the right primitive all along, `asm:MODULE`, *"VMFASM a module already on disk"*, documented in its own spec grammar, which I did not read before concluding the grammar could not express this. Third time in one day that the answer was already in the tree (`I-141`, `I-142`). | fixed |
| **I-147** | The module denominator was reported wrong three times running, by the tool written that morning to stop exactly that | Asked for a standing count of modules OK, defective and missing, I reported the expected total as **198**, three times. Corrected it to **192**. The truth is **186**. Both wrong figures were counts of `ASMBLING` *lines*, and some modules assemble twice -- in the 13:15 build the repeats are `DMKCDB`, `DMKCDM`, `DMKCPP`, `DMKDRD`, `DMKPGS` and `DMKRIO`, which is the five defective modules plus one. So the inflation was **caused by the failures the count existed to report**. Two separate defects, each the day's pattern: `read()` counted occurrences rather than distinct modules, and `expected()` took the largest count from **any** archived log, including `f1-20261001-111951.log`. A third thing was hidden underneath: the four categories summed to 185 of 186 and the tool said nothing about the one left out -- `DMKRIO`, which emits only `IFO197 *** MNOTE ***` (`I-34`'s 3375/3390 notes) and is therefore neither `NO STATEMENTS FLAGGED` nor defective. **A category that silently absorbs a module is how a real failure hides**, so there is now an explicit `MNOTE-ONLY` column and an assertion that the four categories sum to the modules started. Re-measured, the completed 13:15 build is **186 modules: 180 OK, 5 DEFECT, 0 MISSING, 1 MNOTE-only.** Worth recording as its own entry rather than folded into `I-141`, because `I-141` was about quoting a figure somebody else wrote down and this is about a figure my own tool computed from the evidence and got wrong -- which is the harder failure to notice, since it arrives with the authority of a measurement. | fixed |
| **I-148** | The build spent 42 seconds of pause per card file to cover one hundredth of a second of work, which is why it could never finish | `I-146` established that this container is reclaimed at every turn boundary, so a build only runs inside a single foreground call -- about ten minutes. That made the per-file cost decisive, and measuring it was overdue: each staged file carried `pause 8` after the `devinit`, `pause 14` after `/cp start 00c`, and `pause 20` after the `readcard`. **Forty-two seconds.** The log reports every one of those CMS commands finishing in `T=0.01/0.01` -- *including* the `readcard` of a 272-card deck. Over 49 files that is 34 minutes of pure waiting, and it is the entire reason a `full` build takes seventy minutes rather than fifteen. Cut to 3/5/7. **The cut is only safe because of what was added with it**: a short pause does not fail visibly, it reads a card file PARTIALLY, and a truncated UPDATE deck applied to a module is far worse than a slow build -- it is `I-137`'s shape, an object deck that exists and is a lie. So `incomplete()` now counts `RDR FILE nnnn HAS BEEN READ` against the readcards issued and refuses the run if any file did not report. The `vmfasm` pauses are deliberately untouched: those cover real work and the 30-to-70 correction above them is a scar. Two further findings from slicing the build. **Hercules outlives the shell that starts it** -- a 9-minute `timeout` killed the driver and Hercules staged on alone to 17 files, after which `run()` refused to start a second instance (`I-117`'s guard, earning its place again). And **I sized the first slice from the specs rather than from the generated script**: 13 specs that I counted as 17 files, where `hercules.rc` asked for 21, because `mac:DMKLCL` restages the macro library's member list. Same error as every wrong number today -- an arithmetic assumption standing in for reading the artifact. Slices are now sized from the rc. | fixed |
| **I-149** | A move is two halves and only one was wired up: nine constants left `CORE COPY` and never arrived in `EQU COPY` | `I-143` moved the nine ESA/390 architecture constants out of `CORE COPY`, copied by 42 modules, into `EQU COPY`, copied by 179 of 192, because `DMKCDB`, `DMKCDM` and `DMKDRD` name `SEGINVAL` and `SEGSTOM` without copying `CORE`. The next assembly returned **all nine as `IFO188 UNDEFINED SYMBOL`** -- `SEGINVAL` 14 times, `PAGINV` 5, `SEGSTOM` 4, `SEGPTOM` 3, `SEGPTLF` 3, `PAGPFRM` 2, `PAGINVW` 2, `SEGSTLM` 1, `SEGFLGM` 1 -- across five modules including `DMKATS` and `DMKBLD`, **both of which had assembled clean the previous build**. `CORE XA0033DK` deleted them and `EQU XA0037DK` never added them. The deck was not missing and not misapplied: `EQU.XA0037DK` and `EQU.AUXLCL` were both staged onto the A-disk and the archived staging log shows both `readcard equ auxlcl a` and `readcard equ xa0037dk a` with `HAS BEEN READ`. The assembly log shows `APPLYING 'CORE R10547DK H1'`, `APPLYING 'CORE XA0033DK A1'`, and **nothing whatever for EQU**. `DMKLCL EXEC` is why. It reads like a macro-library member list and it is not one -- it is the list of members whose **AUX decks `VMFASM` applies** -- and it named `CORE` because `I-143` added CORE's deck, while `EQU` had never needed an entry before because this project had never updated it. One line, ` &1 &2 EQU      COPY`. **The generalisable part is the shape, not the line.** Every check this project has asks whether an artifact is correct: `mkdeck` that a card is well formed, `auxcheck` that a deck is listed in its member's AUXLCL, `symchk` that a symbol does not collide, `replchk` that a replacement preserves what it covers, `iochk` that the staged card matches the deck. **Not one of them asks whether a member this project updates is wired into the control file that causes updates to be applied at all** -- so a deck can be generated, verified, staged, read and confirmed, and still be inert. `auxcheck` came closest and was satisfied, because `EQU.AUXLCL` did list `XA0037DK` faithfully; the AUXLCL was correct and nobody was reading it. | fixed |
| **I-150** | A run in which every command was rejected by CP shut down cleanly and `chk` passed it | The slice after `I-149`'s fix staged its card, ran `vmfmac`, ran ten `vmfasm`s, shut Hercules down tidily, and did **nothing**. CP had booted correctly -- `DMKCPI966I Initialization complete` -- and then `cp disc` / `logon maint cpcms` returned **`?CP: LOGON`**, so the virtual machine stayed in CP READ and every command after it went to CP, which did not know any of them: `?CP: READCARD`, `?CP: CPACC`, `?CP: VMFMAC`, and ten `?CP: VMFASM`. **Third time in two days a run has reported success while doing nothing** (`I-144`, the reaped build; `I-112`, 48 identical card failures over 25 minutes), and `boot_failed()` could not see it because it looks for a boot that did not happen rather than for commands that were refused. `incomplete()` now voids a run containing a rejection of a command the script issued. **The cause is a race, not a broken sequence**: the identical slice with the identical preamble ran correctly twenty minutes later, and the failing one landed while CP was still auto-logging `CPWATCH`, `CMSBATCH` and `WAKEUP` with `AUTOLOG1` logging off in the same second -- inside the 35-second pause that exists to cover exactly that. A wait-for-quiet would be better than a fixed pause; it is not blocking while the detection holds. **And the check was too strict on its first outing, which is the part worth keeping.** It voided any run containing `?CP:` at all, and CP's own IPL dialogue emits `?CP: COLD` before the logon as ordinary noise -- so it would have discarded the first good run in a day. Narrowed to the commands the script actually issues. I wrote that check to catch a run that did nothing and then nearly used it to throw away a run that did everything: **a check that is too strict destroys good evidence as surely as a lax one accepts bad**, and nothing in this register had said so before. | fixed |
| **I-151** | `mkrun` left staging files from a previous, larger run, and `iochk` refused the next one | A 49-file run left `io/r12.txt` through `io/r48.txt` behind. The next run needed 11 files, wrote `r00`-`r10`, and `iochk` compared all of them against the decks on disk -- finding that a leftover held the **old** `DMKLCL.EXEC`, which `I-149` had just changed -- and refused to start. That is `iochk` being right: those cards would not have been read, but a check cannot tell a leftover from a live file without being told, and guessing is how `I-140` happened in the first place. `mkrun.main()` now clears `io/r*.txt` before writing. Leftovers from a bigger run are the same stale-artifact shape as the overwritten build log (`I-131`), the overwritten AUXLCL entry (`I-138`) and the snapshotted card file (`I-140`), one directory further in -- the fourth door onto it, and the first where the existing guard caught the problem instead of me. | fixed |
| **I-152** | `PRG018` was never the segment tables: `CR0`'s translation-format field is checked first, and CE's value says 64 KB segments | **The most consequential retraction in this register.** `IPL-WALLS.md` recorded wall 11 as *"`TRANS` loads an S/370 STD and `LRA` rejects it"*, diagnosed from a dump showing `CR1 = 05FFC840` and a segment table of `F00561D0 F00562A8 …` -- System/370 STEs with `SEGPLEN` in the high nibble where ESA/390 requires bit 0 to be zero. That reading was correct about the tables and **wrong about the cause**, and the whole DAT conversion was sequenced behind it. Hercules's `dat.c` checks `CR0` at `[3.11.3.2]`, **before it fetches a single table entry**: `if ((regs->CR(0) & CR0_TRAN_FMT) != CR0_TRAN_ESA390) goto tran_spec_excp;` with `CR0_TRAN_FMT = 0x00F80000` (bits 8-12) and `CR0_TRAN_ESA390 = 0x00B00000` (1 MB segments, 4 KB pages). CE sets `CPCREG0 DC X'81800CC0'` in `094/PSA.HRC004DK`, and `X'81800CC0' & X'00F80000'` is **`X'00800000'`** -- System/370 for 4 KB pages and **64 KB segments**. Two bits, 10 and 11. **The proof is a converted nucleus whose tables were verified correct first**, read out of the stopped machine on 2 October: `CR1 = X'00FFC005'` (bit 0 clear, origin `X'FFC000'` 4096-aligned, STL in bits 25-31), `STE 0 = X'00FFAC0F'` (bit 0 clear, PTO `X'FFAC00'` 64-aligned, I clear, PTL 15), a page table reading `00000000 00001000 00002000 00003000 …` -- textbook ESA/390 fullword PTEs stepping by 4096 -- and the **identical `PRG018` at the identical instruction**, `B170 1000` = `LRA R7,0(,R1)` at `X'3DA1A'`, inside `TRANS.MACRO`'s expansion. Everything the conversion touched was right and the one constant it never touched was wrong. Fixed with one card, `CPCREG0 DC X'81B00CC0'`, which keeps bit 7 (`CR0_STORKEY_4K`, which `23-STORAGE-KEYS.md` depends on) and the external masks in `X'0CC0'`. **Only `DMKPSA` reassembles**, not the 166 modules that invoke the `PSA` macro: `PSA.MACRO` opens with `AIF ('&SYSECT' EQ 'DMKPSA').PSA1`, so it expands as a `DSECT` everywhere else and generates no storage -- the `DC` is CP's real page 0 in `DMKPSA` and documentation everywhere else. **The lesson is about diagnosis, not about CR0.** A dump showed a wrong value, the wrong value was real, and I never asked whether it was the value the hardware complains about first. The emulator's own source answers that in four lines and I read it a day late. | fixed |
| **I-153** | The 58 reassembled modules were lost because no BUILD snapshot was taken before the nucleus write -- the one thing `BUILD-CYCLE.md` says in plain words | `snapshot.py` enforces two kinds. A **build** snapshot is taken *before* a nucleus write: it boots under S/370, runs CMS, and can assemble. A **test** snapshot is taken *after*: it holds the converted nucleus and boots only under ESA/390. `BUILD-CYCLE.md` states the circularity -- *"a successful build replaces the CP that performed it with one that cannot perform the next one"* -- and says to snapshot before the write. After reassembling all 58 modules (58 OK, 0 DEFECT, each run verified complete) I went straight to the write and **took no build snapshot**, so the only copy of that work is inside `SNAP-DAT`, which cannot run a build. Restoring it to add `I-152`'s one-card `CPCREG0` fix gave `ipl 6A1` with **no `DMKCPI` messages at all** -- an ESA/390 nucleus asked to boot under `ARCHMODE S/370`. The 58 assemblies have to be redone. **The compounding error is the diagnosis.** The symptoms were `HHCPN096E Device 0:000C busy or interrupt pending` and no CMS output, and I read the first as the card reader failing to drain and raised the staging pauses from 3/5/7 to 4/6/14 on that basis. The reader was busy because **nothing was reading it**: CP was not up. The second run failed identically, which is what finally sent me to look at whether CP had booted rather than at the reader. The pause change is reverted -- 3/5/7 is measured good at 23 files and 20 files, every one reporting `HAS BEEN READ`, and a change made on void evidence does not get to stay just because it is harmless. **Two things did work.** `incomplete()`'s `HAS BEEN READ` count refused both runs instead of letting an unapplied deck pass as success (`I-148`), and `I-111`'s staleness guard was already shouting that `SNAP-2` was stale -- I just restored the wrong snapshot rather than the stale one. Fourth time in two days that `I-111`'s property has cost work. | fixed |
| **I-154** | The completeness check passed a run that assembled 15 of 19 modules, because it counted the wrong verb | `incomplete()` was written for `I-144` to stop a reaped run being mistaken for a finished one, and it counts `/readcard` and `/asmdmk` lines in `hercules.rc` against the log. The sliced build added a third mechanism, **`/vmfasm`** -- one line per module from an `asm:MODULE` spec -- and the check knew nothing about it. Half 2 of the assembly pass was reclaimed after 15 of its 19 modules and `incomplete()` reported **COMPLETE**. The four survivors still held their previous object decks, so a nucleus written from that state would have mixed 55 modules built against the new `CORE COPY` DSECT with 4 built against the old one -- `PAGORIG` absent, `PAGBMP` 1048 instead of 3136 -- which assembles silently and corrupts two page tables at run time. **Fourth false pass in three days, and this one by the check written to stop the other three.** The lesson is narrow and worth having: a completeness check that enumerates mechanisms has to be extended whenever a mechanism is added, and nothing warns you. It now counts `/vmfasm` against `ASMBLING` and, on the failed log, says *"the script asked for 19 module assembly(s) and the log shows 15 -- the modules after the last one named still hold their PREVIOUS object deck."* | fixed |
| **I-155** | `snapshot.py` assumes one build is one log, and the sliced build is nine | Taking the build snapshot that `I-153` was about failed: `### SNAP-DAT2 stays INVALID -- no deck APPLYING line for 37 patched module(s)`, naming almost every module in the conversion. The snapshot was fine; the **evidence** was not reachable. `snapshot.py take` validates a snapshot against ONE log, because a `full` build is one Hercules run and therefore one log. A sliced build (`I-146`) is nine runs, and `run()` archives each log as the next begins (`I-131`), so no single log contains more than its own slice. Resolved by concatenating the day's nine archived slice logs plus the current one -- 78 assemblies and 766 `APPLYING` lines between them -- and validating against that, which gave `--- SNAP-DAT2 is VALID (build): 40 patched modules with their decks applied and a TXTLCL created, taken before the nucleus write`. Recorded rather than patched because the fix has a design question in it: either `snapshot.py` learns to read a set of logs, or the driver maintains a running build journal across slices. The second is better -- a journal is derived from the runs rather than reconstructed from whatever logs happen to survive -- and neither should be written while a measurement is in flight. **Three of this project's own guards interacted to produce a false negative**: log archiving, one-log validation, and sliced building. Each is right alone. | documented |
| **I-156** | Wall 11 has fallen: `PRG018` is gone, and the next wall is a privileged-operation exception on `SSM` | Recorded as the measurement rather than a defect. With `I-152`'s `CPCREG0 DC X'81B00CC0'` and the full DAT conversion assembled -- 59 modules, 59 OK, 0 DEFECT -- the nucleus was written (`SYSTEM LOAD DECK COMPLETE`, `Nucleus loaded on VM50-1`, wait state `12`) and IPLed under ESA/390. **The translation-specification exception that has stood since 30 September is gone.** SDL 4.9.1 reports `CR00=81B00CC0` and `CR01=00FFC005` -- the fix live and the designation in ESA/390 form -- and the new failure is `HHC00801I Privileged-operation exception`, `PSW=040D0000 0006D130`, `INST=8000D129` = **`SSM 297(13)`** at `X'6D130'`. So the hardware now accepts the designation, the segment table and the page tables, and CP runs past the point where `TRANS` used to fault. Decoding the new PSW: byte 0 `04` sets **bit 5, DAT ON**; byte 1 `0D` sets bit 12 (ESA/390 mode, `wall 2`'s requirement) and **bit 15, problem state**. `SSM` is privileged, so problem state is sufficient to explain the exception; what is not yet known is whether this is CP's own code running with the problem-state bit wrongly set, or a virtual machine's `SSM` that CP failed to intercept. `GR03=00FFC000` is the segment-table address, so the machine is running translated. **On the engines**: `dualdiff` printed *"the engines DISAGREE"*, and that verdict is mostly missing data rather than disagreement -- it says so itself for the registers, *"NOT a disagreement, missing data"* -- because Hercules 3.13 produced no CP output beyond `ipl 6A1` while 4.9.1 gave the full diagnosis. Worth running down separately, and not an architectural difference. | documented |
| **I-157** | The ESA/390 test machine had 256 MB where `R-25` requires 16, so every IPL this week measured M1 on an M5 machine | `mkrun.archmode()` sets `MAINSIZE 16` for S/370 and set **`MAINSIZE 256`** for ESA/390, with a comment reasoning that *"ESA/390 is 31-bit and the whole point of the project"*. That reasoning skips a decision already taken: `R-25` keeps Hercules at **16 MB real** deliberately, because real storage above 16 MB is **M5** and deferred (`STATE.md`: *"a gate to be lifted once the conversion lands, not a decision to stay below the line"*). So the configuration quietly granted the thing the milestone withholds. **The cost was a day of diagnosis of a wall the harness manufactured.** On 2 October, with wall 11 closed, CP IPLed, stayed alive, printed nothing, and looped in `DMKFRE` -- three PSW samples in `X'40766'`-`X'40BEE'`, `GR0` of 10, 11 and 59 doublewords, small ordinary allocations failing, and `DMKFRE`'s own documentation describing the loop exactly (*"DMKPTRFR IS CALLED ... AND STEP 2 IS THEN REPEATED (FROM THE BEGINNING)"*). I measured it, named it, reported it as the live wall, and chased it into my own `DMKBLD` arithmetic, where I checked and discarded two further guesses -- `SLL R5,4` (correct: `R5` comes from PTL, `+1` gives units of sixteen, `x16` gives 256 pages) and a 256-segment allocation (refuted by measurement: six page tables at a 3200-byte stride, 19 KB in total, not an exhaustion). **Setting `MAINSIZE 16` moved the loop out of `DMKFRE` entirely**, to `X'3D9FA'`-`X'3DB68'`, the module holding `TRANS`'s `LRA`. So the exhaustion was a consequence of a 256 MB machine whose CORTABLE and paging structures CP sizes from real storage, and it was never CP's defect. **The general failure is that a test harness parameter contradicted a documented project gate and nothing checked.** `archmode()` has guards for `CPUMODEL` and `ECPSVM` because each caused a real failure (`I-115`); `MAINSIZE` had a comment arguing for the wrong value instead. A risk register entry is only as good as the configuration that honours it. | fixed |
| **I-158** | Four wrong module identifications in one afternoon, all from matching numbers to plausible owners, and the fix is a lookup table | On 2 October, with wall 11 closed and CP alive but mute, I identified the failing code **four times** and was wrong three times. **(1)** The `SSM` privileged-operation exception, reported as the wall from a single `pgmtrace` line -- CP handled it and ran on for three minutes. **(2)** A loop in `DMKFRE`, measured and written up as the wall -- an artefact of `I-157`'s 256 MB test machine. **(3)** `SHRTABLE`, and therefore `DMKATS`, because three offsets fitted its DSECT -- the block is a `VMBLOK`: offset 20 is `VMSIZE`, not `SHRTSIZE`, which `TRANS.MACRO` compares for its range check, and I had ignored that the base registers are loaded from different places. **(4)** `PSA` field names from an offset calculator I wrote in thirty seconds, which ignores `ORG` and `AIF` and returned `DSPLPSW` where the answer must be `ACORETBL`. I also presented four instructions of the CALLER's code (`N`, `SRDL R7,8`, `A`, `L R9,12(,R7)`) as part of `TRANS`'s expansion; `SRDL` appears nowhere in the macro. **Every wrong answer came from matching a number against a plausible owner with nothing to check it against, and every right one came from a uniquely-owned fact** -- `DC C'FREE'` exists only in `DMKFRE`, which is why that one identification survived scrutiny. **The way out was a table, not more care.** Hercules's `savecore` writes storage to a host file in one command (`savecore /tmp/nuc.bin 0 7FFFF`, 512 KB), CP modules carry their own names and message identifiers as EBCDIC constants, and `CPLOAD EXEC` gives the load order -- so `whereis.py` maps an address to its module, with the load order validating the map's own ordering. Combined with **signature search instead of structure matching** it closed the question in minutes: the disassembly shows `LRA R7,0(,R1)`, `TRANS.MACRO` puts the result register in that position, and exactly three of 174 nucleus modules issue `TRANS 7,1` -- `DMKGRF`, `DMKPGS`, `DMKRGB` -- of which only `DMKPGS` loads anywhere near the address. A register number is a fact checkable against the whole tree in one grep; an offset that happens to fit a DSECT is not. **The standing rule this earns: no identification without its source named inline.** All four wrong answers were stated flatly; every correct one came with a file to check. | fixed |
| **I-159** | A converted test kept its branch sense and changed its QUESTION, making a `TRANS OPT=(DEFER)` into dead code | `DMKPGS` 00956000 is `CLI SEGPAGE+3,SEGINV` -- an **equality** test. Byte 3 equals `SEGINV` exactly only when the page-table origin's low byte is zero *and* the invalid flag is set: the "never built" case. The two cards after it separate a **second** case, built-but-invalid (migrating), and that is the one the `TRANS 7,1,OPT=(DEFER)` at 00962000 exists to wait for. I converted it to `TM SEGPTO+3,SEGINVAL` with `BO NEXTSEG`. **The branch sense was right** -- `BO` branches on CC3, matching `CLI`+`BE` -- so this is not the inversion `block.py`'s docstring warns about. It is worse: a bit test cannot ask *equals exactly*, so any invalid entry took `NEXTSEG`, reaching 00958000 implied the bit was clear, `BZ PGOUT2A` was always taken, and **the `TRANS` became unreachable**. Fixed by keeping the equality test with the new constant -- an unbuilt ESA/390 STE reads `X'20'`, invalid with PTL zero, confirmed by reading CP's own segment table where unused entries are `00000020`. **Found by a systematic sweep rather than by reading**: every record a deck replaces, compared by opcode class against its original, which turned up exactly three equality-to-bit conversions across 53 decks. The other two survived scrutiny -- `DMKBLD` 00321000 is correct (`TM` with mask `X'0F'` gives CC3 only when all four PTL bits are set, i.e. PTL 15 = 256 pages, precisely what `CLI SEGPLEN,X'F0'` asked) and `DMKPGS` 00317000 is equivalent or better. One defect, one equivalence, one improvement, from one sweep. Now `testchk.py`, which reports CANDIDATES only: the tool finds the shape change, a human reads the mask. **It did not clear the hang.** CP moved from `X'3D9FA'`-`X'3DB68'` to `X'407F2'` and still prints nothing, so the defect was real and not the cause -- making the `TRANS` reachable changed the path and CP stalls further on. | fixed |
| **I-160** | The snapshot validator now reads a build JOURNAL, not one log | `I-155` was worked around by concatenating the day's archived slice logs and validating against that. The workaround failed on the next cycle, because a second nucleus write put a write log into the concatenation and `snapshot.py` correctly refused to call that a pre-write BUILD state -- so a fix cycle completed with no build snapshot at all. Replaced with a journal the driver maintains: `restore` truncates `$C/build.journal`, and `run()` appends each finished log to it, so the journal is the evidence for everything done since the restore, **derived by appending rather than reconstructed from whichever logs survive**. A nucleus write lands in it too, which is right: after a write the state is no longer a build state, and the validator says so, which makes the rule *snapshot before the write* enforced rather than remembered. | fixed |
| **I-161** | A sweep for multi-bit `TM` masks found two candidates and both were correct, including one that preserves an IBM overlap trick | Written after `I-159` on the reasoning that `TM` gives CC3 only when **all** selected bits are set, so `BO` asks *all* and `BNZ`/`BM` ask *any* -- the same confusion as `I-159` from the other direction. Two hits across 53 decks, both sound. `DMKBLD` 00321000: mask `SEGPTLF` (`X'0F'`, four bits) with `BO`, which is CC3 only when PTL is 15, i.e. 256 pages -- exactly what the original `CLI SEGPLEN,X'F0'` asked. `DMKBLD` 00246230: mask `SEGENQ+SEGINVAL` (`X'60'`) with `BNO` kept from the original. **`SEGENQ` is `X'40'` -- bit 25, the lowest bit of the ESA/390 page-table origin** -- and `CORE.XA0033DK` justifies keeping it there with *"IT IS READ ONLY WHEN THE POINTER IS ZERO, SO ONLY WITH I SET"*. That justification is **wrong about the order**: at 00246230 the `TM` is read *before* the pointer check two cards later. The conclusion survives for a better reason, which the comment should have given: bit 25 is inside the **System/370** page-table origin as well (bits 8-28), so `SEGENQ` overlapped the origin in the original architecture too. It is IBM's own trick and the conversion preserves it exactly, because the bit position is identical in both. **A clean sweep is a result**: two classes now swept (`I-159`, this) with one defect and three vindications between them, which is what a check that is neither too strict nor too lax looks like. | documented |
| **I-162** | The five-day IPL hang was **our** half-converted group: one of five instructions in `DMKPTR` `GETENTRY` was converted to 1 MB geometry and the four feeding it were left at 64 KB | `XA0036DK` converted 00338000, the swap-table displacement, to `LA R5,256*L'PAGPFRA+(SWPFLAG-SWPVM)(R1,R7)`. The four cards that produce its operands kept System/370 geometry: 00321000 `N R1,=XL4'0000F000'` (a **four-bit** page index -- 16 pages, so every page above 15 aliases), 00322000 `SRL R1,11` (page*2, a two-byte entry), 00323000 `SR R7,R1`, 00337000 `SLL R1,2`. `LRA` returns the real address of the invalid PTE in R7 on CC=2, so R1 must be page*`L'PAGPFRA` for `SR R7,R1` to reach the start of the array and page*8 for the swap entry; the fix is three cards -- mask `X'000FF000'`, `SRL R1,10`, `SLL R1,1`. **Verified arithmetically against the live nucleus before changing anything**: for virtual `X'B7000'` (page 183 of segment 0) the code computed `R5 = X'FFB30E'`, which is not even entry-aligned, and read `X'61'` there -- `SWPALLOC` set -- so `TM SWPFLAG,SWPTRANS+SWPALLOC / BNZ INTRAN` enqueued the request and waited for an I/O nobody had started. Correct is `R5 = X'FFB5B8'`, `SWPFLAG = X'40'`, and the test falls through to the not-resident path. A CCW trace on `06A1`-`06A3` across twelve seconds of the loop showed **zero channel programs**, which is what waiting for an unissued I/O looks like from outside. After the fix CP printed `DMKDMP908I SYSTEM FAILURE; CODE PTR020` and a 30,669-line dump -- **the first CP message this nucleus has ever produced**. **Converting one member of such a group is worse than converting none**: the result is a plausible address pointing at the wrong entry, which is why this presented as a paging hang rather than as an assembly error. Three of my own conclusions were retracted on the way here, each by a dump rather than by rereading: the savearea pointer `FFFFF9F8` (real, not corrupt -- the saveareas genuinely live in the top megabyte and the chain is well-formed; a latent `I-128`, not this bug), "the loop moved after `I-159`" (`RESTART` **is** `X'3D9F8'` -- it never moved, only my sampling did), and `X'4EC'` as `AFREE` (it is `AFRET`; I asserted a PSA offset without checking it, and the six literal constants around it would have told me). | fixed |
| **I-163** | The very next card is the same shape: `N R7,=A(X'FFF0')` did **two** jobs on a halfword PTE, and the ESA/390 fullword breaks both | An S/370 PTE was a halfword holding the frame address divided by 256 in bits 0-11 with flags in 12-15, so one mask stripped the flags **and** left page*16 -- which is the `CORTABLE` index, because a `CORTABLE` entry is 16 bytes (`CORFPNT`, `CORBPNT`, `CORSWPNT`, `CORPGPNT`, with `CORFLAG` ORG'd over `CORSWPNT` at +8, confirmed from the DSECT and from `TM X'008'(R7),X'08'` in the disassembly). An ESA/390 PTE holds the real address itself, so the mask leaves `X'F000'`, `A R7,ACORETBL` lands on an unrelated entry, and 00357000-00361000 **write through it**. Measured at the abend: the bogus entry read `CORFREE` off, `BZ CNTFLR` did flush-list accounting for a page that was never flushed, and `DMKPTRUC` went 0-1. `DMKPTRUC` (R10+`X'2CC'`) was 0 and `DMKPTRP2` (R10+`X'2EC'`) was 1, so `CNTFLR` had run exactly once -- `ABEND PTR020`, `DMKPTR.ASSEMBLE:412`. The two jobs separate: `N R7,=A(PAGPFRM)` then `SRL R7,8`, in that order because `SRL` sets no condition code and the following `BZ READPAGE` must still test the `N`. **The class is not confined to `DMKPTR`**: `tools/geomchk.py` finds the identical swap-table expression `16*2+8` unconverted in `DMKCDB` 01063200, `DMKCDM` 00783000 and `DMKPGS` 01015000, and the `X'FFF0'` mask still unconverted at `DMKPTR` 00527000 and `DMKCPU` 00889000. Generated and passing every static check; not yet assembled. | fix known |
| **I-164** | `snapshot.py` cannot validate an incremental build, so the guard that `I-153` added refuses the very snapshots it should bless | After a `spec` build of two modules it demanded a deck `APPLYING` line and a `TXTLCL CREATED` for all 40 patched modules, because `restore()` truncates `build.journal` (`I-160`) and the other 38 modules' TEXT decks came from the restored snapshot rather than from this run. Both the single log and the journal fail the same way. Not worked around -- defeating the guard is what `I-111` and `I-153` exist to prevent -- and not fixed either: `SNAP-DAT2` plus one `spec` invocation reproduces the state deterministically in about five minutes, which is what the guard was protecting. Recorded so the cost is visible rather than rediscovered: the tool needs a notion of "incremental on top of a valid snapshot", checking only the modules named in this run and inheriting the rest from the parent manifest. | open |
| **I-165** | CP is not silent: it writes **one byte** to the console from a buffer still full of DMKFRE's `X'EE'` fill | With CCW tracing armed **before** the IPL -- which `build.sh test` could not express until now -- the whole console conversation is six CCWs: `0009: Halt subchannel`, then `CCW=0406DA80 20000020` (sense, 32 bytes, `Stat=0C00 Count=001F`), then **`CCW=09056360 60000001`** (write, **count 1**) with the buffer at `X'056360'` reading `40 EE EE EE EE ...`, then `CCW=03000000 20000001` (NOP). Every one completes with `Stat=0C00` -- CE+DE, no error. So the channel program is correct, the device works, and the one byte written is `X'40'`, an EBCDIC blank: **that lone blank line in every run's output is CP's message.** `X'EE'` is DMKFRE's `&FRETRAP` fill for freshly allocated storage that has never been written (`DMKFRE.ASSEMBLE:341`, `L R1,=X'EE000000'` / `MVCL`), so the text was never moved in and the length never set. **There is no read CCW anywhere in the trace** -- opcodes `04`, `09` and `03` only -- which separates wall 16's two readings: CP never posts a console read, so the `cold` sent at `0009` was discarded by the 3215 with no error and no trace, exactly as a line typed at a device with no outstanding read must be. Three earlier runs that armed `t+009` *after* the ipl recorded an empty trace, and I read that emptiness as "CP issues no I/O" -- it was not evidence of anything, and the harness could not have produced the evidence. A length that collapses to 1 beside a buffer that is never filled is the signature of a length taken from the wrong place, which is `I-128`'s structural law (S/370 packs a length in the **high** byte of a pointer word, where 24-bit address formation ignores it) and therefore the same class as `I-162` and `I-163`. Next: find where `DMKQCNWT`/`DMKCNS` build that buffer and where the count comes from, by sweeping rather than reading. | open |

| **I-166** | CP builds its startup logo correctly, queues it, and `DMKCNS` writes **one** CONTASK and never advances | Breakpoint at `DMKQCNWT` (`AQCNWT` = `X'419F8'`) is reached with `GR00 = X'47'` (71 bytes) and `GR01 = X'6DD92'`, and that storage reads `151515` + *"VM/370 Community Edition Version 1 Release 1.2 10/02/26 18:27:53"* + `1515` -- `STMSG` in `DMKCPI`, with today's date and time filled in by `DMKSAV`. **The furthest CP has ever been observed to get.** Scanning the nucleus for EBCDIC `VM/3` finds the logo text in **three** places in CP free storage (`X'056480'`, `X'056620'`, and the `DMKCPI` original) -- i.e. the messages were built AND copied into CONTASKs AND queued. The single CCW that reaches the console is `09 056360 60 000001`, one byte of `X'40'` from `X'056360'`, a **different** CONTASK 288 bytes before the first logo one: the leading blank line VM/370 writes ahead of its logo. So the defect is neither the message build nor `DMKQCN`'s trailing-blank stripper (which leaves the count at 71, because the message ends in `X'15'`, not a blank): **`DMKCNS` writes the first CONTASK and never advances to the next.** Everything else fits -- the write completes `Stat=0C00`, `CR6 = FF000000` **measured at the stop** so no subclass is masked, CP takes the completion, finds the queue unadvanced and idles in the dispatcher's enabled wait. Supersedes the reading in `I-165` that the empty write was itself the defect. | open |
| **I-167** | A **one-byte corruption of static nucleus data**: `DMKCPI`'s logo literal reads `VM/380` where every source says `VM/370` | `X'6DD99'` holds `X'F8'` (`'8'`) where the `DC C'VM/370 Community Edition Version '` at `DMKCPI.ASSEMBLE:2461` puts `X'F7'` (`'7'`). Checked, not assumed: all **four** `C'VM/3?0 Community Edition '` literals across CE's `source/` and `maintenance/` trees read `VM/370`, the deck that introduced the line is `HRC373DK` (`DMKBOX`/`DMKCPE`), and none of our own `XA` decks touches it -- the `380` matches in `arch/31bit/updates` are sequence numbers. The three runtime copies in free storage all carry `F8`, which is expected once the original is wrong, so there is one bad byte, not many. `X'6DD92'` is `STMSG` itself in `DMKCPI` (base `X'6C008'`, so `+X'1D8A'`), not a copy, so something **stores one byte into the nucleus**. `F7`->`F8` is not reachable by `OI` (gives `FF`) or `NI` (gives `F0'`), so it is a store of the literal character `'8'` -- and `CPIVER`/`CPIREL`/`CPILEV` are "filled in from HDKCPEID" while `DMKCPICD` and the time field are "FILLED IN BY DMKSAV", which makes a misaddressed digit store the first thing to check. Possibly the same root cause as `I-166`, possibly independent; not yet established either way. **RETRACTED 2 October 21:50 UTC -- see `I-173`. This was never a defect.** It is CE's own `HRC370DK`, whose card reads `MVI STMSG+7,C'8'  tell them this is System/380`. My evidence that it was corruption -- *all four `C'VM/3?0 Community Edition'` literals read VM/370* -- was true but **incomplete**: I searched for a corrupted LITERAL and never for code that MODIFIES one, and the modifying card is an `MVI`, which that search shape could not find. A well-formed search for the wrong hypothesis. | **closed -- retracted** |
| **I-168** | Hercules address-range instruction tracing does not observe execution here, and a control experiment is now mandatory before any trace result is believed | `t+419F8-419FC` over `DMKQCNWT` reported zero hits, which was read as "CP never calls it". The control -- `t+32BC0-32BC4` on `DMKDSPCH`'s entry, which executes thousands of times per IPL -- produced **2** log mentions and 2 instruction-trace lines in the whole run, one of them `pgmtrace`'s `SSM` exception. So the facility is not working with this syntax and the zero was worthless; a breakpoint (`b 419F8`) then proved CP *does* call `DMKQCNWT`. This is the **second** empty result today that could have been mistaken for a finding, after three runs armed `t+009` after `herc:ipl` (`I-165`). The rule earned: an instrument reporting absence is not evidence until it has reported presence somewhere it must. `b ADDR` is validated and is the instrument to use; why `t+ADDR-ADDR` is silent is unresolved. | open |

| **I-169** | **P0 answered: CP never enters `DMKCNSIN`.** The console's completion is never recorded anywhere in CP, so the CONTASK queue never advances | Chain of custody, each link measured: (1) Hercules completes the write on subchannel `X'0004'` (device `0009`) with `Stat=0C00`, CE+DE, no error. (2) The console's IOBLOK is `X'056940'`, identified by **two** independent cross-checks -- `IOBUSER = X'052870'` equals every CONTASK's `CONUSER`, and `IOBCAW = X'056340'` is exactly `CONTASK X'056328' + X'18' = CONCCW1`; eight other `IOBRADD=0009` hits in free storage are alignment coincidences and fail both checks. (3) That IOBLOK's **`IOBCSW` is zero** -- no status recorded. (4) The written CONTASK still carries `CONSTAT = AA`, i.e. **`CONACTV`** still set, "CONTASK IS ACTIVE ON REAL DEVICE". (5) A breakpoint at `DMKCNSIN` is **never hit**, with the instrument control-validated the same session at `X'32BC0'` and `X'419F8'`. So the fix is in **interrupt routing, not queue pointers** -- which is the fork P0 existed to resolve. `DMKCNSIN` is at `X'FE40'`, taken from `IOBIRA` and corroborated by the code there (`LM R12,R13,...` / `LH R1,0(R10)` = `LH R1,IOBRADD`, matching DMKCNS's documented "GPR 10 = ADDRESS OF THE UNSTACKED IOBLOK"). **CP is not deaf to I/O**: the I/O old PSW at `X'38'` is the enabled-wait PSW itself and `IOSCHNO` (`X'BA'`, architecturally the subchannel number in the subsystem-identification word) reads `X'0056'` = device `06A1`, so DASD interrupts are taken *out of the wait* while the console's are not -- and `CR6 = FF000000`, measured at the stop, enables all eight subclasses. **CP's whole startup sequence is queued and intact**: nine CONTASKs chain cleanly from `X'056328'` through the logo (`cnt=67`), `"Now 20:16:20 GMT"` (`cnt=32`), `DMKCPI971I` (`cnt=44`) and `DMKCPI977I` (`cnt=30`), every link valid and every `CONUSER` identical. Next fork: the I/O new PSW at `X'78'` puts CP's first-level I/O handler at `X'6638'`, so a breakpoint there distinguishes "Hercules never presents subchannel `X'0004'`" from "CP's first-level handler drops it before `DMKIOT` routes it". | open |

| **I-170** | CP **clamps itself to `RMSIZE`**, so any machine above 16 MB measures Hercules rather than CP — and the first size sweep was written half-wasted | CE generates `SYSCOR RMSIZE=16384K` (`DMKSYS.ASSEMBLE:19,21`) and `DMKCPI` takes the smaller of the real machine and the SYSGEN size: `L R1,=A(DMKSYSRM)` / `L R15,=A(DMKSYSRV)` / `C R1,0(,R15)` / `BNH *+8` (low or equal, use **actual**) / `L R1,0(,R15)` (high, use **SYSGEN**). So 24 MB and 32 MB both give CP a 16384K storage size, identical geometry and identical segment count -- only unused real storage differs. The sweep was first written `8 16 24 32`; the informative range is **`4 8 12 16`**, below and at the cap, and genuinely testing above 16 MB needs `RMSIZE` regenerated, which is M5. Also answers "is 8 MB supported?": yes -- 8192K < 16384K so CP sizes itself at 8 MB, and VM/370 R6 historically ran in far less. There is no fixed minimum constant: `DMKCPI`'s `00D - REAL MACHINE TOO SMALL FOR VM/370` is reached only via `B NOSTOR` when `DMKLOGOP` fails to build the OPERATOR's virtual machine, so "too small" is a consequence, not a threshold. The sweep's post-IPL pause is also cut 200s->100s: 200 is inherited from the assembly path, where it covers 30-95s of emulated assembly work, and a bare IPL settles well inside 100. Caught by Mike asking whether 8 MB was supported at all and why the sweep was slow -- both before any run time was spent on it. | documented |

| **I-171** | The MAINSIZE sweep isolated the trigger for `I-128`'s savearea pointer: **only at exactly 16 MB**, because `X'1000000'` is 25 bits | Four sizes, same nucleus, same four measurements. `ccw=1474  logo=6  msgs=none  psw=enabled wait ia=0` at **4, 8, 12 and 16 MB** -- identical, so the DAT conversion is not tuned to one machine size. That identity was suspicious enough to need a control, and `DMKSVCHI` (`X'1420'`) supplied one: `003C1000 / 00781000 / 00B41000 / 00F01000`, scaling linearly, so MAINSIZE really did change each run. **The find is `NEXTSAVE` (`X'1418'`):** `003FFA58`, `007FFA58`, `00BFFA58` at 4/8/12 MB -- high byte zero, correct -- and **`FFFFFA58` at 16 MB**. The pool sits 1448 bytes below the top at every size (`X'C00000'-1448 = X'BFFA58'`), so 16 MB should give `00FFFA58`; `FFFFFA58` is **-1448**, i.e. the machine size was taken as **zero**. `X'1000000'` does not fit in 24 bits, so a path that truncates it to 24 bits computes `0 - 1448`. `DMKSVCHI` is computed correctly from the full value at the same size, so there are two paths and only one truncates -- the savearea chunk comes from `CALL DMKFREE` in `DMKCPI` (`:1106`), which makes DMKFRE's upper pool bound the place to look. **This retires the open question from `I-162`**, where `FFFFF9F8` was measured, retracted as "not today's bug", and correctly filed as a latent `I-128` instance with no known trigger. The trigger is the 16 MB boundary itself, and it is invisible in AMODE 24 because address formation discards the high byte -- which is precisely why only a comparison across sizes could find it. In AMODE 31 the same word is `X'7FFFFA58'`, beyond any storage. Mike asked for the sweep; it would not have been found without it. | open |

| **I-172** | `geomchk.py` extended to the shift classes: **15 PROVEN, 139 REVIEW, 83 of the REVIEW rows in modules that touch the DAT structures** | The blind spot was shifts by 4, 6, 16 and 20 (`PAGSHFT`, `PTLSHFT`, `SEGSHFT`), including the `SRDL`/`SLDL` double-shift forms `DMKATS` and `DMKCPP` use. These are **ambiguous by construction** -- a shift of 4 is also how you reach a nibble, 16 is also how you reach a halfword -- so they are REVIEW, never PROVEN, and the tool says so in its own output. Each REVIEW row carries a **candidate** class (swap / cortable / segment / page / key / length / `?`) derived from the CORE.COPY symbols and comment words within four cards, and the header states that a candidate is a reading aid and not a verdict, because applying a plausible classification without reading is exactly how `I-162` was introduced. The candidates immediately earn their keep in both directions: `DMKCFG 01149000 SRL R9,4 "16 PAGES PER..."`, `DMKBLD 00250000 SLL R7,6 "TIMES 64 BYTE..."` and `DMKCPI 00638000 SLL R3,4 "*16"` are real geometry, while `DMKVSI 01111000 SLL R5,16 "MOVE CSW STAT"` and `DMKVSP 00534000 SRL R0,16 "GET CYLINDER"` are a CSW field and a DASD cylinder with no geometry in them at all. The DAT-touching module set is **derived** from `COPY CORE` and the CORE.COPY symbol names rather than hardcoded, because a hardcoded list goes stale silently. Composite shifts are reported separately and must never be collapsed into one EQU; the section is **empty**, which is the filter working -- `DMKBLD`'s `SLL R1,4+4` at 00222700 is already replaced by `XA0034DK` with `SLL R1,4+8`, keeping the `4` that undoes the VMSEG packing and taking the other to 8 for pages-per-segment. That is the decomposition an external review warned would be needed, done independently and correctly before the warning arrived. This is the prerequisite for the central `PAGSHFT`/`SEGSHFT`/`PTLSHFT`/`KEYSHFT` EQUs: named constants do not FIND wrong values, they only make future ones fail at assembly time, so the audit comes first and the EQU pass converts the classified subset. | documented |

| **I-173** | Chasing wall 17 found that **CE already probes for 31-bit mode, and our converted CP passes the probe** | `HRC370DK` (CE ordinal 685, touching `DMKBOX`, `DMKCNS`, `DMKCPE`, `DMKCPI`, `DMKGRF`, `DMKGRT`, `DMKSEP`) carries an architecture probe in `DMKCPI`: *"Since we support both System/380 and System/370 machines, let's see on which we are running"*. It sets `MVI INSTWRD1,C'7'`, saves the program-check new PSW and installs its own trap, then `LA R14,CHK3701` / `ICM R14,8,=X'80'` / `DC X'0B0E'` (**`BSM R0,R14`**). Reaching `CHK3701` means the `BSM` was valid, so it stores `C'8'` into both `INSTWRD1` and `STMSG+7`; a program check instead means System/370. **Our CP reaches `CHK3701`**: breakpoint at `X'6E0C6'` fires with `am=31`, `GR12 = X'6D630'`, `GR14 = 8006E0C2` (bit 0 set), and `INSTWRD1` at PSA `X'430'` reads `F8000000`. So the `VM/380` banner is not corruption -- **it is CE detecting that our conversion produced a 31-bit-capable machine**, which is the most direct confirmation of the AMODE-31 work this project has had. Two things worth keeping: (1) `INSTWRD1` byte 0 is a **CE-maintained PSA flag for "is this a 31-bit machine"**, and `DMKCNS.HRC370DK` already reads it (`MVC EBCLMSG+8(1),INSTWRD1`) to choose its console banner -- existing infrastructure we did not know we had. (2) **A live `I-128` instance in CE's own shipped code**: `INSTWRD1` is `DC F'0' - RESERVED FOR INSTALLATION USE` and two independent updates share it -- `HRC370DK` writes a character into byte 0 while `HRC065DK` (Logical Device Support) does `L R8,INSTWRD1 -> LDEVCTL` in `DMKGRF` and `CLC INSTWRD1+1(3),ZEROES` in `DMKCFP`. The `+1(3)` deliberately skips byte 0, and `HRC370DK`'s own comment is *"Note!!!!! We store an '8' in the first byte of INSTWRD1 in the PSA!!!!"*. So `L R8,INSTWRD1` loads a pointer whose high byte is `X'F8'` -- a flag packed into the high byte of an address word, harmless under 24-bit address formation and wild in AMODE 31. **Latent, not live, here**: `INSTWRD1` bytes 1-3 are zero in this configuration, so LDEVs are inactive. It must be fixed before Logical Device Support can run under AMODE 31. | documented |

| **I-174** | **The ORB's interruption parameter was zero, so every asynchronous I/O interrupt was unroutable.** Necessary, and NOT sufficient for wall 16 | `ORBTMPL DC 1F'0'  INTERRUPTION PARAMETER` in `DMKIOS.XA0004DK`, and the same `DC F'0'` in `XAIOB.MACRO`'s `XAIOBORB`. On ESA/390 the interruption parameter is **whatever the program put in the ORB** -- it is not implicitly the device address, which is the System/370 assumption `DMKIOT.XA0012DK` still carries in all four of its routing comparisons (`MVC 2(2,R14),IOINTPRM+2`, `LH R1,IOINTPRM+2`, `CLC IOBRADD(2),IOINTPRM+2`, `CLC IOINTPRM+2(2),2(R14)`). Zero can match no device, so every interrupt was dropped. Measured before: `IOSCHNO = X'0056'` -- only a real interrupt stores that -- with `IOINTPRM = 00000000` from the same interrupt. **DASD hid it entirely**: its status is normally already pending at `SSCH`, so cc=1 takes the synchronous `IOSXCC1`/`TSCH` path and never needs an interrupt. All 1,474 channel programs of nucleus load and directory read went that way, and the console write -- which completes asynchronously -- was the first thing in five days that actually depended on the interrupt path. Fix: `MVC IOBOPARM+2(2),IOBRADD` after each of the **three** ORB builds (two in `DMKIOS`, one in `XAIOB.MACRO`'s `XAIOBSIO`, which is used by `DMKIOT` and `DMKCNS`). The device address is the right payload rather than the IOBLOK address, because it makes all four existing comparisons work unchanged -- this module's own premise, stated three comments up from the patch: *change the ten instructions, not the 1,917 references*. `IOBOPARM` already existed as a named field (`IOBLOKS.XA0003DK`). **Result, measured: `IOINTPRM` went `00000000` -> `00000009` and `IOSCHNO` went `X'0056'` -> `X'0004'`, so the console's interrupt is now the last one CP takes, where before it never appeared at all.** But the console still writes one blank byte, the written CONTASK is still `CONSTAT=AA` with `CONACTV` set, and the queue -- now visibly ten CONTASKs chained from `X'056340'` and ending properly at `CONPNT=0` -- does not advance. **So wall 16 is two defects in series and this clears the first.** I predicted this change would make the messages appear and said so with more confidence than the evidence supported: "one proximate cause" was true of the symptom and not of the chain. The generator's column guard (R-04) rejected the first version of the card at 66 columns against a 61 limit, and failed safe -- the deck kept its old cards rather than emitting a truncated one. **Confirmed against the ESA/390 Principles of Operation, which is a stronger footing than the argument from `DMKIOT`'s comparisons that found it.** On the ORB: *"Bits 0-31 of word 0 are preserved unmodified in the subchannel until replaced by a subsequent START SUBCHANNEL or MODIFY SUBCHANNEL instruction. These bits are placed in word 1 of the interruption code when an I/O interruption occurs."* On the PMCW: *"The interruption parameter **can be set to any value** by START SUBCHANNEL and MODIFY SUBCHANNEL. **The initial value of the interruption parameter is zero.**"* On assigned storage 188-191 (`X'BC'`-`X'BF'`): *"During an I/O interruption, the interruption parameter **from the associated subchannel** is stored at locations 188-191."* So `ORBTMPL DC F'0'` was not even the cause -- it merely agreed with the architectural default. The parameter is program-chosen and arbitrary, it comes from the SUBCHANNEL rather than the device, and **nothing** puts a device address there unless the program asks. That makes this the intended mechanism rather than a workaround, and it vindicates the choice of payload: "any value" means the device address is as legitimate as any other, and it is the one that leaves CP's 1,917 CSW/CAW references alone. | fixed |
| **I-175** | **The I/O interrupt path read a CSW that ESA/390 never stores -- wall 16's second defect.** | On System/370 the I/O interruption *itself* stored a CSW at `X'40'`; the channel subsystem instead keeps the status in the subchannel and makes `TEST SUBCHANNEL` the only way to fetch it. We built that shim for the `SSCH` path (`IOSXCC1`: `TSCH IOBIRB`, then synthesise the CSW at `X'40'`) and never for the **interrupt** path. `DMKIOT`'s interruption supervisor reads `CSW` at about forty sites (`TM CSW+5,CDC+CCC+IFCC`, `CLI CSW+4,CUE`, `MVC IOBCSW(4),CSW`, ...) and `DMKIOT.XA0012DK` converted **none** of them. Measured in the nucleus: `CSW X'40' = 0000000000000000` and `IOBCSW = 00000000 00000000` while the interrupt is demonstrably taken (`IOSSID X'B8' = 00010004`, the console's subchannel). Every status test reads zero, so no unit status is ever seen, the console IOBLOK is never completed, `CONSTAT` keeps `CONACTV`, and `DMKCNSIN` never fires. Fix: at `00288000`, before `LH R1,IOINTPRM+2`, `L R1,IOSSID` / `TSCH IOTXIRB` / `MVI CSW,X'00'` / `MVC CSW+1(3),IOTXIRB+5` / `MVC CSW+4(4),IOTXIRB+8`. Three things make it cheap. The SID the hardware stored at `X'B8'` is **already** in the form `TSCH` wants (`X'0001' || subchannel`), so no scan is needed. R1 is provably free because the next card loads it, and `TSCH`'s condition code is discardable because `LH` sets none and `DMKSCNRU` sets its own before `00290000` tests it. And the copy is **exact, not approximate**: the SCSW's device-status and subchannel-status bytes carry the same eight bits in the same order as the CSW's unit-status and channel-status bytes, so all forty downstream tests need no translation. Byte 0 goes to zero -- key, SLI and CC have no ESA/390 counterpart -- which makes the logout-pending test at `00522000` permanently false, the one behaviour change. The work IRB (`IOTXIRB DS 16F`) is **static**, unlike every other XAIOB area, and has to be: `DMKSCNRU` has not run yet, so there is no IOBLOK to put it in. That is safe only because the I/O new PSW is `000C0000` -- I/O masked off -- so no second I/O interrupt can be taken before the sequence finishes; the reasoning is in the deck because a static area in a module whose prologue declares REENTRANT otherwise reads as a bug. **VERIFIED by measurement, 3 October 08:58 UTC.** `CSW X'40'` went `0000000000000000` -> `000542B800000000`, and our shim is the only thing that writes `X'40'` under ESA/390. The console CONTASK's `CONSTAT` went `AA` -> `82`: **`CONACTV` is clear**, where before it was stuck set, which is DMKCNS completing an I/O. `CONCNT` went `0001` -- the single blank byte that was the whole of wall 16's visible output -- to `002C` and `001E`, real message lengths, on a proper chain `X'567D8' -> X'56868' -> X'568E0'` carrying `DMKCPI971I System is Uniprocessor generated` and `DMKCPI977I Free Trap Installed`. And `IOINTPRM` now reads `000006A1` with `IOSSID = 00010056`, the DASD, so I-174 works on the paging path too. **The messages still do not reach the terminal**, because CP now runs on and abends in DMKPTR (`I-177`) before the queue drains -- so this closes wall 16 as a HANG and is not yet a working console. Stated that way deliberately: on I-174 I predicted the messages would appear and said so with more confidence than the evidence supported. | fixed |
| **I-176** | **Our source-reading tools scan raw `.ASSEMBLE` and so can report cards the build never sees.** | Chasing I-175 I spent a sequence of steps on `DMKIOS`'s I/O interruption supervisor -- `DMKIOSIN` at `DMKIOS.ASSEMBLE:436`, with eight surviving `INTTIO` references and no deck of ours touching sequence `00400000`-`00699999`. All of it was true of the file and false of the build: `DMKIOS.R09587DK` contains `./ * DMKIOSIN BEING MOVED INTO DMKIOT` and `./ D 418000 890000`, so those 473 cards are **deleted by an APAR before our decks apply**. The real handler is `DMKIOTIN`, which `XA0012DK` did convert. Two things should have caught it sooner and did not: `DMKIOS` assembling clean with eight references to a symbol our PSA deck renames away is a contradiction that was visible in the log, and `abendmap` placed the handler in `DMKIOS` from an 8 KB bracket it cannot resolve -- the same sparse-coverage caveat already recorded against it. What settled it was decoding the nucleus: `LH R1,X'BE'` at `X'6708'` with `IOOPSW+4 = X'3C'` and `CSW = X'40'` as independent anchors in the same macro expansion. **`geomchk`, `replchk`, `s370only` and `coverage` all read `SRC/*.ASSEMBLE` directly**, without applying the ~40-deck APAR chain, so every count they report may include deleted cards. **Fixed by `tools/applied.py`**, which applies the chain and returns the cards the assembler actually sees. `chain()` is validated against a build log rather than assumed -- an AUX file lists its decks newest-first and they are applied oldest-first, so each file's entries reverse, and the thirty names it returns for `DMKIOS` match `h1.log`'s thirty `APPLYING` lines in order. `assembled()` then applies conditional assembly over the merged cards, with the globals read rather than assumed: `&AP` is 0 only because `HRC035DK`'s `&AP SETB 1` sits behind `AIF (&MP EQ 0).O1` with `&MP` 0. It self-validates on the fact that started this: `DMKIOS` has **zero** assembled references to `INTTIO`, which is exactly why it compiles clean against a PSA that renames the symbol away, and `DMKIOT` has **ten** to `IOINTPRM` -- the same ten I had measured independently as `X'BE'` references in the nucleus core. Two methods, one answer. `geomchk` and `s370only` now scan the effective source. The base file was wrong in **both** directions, which is the part I had not expected: it showed phantoms (geomchk 2 PROVEN and 17 REVIEW in cards the build never assembles -- one in `DMKPTR` inside an `&AP` block, where the patch would have had no effect and the debugging would have been baffling; s370only's channel-family count fell from 133 to 32 and its key family from 67 to 58, because `DMKIOS`'s deleted interruption supervisor is full of SIO/TIO/HIO) and it **hid** real work on the APAR decks' own cards -- 36 S/370-only instructions tree-wide, and 7 geomchk REVIEW sites including **two `PTLSHFT-6` sites in `DMKVAT`**, the DAT module, where 6 -> 10 is a real conversion and no base-file scan could ever have seen them. Scanning the effective source also needs one guard I got wrong first time round and caught by reading a flagged site: 19 of the newly-visible rows were **our own conversions**, because `covered()` holds the sequence numbers a deck REPLACES and an inserted card gets a new one -- `./ R 01054000` writes card `01054100`. `SRDL R14,20` beside `SLL R14,2` and `SEGSTOM` is 1 MB segments and a fullword STE, not a site to fix. Cards carrying an `XAnnnnDK` identifier are now skipped, and the arithmetic closes: 139 - 17 phantoms + 7 genuine = 129 REVIEW, 15 - 2 = 13 PROVEN. A by-product invariant worth keeping: **zero** of our own cards carry an S/370-only instruction. So the planning numbers this project has been quoting were wrong in the flattering direction for the channel work and the unflattering one for the geometry work. The rule earned is the I-168 rule in a new costume: an instrument reporting a site is not evidence the site is in the program -- and one reporting none is not evidence there is no work. | fixed |
| **I-177** | **Wall 18: storage keys. CP now abends `PRG001` on `SSK` in DMKPTR, and the conversion is structural rather than an opcode swap.** | With `I-175` in, CP runs past the console hang into DMKPTR's page-in path and takes an operation exception at `X'3DD78'` on `SSK 3,8`. `abendmap` brackets it between `DMKPTR ABEND 20` at -508 and `DMKPTR ABEND 8` at +1422 -- same module both sides -- and `GR12=0003DA48` is DMKPTR's base. The registers identify the card exactly: `GR06=00EC4000` and `GR08=00EC4800`, 2 KB apart, which is `DMKPTR 00643000`. The structure is the point. System/370 storage keys cover **2 KB**, so a 4 KB page takes TWO `SSK`s and two key bytes packed in a halfword: `N R3,=A(X'F8F8')  CLEAR REF/CHANGE BITS` / `SSK R3,R8  SET KEY FOR 2'ND HALF PAGE` / `SRL R3,8  JUSTIFY KEY FOR 1'ST HALF PAGE` / `SSK R3,R6  SET KEY FOR 1'ST HALF PAGE`. The ESA/390 PoO is explicit: *"The 370-XA protection facilities include key-controlled protection on **only 4K-byte blocks**"*, contrasted in the same sentence with System/370. So `SSKE` (B22B) sets one key for the whole page, the pair collapses to one instruction, the `SRL R3,8` between them disappears, and `SWPFLAG`'s two key bytes become one -- the I-128 structural law again, where S/370 packs something in a place ESA/390 does not. `ISKE` (B229) and `RRBE` (B22A) are the counterparts for `ISK` and `RRB`, all three RRE and privileged. Inventory against the EFFECTIVE source (I-176): **59 real instructions -- 15 `SSK`, 32 `ISK`, 12 `RRB` -- in 15 modules**, DMKPTR holding 19. The `SSK`s are mostly ADJACENT PAIRS (DMKCCW 976000/979000 and 3586000/3589000, DMKCDS 884000/888000, DMKDGD 520000/523000, DMKPTR 643000/645000 and 939000/940000), so six pairs collapse to six `SSKE`s; DMKMCH's two are 32 cards apart and do different jobs (exercise, then restore) and DMKPRV's is single. The precedent is already set and worked: `DMKCPI.XA0013DK` turned that module's `SSK` into `SSKE R2,R5` with the same registers under `I-104`. **This confirms the prior analysis rather than correcting it.** `I-44` and [23-STORAGE-KEYS.md](23-STORAGE-KEYS.md) already said "a third of the family is in `DMKPTR`, the page manager" and that "the pairs must collapse rather than translate" -- both now demonstrated by a live abend on exactly such a pair. I first wrote this entry claiming a standing scope claim was wrong, on the strength of a remembered parenthetical that the key sites were "none in the I/O or paging supervisor". That phrase appears nowhere in the docs; the nearest real claim is about the CHANNEL sites, and the storage-key docs say the opposite of what I was about to correct. **Retracted before it was acted on**, and the lesson is the plain one: check the doc, not the memory of it -- which is `I-176` over again, one level up. The one genuine refinement is the count: those docs say 67 sites and the effective source has 59, the difference being cards the build never assembles.
