What is newly measured here is which modules have NONE -- DMKIOS, DMKIOT, DMKPGS and DMKVAT are all zero, so the I/O and DAT-table modules are clear and the exposure is DMKPTR plus the command and simulation modules. **VERIFIED, 3 October 09:29 UTC**: with the two DMKPTR pairs collapsed to `SSKE`, CP ran straight past the abend and printed its whole start-up banner for the first time — `VM/380 Community Edition Version 1 Release 1.2`, the date and time, `DMKCPI971I`, `DMKCPI977I` and `DMKUDR476I System Directory loaded from volume VM50-1`. So this also closes wall 16 for real: the console messages reach the terminal, which `I-175` alone did not achieve. The remaining 57 key sites are unconverted and have not been reached. | fixed |
| **I-178** | **Wall 19: CP will not start without the interval timer, and 370-XA deleted it.** | With `I-177` in, CP prints its full start-up banner -- `VM/380 Community Edition Version 1 Release 1.2`, the time, `DMKCPI971I`, `DMKCPI977I` and `DMKUDR476I System Directory loaded from volume VM50-1` -- and then writes `Turn on the Interval Timer` **181,775 times** in one run. `DMKCPI`'s `TIMETEST` (02785000) reads location `X'50'`, polls it 40,000 times for a change, warns, and branches back to poll again. The value cannot change because the timer does not exist. **The authority is the same PoO table that justified `I-174` and `I-175`**, Appendix F, "Comparison between System/370 and 370-XA", which lists the assigned-storage locations that differ -- and three of this project's defects are one row each: channel-status word 64 (`X'40'`) -> gone (`I-175`); channel-address word 72 (`X'48'`) -> gone (still open, flagged in `DMKLD00E` by `s370only`); **interval timer 80 (`X'50'`) -> gone (this)**; subsystem ID -> 184 (`X'B8'`) and I/O-interruption parameter -> 188 (`X'BC'`), both `I-174`. CR0 bit 24, the interval-timer subclass mask, goes with it. That table should have been a checklist from the start; it was read for one row at a time instead, which is why each of these arrived as a surprise. Fix: replace the `TIMETEST EQU *` card with `DS 0H` + `B TIMERON`, so the poll is never entered. Only that one card changes -- `TLOOP1`, `TLOOP` and `NOMPMSG` stay defined because cards below still branch to them, and `B TIMETEST` at 02700000 is the only branch in. **This is not "timing is gone"**: the CPU timer and clock comparator are ESA/390 facilities, `STPT`/`SPT` are already used in CP's dispatcher, and only the location-`X'50'` timer is affected. CP reads it elsewhere -- `DMKSCH` and `DMKDSP` most of all -- so the accounting and time-slicing built on it are a separate and larger piece of work that clearing this gate does not touch. | fix built, unverified |
| **I-179** | **The system announced itself as VM/380. It is VM/370plus.** | CP's first successful start-up printed `VM/380 Community Edition Version 1 Release 1.2`, and Mike corrected it immediately: **we decided not to follow the /380 path**, this is a proper 31-bit conversion on the way to 64-bit, so the name is **VM/370plus**. The `8` was not ours. CE's `HRC370DK` probes for System/380 -- a program-check trap around a `BSM` -- and on success zaps a `C'8'` over the `C'7'` of "VM/370" in three separate places: `MVI STMSG+7,C'8'` in DMKCPI (the console banner), `MVC EBCLMSG+8(1),INSTWRD1` in DMKCNS (the "VM/370 Online" message), and two `MVC`s into 3270 logo screen data in DMKGRF. This is also the retraction of wall 17 reaching production: the one-byte difference I once diagnosed as nucleus corruption is CE deliberately renaming itself, and it worked exactly as CE intended. Fix: the two literals become `VM/370plus` (`DMKCPI.XA0013DK`, `DMKCNS.XA0021DK`) and all three zaps are removed; DMKGRF gets a new deck, `XA0039DK`. The probe itself is **left alone** -- it records a true fact about the machine in `INSTWRD1` byte 0 -- but nothing may paint that byte over a banner. Safe because byte 0 is display-only: bytes 1-3 are the LDEVCTL pointer that DMKCFP, DMKGRF and HDKD7C use, and `MVI` writes only byte 0, so removing the three readers changes no behaviour. Both literals grow by one byte, which is free because `STMSGL` and `EBCLMSGL` are computed as `*-label` and the only fixed-offset readers were the zaps being removed. DMKGRF's two sites write into screen data at displacements rather than a named literal, so they are only removed -- the 3270 logo keeps its own text, and putting the `+` there needs the FMT77 screen layout. That is deliberately deferred: the point is that nothing says /380 any more, not that everything yet says /370+. The generator's sequence guard earned its keep again, refusing `inc=10` at 02287560 because the next surviving record is only twenty away, and failing safe rather than emitting a deck that would overrun it. | fixed |
| **I-180** | **`build.sh` could not see Hercules 4.9.1 at all, which faked an engine disagreement and silently disabled the I-63 guard.** | 4.9.1 renames its main thread, so the process `comm` is `impl_thread` and `pgrep -c hercules` -- which matches `comm` -- returns **0 while 4.9.1 is running normally**. `pgrep -f`, which matches the command line, finds it. Blind to engine 4, the harness did three wrong things at once: `w()` returned immediately; `run()` archived each log MID-RUN, so every engine-4 log was truncated at whatever CP had printed by then; and `dual` read those truncated logs, counted **0 CP messages**, and printed *"the engines DISAGREE, and that is the interesting outcome"*. They do not disagree -- 4.9.1's own log contains the whole banner, `VM/370plus Community Edition Version 1 Release 1.2`, all four start-up messages and the Start prompt, reached in **16 seconds** against 3.13's ~200. `dualdiff.py` also cannot parse 4.9.1's console format, which prefixes each line with `/`. **The serious part is the safety guard**: the same `pgrep` is what stops a second Hercules starting on the same pack, which is `I-63` -- a run that once IPLed and wrote concurrently with another. With engine 4 that protection was simply off. Fixed with a `herc_running()` helper using `pgrep -f '[h]ercules -f'`, validated against a live 4.9.1 process: it reports 1 where `pgrep -c hercules` reports 0. The lesson is I-168's again, in its sharpest form yet -- an instrument reporting absence is not evidence of absence, and here the instrument's silence was read as a finding about the two emulators. | fixed |
| **I-181** | **Multiprocessor support: scope item, and the `&AP` blocks hold unconverted code.** | Mike asked for this on the to-do list. Today's system is uniprocessor in **three** places that must agree, and do: the Hercules conf says `NUMCPU 1`; CP's assembly options say `&MP 0` and `&AP 0` (CE's own `&AP SETB 1` in `OPTIONS.COPY` sits behind `AIF (&MP EQ 0).O1`, so it never takes effect); CP's SYSGEN has both `SYSCOR RMSIZE=16384K,AP=YES` and `AP=NO` under `AIF (NOT &AP).E1`, and the **AP=NO** card is the one assembled; and the running system says `DMKCPI971I System is Uniprocessor generated`. So enabling MP means changing all three, not one. **The hazard is that the `&AP` blocks have never been compiled, let alone converted.** `DMKPTR 00527000` -- `N R14,=A(X'FFF0')`, a 24-bit page-frame mask of exactly the class that cost five days in `I-162` -- is inside an `&AP` block, which is why `geomchk` reported it as a phantom once the effective source was scanned (`I-176`). DMKIOS's AP paths are ~470 cards in the same state. Turning AP on would therefore reintroduce 24-bit geometry into the paging module and compile hundreds of cards of unconverted I/O code at once. Ordering: MP belongs AFTER the single-processor system runs CMS, and its first task is to run `geomchk` and `s370only` with `&AP` forced to 1 so the `&AP`-only sites are counted before any of it is enabled. | open |
| **I-182** | **Wall 20: CP loops in DMKPGS's segment-release scan after start-up, which is why the console is never read again.** | After `MONITOR AUTO STARTING` CP never returns to the dispatcher. Four PSW samples: `ia=3DA38`, then `ia=C58` three times, with `PSW=` addresses `CD0`, `F4E`, `CAA`, `3DA46` -- and `savecore rejected: CPU not stopped`, so it is RUNNING, not hung. The decoded `ia=` and hex `PSW=` lines disagree within each sample because they are two reads of a live CPU, so an earlier reading of "stuck disabled at X'F38'" was a sampling artefact and is **retracted**; the I/O old PSW at `X'38'` is `020C0000 000338CE`, `sm=02`, **I/O mask set**, so interrupts are being taken. Low core `C58`/`CAA`/`CD0`/`F38`/`F4E` is all DMKPSA's SVC machinery -- `X'C58'` the first-level handler (`OI CPSTATUS,CPSUPER`, `STM R12,R15`, trace, branch table on the SVC code) and `X'F38'` its savearea chain. The SVC old PSW at `X'20'` is `000C2000 00063906` and `X'88'` is `00020008` = `SVC 8`; the bytes at `X'638F0'` decode to `LCTL C1,C1,16(R11)` / `LRA R7,0(R1)` / `BZ` / `LA R2,64` / `L R15,X'4F8'` (APTRAN) / `SVC 8` -- a `CALL DMKPTRAN,PARM=DEFER`, since DEFER is X'40'. **That sequence appears in no `.ASSEMBLE` card**: it is the `TRANS` macro expanding, which `applied.py` cannot see and says so ("macro expansion is not modelled at all"). Identified by byte-matching the epilogue instead -- `LR R2,R4` / `BC 2,NEXTSEG` / `TM X'00C'(R13),X'08'` (`SAVEWRK1,CALLPO`) / `BNO` / `OI X'00F'(R13),X'80'` (`SAVEWRK1+3,REPEAT`) -- which pins it to **`DMKPGS 00962000`, `TRANS 7,1,OPT=(DEFER) ENQUEUE ON SEGMENT`**, with `GR12 = 00063000` DMKPGS's base and the site at offset `X'8F0'`. The scan reaches it only when byte 3 of the STE is not `X'20'` (so the page table was built) **and** the invalid bit is set -- "built but invalid, migrating" -- and the `TRANS` exists to clear that bit and wait. It never clears, `REPEAT` is raised, and the scan goes round. Everything downstream follows: no dispatcher, so the one console read is never re-armed, so `HHCCP066I DEV0009: attention` is raised and never serviced, and a terminal on another device gets nothing at all. **One more retraction**: I then blamed the `CLI SEGPTO+3,SEGINVAL` at 00956150 -- the only `CLI` among thirteen `TM`s against that byte tree-wide -- as an unconverted I-162-class site. It is ours and it is deliberate (`I-159`): the original is an equality test separating "never built" from "built but invalid", a `TM` would make the `TRANS` dead code, and `SEGINVAL EQU X'20'` is exactly the constant the comment says it should be. Checking the EQU, not the mnemonic, is what settled it. Next: whether DMKPTRAN clears the segment-invalid bit at all under the new STE format. | open |
| **I-183** | **Wall 20 found: DMKPGS's segment scan advanced its STE pointer by a megabyte and its virtual address by 64 KB.** | At `NEXTSEG`: `LA R3,SEGPTO+4` -- **our** card, one fullword ESA/390 STE, one 1 MB segment -- followed by two **unconverted** originals, `N R1,=X'00FF0000'  SAVE SEGMENT NUMBER` and `A R1,=X'00010000'  BUMP SEGMENT BY 1`. Those are the 64 KB segment-number mask (bits 8-15) and a 64 KB bump, so R3 and R1 drift by a factor of sixteen from the first iteration. **This deck had already made exactly this conversion five cards up**, at 01049300, with the reasoning spelled out in its own comment: *"X'00FF0000' KEPT THE 64 KB SEGMENT NUMBER, BITS 8-15. A 1 MB SEGMENT NUMBER IS BITS 8-11"* -- and then the deck jumps from 01049000 to 01053000, skipping the pair. Converting one of a group and not the rest is `I-162` precisely, in the same module, by the same deck. **Measured, not inferred**: a breakpoint on the `LRA` inside the `TRANS` at 00962000 (`X'638F4'`) stopped with `GR03 = 00FFD400` -- entry **256** of a segment table that `CR1 = 00FFD001` says holds **32** entries (STO X'FFD000', STL 1) -- while `GR01 = 0`, so DMKPGS was testing segment 256's invalid bit and asking DMKPTRAN to translate **segment 0**. DMKPTRAN duly cleared segment 0 (`NI 3(R7),255-SEGINVAL` at 00748000, which works), the test at 00958100 never cleared, and both exits from the `TRANS` branch back to `PGOUT2`, so it looped for ever. The `LRA`'s `cc=3` is the length violation entry 256 earns. Everything downstream was a consequence: CP never returned to the dispatcher, so the single console read was never re-armed, so `HHCCP066I DEV0009: attention` was raised and never serviced, and a terminal on another device received nothing at all -- which is why this was mistaken for a console defect for several hours. Fix: both cards to 1 MB granularity, matching 01049300. **And the class, swept**: surviving `X'00FF0000'`/`X'00010000'` constants tree-wide are mostly DASD cylinder arithmetic and correctly unchanged, but four are segment constants still at 64 KB and need reading -- `DMKATS 00863000 FORCEPG0 DC X'00FF0000' MASK FOR SEGMENT`, `DMKVAT 01018000 DC X'00FF0000'` (the DAT module, adjacent to the CODE80/CODEA0 page masks geomchk already flags), and `BUMPLOW DC X'00010000'` in both `DMKATS 00857000` and `DMKVMA 00515000`. `DMKPGS 01281000 UPDTACT DC X'00010000'` also wants a look. | fixed |
| **I-184** | **The six cards I-183's fix exposes: DMKPGS's and DMKATS's page/segment arithmetic inside the very loops that were spinning.** | Fixing `I-183` lets `PGOUT2` advance, and the cards it then reaches were never converted. **DMKPGS**, three cards in `PGOUT2`'s own body: `01011000 ALR R14,R14  PAGE NO. * 2 -> PTE INDEX` doubles a page number for a **halfword** S/370 PTE and must be `SLL R14,2` for a fullword one; `01013000 SLL R14,2  PAGE NO. * 8` then takes page*2 to page*8 and must drop to `SLL R14,1` now that the card above leaves page*4 -- an `SWPTABLE` entry is still eight bytes, so only the division of labour between the two shifts moves; and `01015000 LA R5,16*2+8(R14,R15)` writes out sixteen halfword PTEs plus an eight-byte swap-table header as a literal, where `CORE.XA0033DK` had already moved `PAGTSWP` to 256 fullword entries -- replaced by `PAGTSWP-(PAGPFRA-PAGSTMP)+8`, the same place `DMKATS 00341000` spells `PAGTSWP+8` from the other base. **DMKATS**, four cards in the near-duplicate `NXTRANGE`/`REBRANGE` loops: `00289000` and `00335000` both `SRL R2,16  SEGMENT NO. ONLY`, where the three cards immediately below each of them -- `L R6,VMSEG` / `N R6,=A(SEGSTOM)` / `ALR R2,R6` -- are **ours**, so the group was converted and its first member was not, `I-162` for the third time this session; `00298000 N R1,F15  PAGE NO. WITHIN SEGMENT`, which is literally the same card as `DMKPGS 01010000` that this project already reads as `F255`; and the constant pair `00863000 FORCEPG0 DC X'00FF0000'` / `00864000 FORCEPGF DC X'0000F000'`, the segment-number mask and the last-page-of-segment mask, used together at `00330000`/`00331000` to bracket a range to whole segments and again at `00343000` to extract a page number -- `X'00F00000'` and `X'000FF000'`. The `SRL 9` that follows `00343000` **stays**: it relates a page's address weight of 4096 to an 8-byte swap-table entry and neither of those moved. Note what retired the other candidates `I-183` listed: `BUMPLOW` and `UPDTACT` are not address constants at all -- `CORE.COPY 00118200` shows `PAGACT` and `PAGTOT` as a halfword pair, so `X'00010000'` increments the first and `X'00010001'` both, and `DMKATS 00285000` reuses `BUMPLOW` as a **cylinder** bump; and `DMKVAT 01018000` is one row of the per-`CR0`-code **guest** geometry table (`CODE70`/`CODE80`/`CODE90`), which must stay S/370 for as long as CP runs S/370 guests. **A near-miss worth recording**: I read `PAGTSWP EQU (PAGCORE-PAGSTMP+16*L'PAGCORE)` straight out of the base `CORE.COPY` and briefly believed the central geometry EQU was unconverted and that a full rebuild was owed. `CORE.XA0033DK` had already replaced it -- 256 entries, `PAGCORE` renamed `PAGPFRA` with no alias. That is `I-168` and `applied.py`'s whole reason for existing, one layer down: I had built a tool so that scanners read the source the build sees, and then grepped a **copy file** by hand. Copy files need the same treatment as modules. | fixed |
| **I-185** | **`DEF STOR 32M` is capped by one card -- `DMKBLD 00611200 N R9,F15` -- and the ceiling it restores is 256 MB, not 2 GB.** | `DMKBLDRT`'s documented entry conditions settle the layout, and they were sitting in the module all along: *"GPR 1 = BEGINING AND ENDING ADDRESS TO BUILD TABLES. BYTES 0-1 = BEGINING ADDRESS. FIRST 4 BITS = 0, NEXT 8 BITS = SEGMENT, NEXT 4 = PAGE. BYTES 2-3 = ENDING ADDRESS"* (`00055000`-`00059000`). Every caller confirms it -- `DMKCPI 01461000`, `DMKLOG 00933000`, `DMKCFP 00409000` all do `SRL R1,12` then `BCTR R1,R0` and call -- so R1 is **two 16-bit page numbers**, start in bits 0-15 and end in bits 16-31, and the "4 bits zero, 8 segment, 4 page" in the comment is just how a 12-bit page number decomposes **under S/370 geometry**. Under ESA/390 the same page number decomposes as 4 segment bits and 8 page bits, which is exactly what the four `CHKSEG` cards do: `00605400 SRL R8,24` takes the start page number's top byte, `00607200 N R15,=A(X'FF0000')` tests its low byte as page-within-segment, and `00610700 SRL R9,8` takes the end page number's top byte. **So all four of our cards are coherent and I was wrong to call them incoherent** -- the only thing missing was the comments that would have shown it, and absent comments are not evidence of a defect. The real defect is an asymmetry between two of them. `SRL R8,24` leaves the start segment number with **no mask at all**, which is already fully general: `R1>>24` is the top eight bits of a 16-bit page number, an 8-bit segment number, good to 256 MB. `SRL R9,8` then `N R9,F15` keeps **four** bits, so the highest end segment nameable is 15 and the ceiling is 16 MB. The mask should be `F255`, giving symmetry with R8. The failure mode is silent: `SR R9,R8` at `00612000` goes negative when the real end segment exceeds 15, and `BM RETBLDRL  RETURN IF PARTIAL SEGMENT` returns having built nothing. It is invisible on a 16 MB machine, which is every machine this project has tested, and the asymmetry is why it escaped -- one of the pair carries no mask to compare against. Past 256 MB the interface itself is the limit: two 16-bit page numbers in one register cannot express a 31-bit address, so that is a parameter-convention change across eight callers, which is M5. **Two more clusters are on the same `DEF STOR` path and are genuinely unconverted.** `DMKCFG` `PAGRELCK`: `00651200 SRL R1,4  FORCE LOGICAL PAGE ZERO` and `00651400 SLL R1,16  FORM FULL PAGE ADDRESS` are a 64 KB page-number-to-segment-and-back pair needing 8 and 20, and `00652500 O R2,F15  MAKE IT A COMPLETE SEGMENT` rounds a page number up to a segment boundary and needs `F255`. `DMKPGS 00328000`: `SLL R1,2  TIMES 16 PAGES FOR EACH SEGMENT ENTRY` turns a byte difference between two STE addresses into a page count -- bytes/4 segments times 16 pages is a net times 4, which is why the shift reads 2 and not 4, and times 256 pages makes it `SLL R1,6` -- and `00331000`-`00334000` read the page-table length from **byte 0** of the STE as a raw page count, where ESA/390 keeps `PTL` in the low nibble of byte 3 in units of 16 entries; `DMKATS 00197000` already converts exactly that read, so the idiom to copy is in the tree. Also owed: the two interface comment cards at `00057000`/`00059000` still describe the S/370 decomposition and now misdescribe the code below them. All held out of the `I-184` build deliberately -- none of it is on the IPL path, all of it is on the `DEF STOR` path, and a wrong guess in `DMKPGS` would spoil the one test that matters next. | open, characterised |
| **I-186** | **`DMKCDB` and `DMKCDM`'s `GETKEY` are still S/370 below the cards we converted.** | Both modules' `GETKEY` -- the routine behind `DISPLAY` and `DUMP` -- had its segment-table addressing converted (`L R3,VMSEG` / `N R3,=A(SEGSTOM)` / `SRDL R14,20`, all `XA0036DK`) and then nothing below it. `SLDL R14,4  GET PAGE NUMBER` takes four bits from the top of R15, which after a 20-bit `SRDL` is bits 12-15 of the address: the top half of an 8-bit page number, so it must be `SLDL R14,8`. This is the sharpest form of `I-162` yet -- **our own card** made the neighbour wrong, because the pair is a single 64-bit shift split across two instructions and we changed one shift count without the other. Below that: `LA R2,16*2+8(,R3)` is `I-184`'s literal again; `LA R3,0(R14,R3)` indexes a halfword PTE; `TM 1(R3),X'08'` tests `PAGINVAL` where `CORE.XA0033DK` put it at `PAGPFRA+3`; `LH R14,0(,R3)` loads a halfword PTE and `SRL R14,4` / `SLDL R14,12` unpack a 2 KB-granular frame address that `PAGPFRM` now gives directly; the `SLDL R14,1` / `SRDL R14,1` pair selects between two 2 KB storage keys per page, which `I-177` reduced to one; and `ISK R2,R14` does not exist in ESA/390 at all -- it is `ISKE`. The two routines are near-identical, `DMKCDB` differing only by the `@VA09057` swap-table bounds check. Neither runs at IPL, so this is queued behind `I-184` and `I-185`, but it means `DISPLAY` would misreport keys and addresses the moment the console accepts commands. **The class is closed by a sweep that found nothing else**: every `SRDL`/`SLDL`/`SRDA`/`SLDA` in every module was paired with every other double or single shift within 22 cards, and the only clusters mixing a converted count (20 or 8) with an unconverted one (16 or 4) are these two. The sweep's silence is worth something here precisely because it reported presence where it must -- `I-168`'s rule -- naming `DMKCDB 01054100/01067000` and `DMKCDM 00768100/00785000` before being believed about anywhere else. **Traced through on 3 October, and `DMKCDM` is fully determined.** The register chain holds up: after the `SRDL R14,20` that is ours, `SR R14,R14` and `SLDL R14,4` take the top four bits of R15, which are address bits 12-15 -- the top half of an 8-bit page number -- so that becomes `SLDL R14,8`, **and nothing else in the half-page selection moves**, because shifting the pair eight instead of four leaves address bit 20 exactly where the following `SLL R14,2` / `SLDL R14,1` expect it: as the half-page flag. So `IC R1,2(R14,R2)` still picks `SWPKEY1` or `SWPKEY2` correctly, which is right -- that is CP's software record of the guest's per-2 KB key, which `R-12` keeps. Then `SRDL R14,1` leaves page*4 and the `SRL R14,1` after it, which made page*2 for a halfword PTE, is deleted; `L R3,0(,R3)` needs `N R3,=A(SEGPTOM)` after it; `LA R2,16*2+8(,R3)` becomes `PAGTSWP-(PAGPFRA-PAGSTMP)+8`, which is 1032 and reduces to the original's 40 under S/370 geometry; `TM 1(R3),X'08'` becomes `TM 2(R3),PAGINV`; and `LH R14,0(,R3)` / `SRL R14,4` / `SLDL R14,12` / `ISK R2,R14` become `L R14,0(,R3)` / `N R14,=A(PAGPFRM)` / `ISKE R2,R14` -- R14 is not read again after the key is inserted, which was checked rather than assumed. **`DMKCDB` is the same except for one block I do not yet understand**, the `@VA09057` bounds check at `01068200`-`01068600`: `LR R0,R3` / `SRL R0,4` / `LA R1,1(R14,R2)` / `CLM R0,B'1000',0(R1)` / `BL INVDKEY  THATS MORE THAN WE HAVE`, comparing a page-table pointer's high byte against a byte in the swap table. What it asserts has to be read before the cards around it move, because converting a group and leaving one member is `I-162` by definition. So `DMKCDM` can go in whole and `DMKCDB` waits on that one read. | fix determined |
| **I-187** | **The error-recording area is two cylinders at 549 on VM50-1, CP reformats it by itself, and what is in it is a free log of every I/O error this conversion has caused.** | `DMKSYS 00120000` is `SYSERR=(549,2,VM50-1)` -- cylinders 549 and 550 of the 3350 at 6A1. `DMKCPI 03304000` calls `DMKIOEFL` at `CPIEXIT`, and the module's own step 19 says what that does: *"CALL DMKIOEFL TO INITIALIZE MACHINE CHECK AND I/O ERROR RECORDING AREAS **IF THEY HAVE NOT BEEN PREVIOUSLY FORMATTED**"* (`00252000`-`00254000`). So clearing `ERROR RECORDING area full; run CPEREP` does not need `CPEREP` and does not need CMS: zero those two cylinders in the pack image and CP formats them fresh at the next IPL. The supported route is `CPEREP`, which is a CMS program and so is blocked behind CMS running at all. **But the message is a symptom and the area is evidence.** It filled because every ESA/390 IPL this project has run has been generating I/O error records, and those records are a log of exactly the failures the I/O conversion is still causing -- device, CSW or IRB status, CCW address, retry count, per error, written by CP without being asked. Reading them is likely worth more than clearing them, and clearing them without reading them first throws away a diagnostic that was free. The record layout is `DMKIOE`'s; `DMKIOEFL` is the formatter and the place to start. Zeroing is also not a fix in any lasting sense: the area refills as long as CP is logging errors, so the question the message actually raises is which errors, not how to silence it. | open, characterised |

| **I-188** | **The segment table was built sixteen times longer than the machine, and my `I-183` constant turned that into an endless loop instead of a wasted page.** | The loop is closed and measured. Disassembling the window at `DMKPGS` against CP's own symbol table (`symtab.py`), every instruction matches the source: `PGOUT2 = X'638CE'`, `B2 = X'638E6'` holding `TM 3(R3),X'20'`, the `TRANS` expansion at `X'638F0'`-`X'63904'` as `LCTL C1,C1,16(R11)` / `LRA R7,0(,R1)` / `BZ` / `LA R2,64` / `L R15,APTRAN` / `SVC 8`, and `BNO PGOUT2` at `X'63912'`. The `SVC` old PSW at `X'20'` is `000C2000 00063906` -- `DMKPGS + 2310`, the instruction after that `SVC 8` -- so the caller is the same card as before the fix, `00962000`. `GR00 = 020FC4D9` is `DMKPTRCT`, CP's own count of calls to `DMKPTRAN` (cards `00291000`-`00293000`): **34,587,353**. `GR02 = GR04 = 01FFFFFF`, and `LR R4,R2` at `00961000` means that is `PGOUT2`'s scan limit, 32 MB minus one; `CR01 = 00FFD001` gives `STL = 1`, a two-unit table of 32 fullword entries, which is where the limit comes from. `GR03 = 00FFD080` is entry **32**, past the end, and climbing. **The arithmetic**: at `NEXTSEG` the fix I made reads `N R1,=X'00F00000'` then `A R1,=X'00100000'`. `X'00F00000'` is bits 8-11 -- sixteen segments. R1 climbs 1 MB at a time to `X'00F00000'`, the `A` makes it `X'01000000'`, and the next pass's `N` **zeroes it**, because 16 MB is bit 7 and the mask does not reach it. So R1 cycles 1-16 MB for ever, `CR R1,R2` against 32 MB always says within range, `BNH PGOUT2` is always taken, and R3 advances four bytes a time past the end of its table. Every register agrees: R3 beyond the table, R1 bounded, thirty-four million translations, the loop re-entering `PGOUT2` rather than `NEXTSEG`. **What I got wrong.** The original `X'00FF0000'` was bits 8-15, which for 64 KB segments is every segment a 24-bit address can name -- the original was fully general for its architecture. I narrowed the field to the first sixteen and called it the conversion, reasoning in `I-183`'s own deck comment that *"a 1 MB segment number is bits 8-11"*. That is true of a 16 MB machine and of nothing else; a 31-bit address has 2048 segments in bits 1-11. The mask is `X'7FF00000'`. It is `I-185`'s mistake exactly, made by me, one day after writing `I-185` up: converted against the machine in front of me rather than against the architecture, and invisible until something legitimately reached 16 MB. The same constant is at `DMKPGS 01049300`, which predates today, and I propagated it to `DMKATS 00863000 FORCEPG0` in `I-184` this afternoon. **And the mask alone will not close this.** With `X'7FF00000'` R1 reaches `X'01000000'` correctly, but `DMKPTRAN`'s first instruction is `LA R1,0(,R1)  STRIP HIGH BYTE - 24 BIT ADDRESSING ONLY`, so 16 MB arrives as 0, `CL R1,VMSIZE` accepts it, and `DMKPTRAN` clears segment **0**'s invalid bit while `DMKPGS` tests segment 16's -- `I-183`'s mechanism with a different pair of numbers. The scan must not run past `VMSIZE` at all, and it does because the table is too long: `DMKBLD 00207000 SRDL R0,8  SHIFT TO OBTAIN NUMBER OF SEGMENT TABLES` turns a page count into 64-byte units, and for S/370 it is exactly right -- 16 MB is 256 STEs, 1024 bytes, 16 units, `BCTR` to the `STL` of 15. An ESA/390 64-byte unit holds the same sixteen entries but they now cover **16 MB, not 1 MB**, so 16 MB needs one unit and the shift must be 12. Our deck left the shift alone and annotated the `STC` with *"THE VALUE IS UNCHANGED: ESA/390 COUNTS THE SEGMENT TABLE IN 64-BYTE UNITS MINUS ONE, EXACTLY AS S/370 DID, AND ONLY THE MEANING OF A UNIT MOVES -- 1 MB TO 16 MB"* (`I-129`). The comment states the fact that makes the value change by sixteen and then concludes it does not. **R3's units, settled by reading rather than guessing**, and the answer reverses which of the two defects is the root cause. `00249000 LR R7,R3` with `00250000 SLL R7,6  TIMES 64 BYTES PER TABLE` makes R3 a count of 64-byte units, and `00222500 LA R1,1(,R1)  SEGMENT COUNT/16` says the same thing in the original's own words; the `SLL R3,2` at `00300000` that worried me is a **different value** -- `00296000 SLR R3,R3` and `00298000 LH R3,SAVER1` reload it as a segment number first. So `SRDL R0,8` -> `SRDL R0,8+4` is a one-card change with its exact inverse already converted five cards away at `00222700` (`SLL R1,4+8  SEGMENTS, THEN PAGES PER SEG`). `I-162` once more: the pair was split and the easier half was done. **And the mask is not what closed the loop.** With a correctly sized table the `N` never sees 16 MB: at the last in-range segment R1 is `X'00F00000'`, the `N` leaves it, the `A` makes it `X'01000000'`, and `CR R1,R2` against `X'00FFFFFF'` ends the scan before the next mask. The wrap only happens because a 32-entry table set the limit to 32 MB and let R1 be masked while already past 16 MB. So the root cause is `DMKBLD 00207000`, and the narrow mask is what converted an over-long table from a harmless waste of sixteen invalid entries into a loop. Both are fixed: the shift, and all four of our `X'00F00000'` masks widened -- `DMKPGS 01049300` and `01054000`, `DMKATS 00863000`, `DMKDRD 01093000`. **The authority for the mask is IBM's, not mine**: `DMKVAT`'s guest-geometry table has a row per `CR0` code, and `CODEB0` -- *"LARGE PAGE, LARGE SEG, FULLWORD ENTRIES"*, which is this architecture exactly -- reads `DC X'000FF000'` / `DC X'7FF00000'` / `X'08',X'06'` / `H'10',H'18',H'4'` / `H'127',H'1024',H'64'`. Every one of those is a constant this project derived separately and painfully: the page mask our `DMKPTR 00321400` now carries, the segment mask, `PAGSHFT` 10 matching our `SRL R1,10`, `SEGSHFT` 18 matching our `SRL R8,18`, `PTEINCR` 4 for the fullword PTE, `PAGTLEN` 1024 matching `PAGTSWP`'s 256 fullword entries, and `PAGINCR` 64, the unit this issue is about. `MAXSEGS H'127'` confirms the `STL` field is seven bits, so 128 units of 16 MB is 2 GB and the field was always wide enough -- only the shift feeding it was wrong. | fixed |
| **I-189** | **`DMKDRD`'s dump reader still walks an S/370 page table three cards below ones we converted.** | Found while widening the segment mask at `01093000`. `01099000 TM 3(R7),X'01'  IS THE SEGMENT AVAILABLE ?` tests `X'01'`, the **S/370** segment-invalid bit; ESA/390's is `SEGINVAL EQU X'20'`, bit 26, which every other converted site in the tree already uses. `01101000 ICM R7,7,1(R7)  GET PAGE TABLE ADDRESS` loads three bytes from `STE+1`, the S/370 page-table origin in bits 8-28; the ESA/390 origin is bits 1-25, so it has to become `L R7,0(,R7)` with `N R7,=A(SEGPTOM)` -- the same replacement this project has already made in `DMKBLD 00246270` and a dozen other places. `01102000 S R7,F8  BACK UP TO PAGETABLE HEADER` subtracts the S/370 header length, where `CORE.XA0033DK` made the header `PAGPFRA-PAGSTMP` = 16. The cards either side of this cluster are ours -- `01090100`/`01090200` strip the length from `VMSEG`, `01096100` is `SRL R8,18`, `DMKVAT`'s `CODEB0` `SEGSHFT` -- so this is `I-162` yet again, and the fourth instance today. `01097000 SRL R9,9  CONVERT TO SWAP-TABLE INDEX` is **correct unchanged**: it relates a page's address weight of 4096 to an eight-byte `SWPTABLE` entry and neither moved, which is the same reasoning that kept `DMKATS 00344000`. **The fix is now fully determined**, from `CORE.XA0033DK` rather than derived: the converted header is `PAGSTMP` 0, `PAGACT` 4, `PAGTOT` 6, `PAGSHR` 8, `PAGSWP` 12, `PAGORIG` 16, a reserved fullword at 20 to keep `PAGPFRA` doubleword-aligned, and `PAGPFRA` at **24** -- which the deck's own comment confirms by arithmetic, *"a full page table is 24+256*4 = 1048 bytes instead of 16+16*2 = 48"*. So `01099000` becomes `TM 3(R7),SEGINVAL`; `01101000` becomes `L R7,0(,R7)` with `N R7,=A(SEGPTOM)`, the replacement already made at `DMKBLD 00246270`; and `01102000 S R7,F8` becomes `S R7,=A(PAGPFRA-PAGSHR)`, which is 16 and lands on `PAGSHR`, leaving the existing `L R7,4(0,R7)` to pick up `PAGSWP` unchanged -- one card rather than two. The `ICM`'s condition code is not used by the card below it, so dropping it is safe. Held out of the `I-188` build on sequencing grounds only: the decks for that build are already staged on the pack, and regenerating `DMKDRD.XA0036DK` now would put disk and pack out of step for no gain, since `DMKDRD` reads dumps and cannot affect an IPL. | fix determined |
| **I-190** | **Two independent instruments now agree on the `SRL ,11` family, and `CODEB0` supplies the replacement value.** | `geomchk.py` has been reporting five `PTE-SIZE-2` sites as PROVEN for a day -- `DMKDMP 00468000`, `DMKMCH 00857000` and `00858000`, `DMKTRD 00823000`, `DMKVMD 00929000` -- on the reasoning that a shift of 11 divides by 2048, which is a halfword page-table entry's stride. `codeb0.py` reaches the same five from the other direction and **names** the value: 11 is `CODE80`'s and `CODE90`'s `PAGSHFT`, the two **halfword-entry** rows of `DMKVAT`'s table, and `CODEB0`'s is **10**. A heuristic and a citation agreeing on the same five cards is worth more than either, and the citation also gives the fix rather than just the flag. The same scan found six cards with a shift of 11 or 14 that are **not** geometry, which is the useful half of a cross-check: `DMKNES 00542000`, `DMKNLD 00435000` and `DMKNLE 00924400` are all `SLL R1,14  MODEL TIMES 16K = STORAGE SIZE`, a 3705 communications controller's storage, and `DMKCPI 00695000 SRL R6,14  CONVERT TO PAGES/4` is bytes to pages and then a quarter. **One new pair to watch**, correctly unchanged today: `DMKUNT 00256000`/`00257000 SRA R14,11  DIVIDE BOTH BY 2048 .. TO YIELD START & END PAGE ADDR`, counting `NO. OF HALF-PAGES TO UNLOCK` after channel-program translation. 2048 here is the **IDAW** granularity, not a page-table stride, and it is `DMKCCW`'s convention as much as `DMKUNT`'s -- `DMKUNT` untranslates what `DMKCCW` built. So the two must be converted together or not at all, and `geomchk` was right not to flag it as DAT. | documented |
| **I-191** | **After `write`, the pack's 6A1 holds the ESA/390 nucleus, so the next S/370 build step must restore first -- and `stage`/`asmonly` deliberately do not.** | The second `I-188` build was launched as `stage && asmonly && write` on a pack that had been written at 14:03, and the boot produced **nothing at all**: no banner, no `Start ((Warm|Force|COLD|CKPT) ...)` prompt, no `LOGON AT`. `ipl 6A1` loaded our converted nucleus in S/370 mode, where it dies instantly and silently, exactly as `I-115` describes in the other direction. The first `devinit` succeeded because Hercules does it, then `cp start 00c` and `readcard` went to a CP that was not running, 000C was never drained, and every later `devinit` came back `HHCPN096E Device 0:000C busy or interrupt pending` -- seven times, which reads like a device problem and is a dead machine. **The sequencing rule**: `full`, `quick` and `spec` all restore before they stage, and a restore is what puts CE's own S/370 nucleus back on 6A1. `stage` and `asmonly` have no restore by design -- that is their whole purpose, `I-148`'s sliced build -- so they are valid only **before** a `write` in the same cycle, never after one. The first slice today worked for exactly that reason: `spec SNAP-0828` had restored at 13:28 and the write came after. **The guard held.** `mkrun`'s readcard check stopped the chain with *"8 readcard(s) were issued and only 0 file(s) reported HAS BEEN READ"*, so `asmonly` and `write` never ran, nothing was assembled from a truncated deck, and the nucleus on the pack is still the one the measurements were taken against. That is `I-101`'s and `I-117`'s discipline paying for itself: the failure was loud, early, and cost one staging pass. Worth adding to `build.sh` as a check rather than a rule to remember -- `stage` and `asmonly` can refuse to run when the last operation on the pack was a `write`, which the journal already records. | documented |
| **I-192** | **Wall 21: `ISK` does not exist in ESA/390, and CP's storage-protection check runs it the moment a second virtual machine touches storage.** | With `I-188` fixed, CP reaches `DMKCPI966I Initialization complete` and `AUTO LOGON *** AUTOLOG1 USERS = 002 BY OPERATOR` -- both firsts -- and then abends: `PSW=000C1000 000008B4 INST=09FF  ISK 15,15`, operation exception `CODE=0001`, `DMKDMP908I SYSTEM FAILURE; CODE PRG001`. `X'8B4'` resolves through `symtab.py` to `DMKPSADU + 168`, but the routines actually in that range are `DMKPSAFP`, `DMKPSASP`, `DMKPSAFC` and `DMKPSASC` -- `DMKSYM` simply does not list them, so the nearest preceding symbol is `DMKPSADU` and this is **not** the dump path. It is the fetch- and store-protection check CP runs whenever a virtual machine's instruction or channel program touches storage and the real key must be verified against the PSW or CAW key, which is reached as soon as a second virtual machine does anything. **The `LPSW` privileged-operation exception immediately above it in the log is a red herring**, and I nearly reported it as a defect: its PSW is `070D1000`, and byte 1 of `0D` is **problem state**, at virtual address 0. That is AUTOLOG1's virtual machine executing `LPSW` and CP intercepting and simulating it -- CP working correctly. **The family, counted exactly**: 54 instructions in 15 modules -- `DMKPTR` 15, `DMKCCW` 8, `DMKPSA` 5, `DMKMCH` 5, `DMKCDS` 4, then ones and twos in `DMKCDB`, `DMKCDM`, `DMKCFH`, `DMKDGD`, `DMKDIB`, `DMKPRV`, `DMKUNT`, `DMKVCA`, `DMKVMA`, `DMKVMD`. The mnemonics become `ISKE`, `SSKE` and `RRBE`, and the **bit positions survive unchanged**, which is what makes the bulk of it mechanical: `N R15,F8` for fetch-protect, `N R15,F240` for the access-control key and `N R15,F2` for the change bit all still read the right bits of an `ISKE` result. What needs reading at every site is the 2 KB-to-4 KB collapse. The PoO is explicit -- *"the 370-XA protection facilities include key-controlled protection on only 4K-byte blocks"* -- so each `ISK` / `LA R15,2048(,R15)` / `ISK` pair becomes one `ISKE` and each `RRB 0(R6)` / `RRB 2048(R6)` pair one `RRBE`. `DMKPSACC` at `00436000`-`00446000` is exactly that shape, and `I-177` already made the same collapse for `DMKPTR`'s two `SSKE` sites, so the precedent and its reasoning are in the tree. `X2048BND  GET MASK FOR BITS 8-20` can stay: `ISKE` takes the block from bits 1-19 of its operand and ignores the rest, so masking to a 2 KB boundary is harmless. **DMKPSA's five went first** -- on the live path, small enough to read in full, and the measurement was the point: with them in, the `ISK` abend was replaced by a `TCH` one, which is how wall 22 was found. 48 of the 54 are now converted. Six remain: `DMKPRV`'s two, which R-12's detector is for, and `DMKCDB`'s and `DMKCDM`'s four, which `I-186` owns. **One oddity of IBM's, preserved deliberately**: `DMKPTR 01022000` is `BZ *+8  NO REF BIT TO BACK-UP`, and `BZ` tests cc=0 only -- which for `RRB` means reference **and** change both off. On cc=1, reference off but change on, it falls through and sets the reference back-up bit for a page whose reference bit was never set. That looks like a defect in the 1979 code; the conversion reproduces it exactly, because changing behaviour while changing architecture makes both changes unprovable. Recorded so it is not mistaken later for something this project introduced. | fix built, unverified |
| **I-194** | **Wall 23: CE's five `HDK` modules were never reassembled, so they run with the pre-conversion `IOBLOK` and `RDEVBLOK` layouts inside a nucleus that uses the new ones.** After `enable all`, CP died in supervisor state, DAT off, executing data: `PRG007` (data exception, `DXC=01`, a stray halfword decoding as an HFP instruction on FPR 8 -- ESA/390 PoO Fig. 6-2, AFP register, CR0.13 off, action Suppress) at `F020D4`/`F030F4`, or `PRG001` at `5640E`/`F01C58`. The landing site moved whenever a breakpoint was armed, so breaking on the destination (w23f) failed; the sequence that worked was to trace the fixed branch *sources*: the dispatcher's unstack `BR R12` at `338D4` (sane every time, w23g), then DMKFRET's own code (w23h) -- which showed the return via `BR R5` with R5 = block+X'20' set *before* FRET was entered. The IOBIRA target `3D990` is `HDKD8C+X'3B0'`, CE's DIAGNOSE X'8C' module (not in `DMKSYM`, so `symtab.py` could not name it; its eyecatcher is at `3D5E0`). `HDKD8C` appends its work area after `IOBLOK` with `ORG ,` -- `CCWREAD`, `SAVERRET`, `SAVERDEV` -- and its compiled code reads `SAVERRET` at `X'50'`, i.e. an `IOBLOK` of `X'48'` bytes, the **old** nine doublewords. `XA0003DK` made it 23. The reassembled `DMKIOS` therefore stores `IOBOCCW`, the CCW address, on top of `SAVERRET`; on completion `HDKD8C` does `L R5,SAVERRET` / `BR R5` and branches into the channel program. `enable` of a 3270 reaches it through `DMKGRFEN`, which is why the wall sat exactly behind `enable all`. **Root cause of the omission**: `ASMDMK EXEC` names the 186 DMK modules and nothing else, while `CPLOAD` also loads `HDKD58 HDKD7C HDKD8C HDKCQU HDKCQA`; the build journal shows zero HDK assemblies, so all five rode on CE's shipped TEXT decks. `HDKD58` and `HDKD7C` also allocate `LA R0,IOBSIZE` with the old 9, so every IOBLOK they build is 112 bytes short for the ORB/IRB/XSAV that DMKIOS writes into it -- a second, silent free-storage corruptor from the same cause. None of the five contains an S/370-only instruction; reassembly is the entire fix. `build.sh full` now carries `$HDKMODS`. **Eliminated on the way, each by measurement, and worth keeping**: the PSA interrupt new-PSWs (all correct, `psa.py`), `IOBSIZE` (computed after the insertion), `RDEVBLOK` sizing (`RDEVICE.XA0032DK` emits `RDEVSSID` on every device-class path), `IOINTPRM=0` for unsolicited status (`DMKCPI.XA0013DK` sets `PMCWPARM` at IPL). Two wrong turns retracted in the row's own words: the "last SVC issuer" is noise, and the fixed free area starts at `DMKFREHI=F01000`, so the blocks were ordinary free storage, not page frames. | **FIXED** -- verified 02:33 4 Oct: `enable all`, `query dasd` (17 DASD), `cp disc`, `logon maint` all complete; CP now answers typed commands under ESA/390. The next failure is FRE013 at logon (wall 24, I-195). |

| **I-199** | **Wall 26: `CP ENTERED; PROGRAM INTERRUPT LOOP` on the guest's first instruction at `IPL 190`.** w31: `Operation exception … PSW=070D0000 000203F4 INST=9C00D000 SIO 0(13)`, then PSW 0, opcode 00, loop. CMS's IPL text issues `SIO`. On S/370 that is a privileged-operation exception (code 2) in problem state; DMKPRG sends code 2 to DMKPRVLG, `GETINST` sees 9C–9F and goes to DMKVSIEX. On ESA/390 the opcode is undefined — **operation** exception, code 1 — and DMKPRV's `BCT R3,GETINST` sends code 1 to `OPSIM`, which knows CS/CDS/EX and reflects everything else. The guest's program-new PSW is zero at IPL time, hence the loop. Not the BC-mode axis (`I-79`): the guest PSW is EC. Fix (XA0041DK, DMKPRV `CHEKPROB`, IBM's own SPKA/IPK pattern widened): opcodes `08 09 B213 9C 9D 9E 9F` go to `GETINST` as privileged ops, with INTPR rewritten to 2; a guest in its own problem state gets code 2 reflected, as S/370 gave it. | **FIXED** -- verified 05:40 (w32): SIO and the TIO polling loop simulated, the CMS nucleus loads and starts executing at `F80A36` (SSK simulated too). They relied on LRA-in-AMODE-24 ignoring byte 0 and on DMKPTRAN's `LA R1,0(,R1)`. **A 24-bit guest cannot form an address above 16 MB, so masking its addresses to 24 bits is correct**, not a bug; the I-208 alias was CP applying that mask to the console's address too. The entry test `TM SAVER2+3,AMODE31` read the *previous* caller's R2: **SAVER2 is written by ENTER (`STM R0,R11,SAVEREGS`)**, not by the SVC handler, so at the module's first instruction it is stale. The test now follows ENTER (R1 survives ENTER; R10 is set up by then, I-218 moot). | **I-224** | **M2 step 2: CP itself at AMODE 31.** Two parts, built and tested separately. **Part 1, the strip sweep (I-126)**: `tools/strips.py` inventories 275 `LA Rx,0(,Ry)` sites in CP (241 in 78 nucleus modules; one CC-sensitive, in the DMKDDR utility); XA0047DK replaces 209 of them in 74 modules with `N Rx,XRIGHT24` (copies: `LR` + `N`), the 30 inside earlier deck replacements already having explicit masks and DMKLD00E (the 24-bit loader) left alone. Behaviour-neutral while CP is AMODE 24, and tested so: i232 assembled clean (190 decks, 94 patched modules), w64 = w62 and w65 = w46 identical. **Part 2, the PSWs**: XA0048DK puts bit 32 into DMKCPI's `CPIPSWS` (external, SVC, program, I/O new PSWs, MCKPSW, RESTPSW) and DMKMCH's five machine-check PSWs as `X'80',AL3(entry)` — DMKWRM's `AL1(flags),AL3(DMKRSPPR)` form, a 3-byte relocation the loader is known to do. SVC 8/12 carry the mode through SAVERETN; guests keep their own 24-bit PSW; the CPEXBLOK unstack LPSW is AP-only. | **closed** 5 Oct 13:22 (i241, w109–w111): CP runs AMODE 31 end to end — IPL, every LINK, AUTOLOG1's init, `IPL CMS`/`IPL 190`, directory recompile, `DEF STOR 32M`, stores above 16 MB, LOGOFF, SHUTDOWN, GCCLIB regression unchanged. | **I-225** | **CP's first run in AMODE 31 (i233) died at IPL before the console was up** — wait code 4 (DMKDMP's "wait stop"), no message. DMKFREE's `FREE08` computes the end of the last larger free block with `LA R5,0(R8,R9)`: for the block that reaches 16 MB that is `01000000`, which `LA` in AMODE 24 makes 0, and `SR R5,R2` then hands out the block's address as a negative number whose low 24 bits are right. Every savearea DMKSVC chained at IPL was one. `LR R5,R8 / ALR R5,R9`. **The class the strip sweep cannot see: a two-register `LA` sum that lands exactly on 16 MB.** All "end of …" sums in the nucleus were read; DMKFRET's two use `CL` against the machine size and are right in both modes; DMKFRE's `FREE08` was the only one at the storage top. | **fixed** (i234) |
| **I-226** | **PRG005 at AUTOLOG1's `IPL CMS`, CP in AMODE 31 (i234, w70/w71).** `pgmtrace`: addressing exception at `800044F4`, `L R2,0(,R3)`, R3 = `06FFCBBC`. DMKDGD's `DGUNLOK1` loads the CCW word whole — `L R3,RCWADDR`, command code 06 in byte 0 — and uses R3 as a base for the IDAL; the `LA R2,0(,R3)` beside it was the only strip, and the only thing the sweep could see. **The class the sweep cannot see: a flagged word used directly as a base or index register.** Fixed by an explicit `N Rx,XRIGHT24` after every `L Rx,RCWADDR` in the nucleus that had no strip of its own (DMKCCW 00793000, 04086000; DMKDGD 00254000, 00286000, 00646000; DMKDIB 01063000); a CCW address is a 24-bit field by definition. Found by running, which is the only way this class is found. | **fixed** (i235) |
| **I-227** | **No usable load map for the CP nucleus.** `CPNUC MAP` on MAINT 191 is CE's 2018 map (the build never rewrites it), the loader's punched map failed with RECORD EXCEEDS, and pageable CP modules execute at real frame addresses, not load addresses — so Hercules breakpoints could be set only by guessing. Fixed by making `build.sh write` punch the nucleus deck itself (first VMFLOAD to the real punch 00D class A, kept as `io/nucleus.deck`; second spooled to `*` for the IPL) and `deckscan.py --map` build the map from the deck: it handles `\x02SPB` (page align), `\x02SLC`, the pageable boundary after DMKCPE and the loader's no-straddle rule. Verified by `dcp` of five module IDs in storage (DMKTAP 57000, DMKBLD 64000, DMKMSG 65000, DMKRSE 66000, DMKVDR 67000; DMKDSP 32D20, DMKPTR 3DBE8). Products: `cpnuc.map`, `nucsyms.txt`. A standalone `write` must follow `reset SNAP` (6A1 holds the ESA nucleus after a write). | **fixed** |
| **I-228** | **Every directory LINK silently failed at logon in AMODE 31 (i235, w83): `q v dasd` showed no 190/19D/19E, `IPL 190` looped.** Breakpoint on DMKUDRRD plus instruction trace across one LINK: DMKSCNVU correctly returns CC 3 (device does not exist); DMKLNK then saves the CC with `BALR R15,0` and restores it with `SPM R15`. In AMODE 24 BALR puts ILC/CC/program mask in byte 0 of the link register; in AMODE 31 it stores bit 0 and a 31-bit address and **no condition code**, so SPM restored CC 0 = "already defined" (ERROR110, suppressed at logon). **The third class invisible to the sweep: the `BALR Rx,0` condition-code save idiom.** `BALR Rx,0` used only to establish a base is harmless (bit 0 ignored as a base). Fixed with the ESA/390 `IPM Rx` (new XAOPS macro, `B222`) at the 16 CC-save sites: DMKCPI 00510000/00530000, DMKCQR 00595000, DMKCSP 00885250, DMKEPS 00220000/00227300, DMKLNK 00654200, DMKLOG 00616000, DMKPAG 01153000, DMKRPA 00217000, DMKTCS 00791000, DMKTRC 01385520, DMKVSI 00208000; DMKPTR's XA0038DK cards become `IPM R14`/`IPM R15` (DMKVSJ 00176000 is dead code, removed by XA0018DK). | **I-229** | **PRG021 one second after `DMKCPI966I`, the first time AUTOLOG1's PROFILE ran under AMODE 31 (i236, w84–w87).** pgmtrace: operand exception on `SSCH` in DMKCNS's XAIOB shim with R1 = 0 (RDEVSSID) and R8 = the RDEVBLOK of line 020, the first 2701 TTY line — a device DMKRIO names and the Hercules configuration lacks (the I-211 class) — being ENABLEd. It was being enabled because its RDEVDISA bit was clear: DMKCPI's IPL device check had seen it as present. **The second CC-save idiom the sweep cannot see: CP's trace subroutines are entered with `BAL Rlink` and read the caller's condition code out of byte 0 of the link register** — `STCM Rlink,8,1(R14)` into the trace entry, `SPM Rlink` to restore it, `B 2(,Rlink)` over the inline trace code. Fixed with `IPM Rlink` as the subroutine's first instruction and `N Rlink,XRIGHT24` wherever the link is then used as an address, in DMKCPI TRACESUB (link R1, also the trace-code `IC`), DMKCNS CNTRACE, DMKIOS TRACESUB and DMKVSJ HIOTRACE (link R15); and the XAIOB shims (DMKCNS, DMKIOT) answer CC3 for RDEVSSID 0 as DMKIOS's own copy already did, so a missing device can never reach SSCH again. It was OK because AMODE 24 ignored byte 0; in AMODE 31 it is an address above real storage. DMKVMI is CP code that executes **inside the virtual machine** (copied to the guest's X'20000'), and the sweep (I-224, XA0047DK) had turned its `LA Rx,0(,Rx)` strips — including `LA R15,0(,R15) STRIP HIGH BYTE (FROM BALR) FROM BASE REG` — into `N Rx,XRIGHT24`, which read the GUEST's page 0, not CP's PSA: zero, so the base register and every data address went to zero. DMKVMI joins DMKLD00E in `STRIP_SKIP`; its XA0047DK is gone and mkdeck now drops AUXLCL lines for decks a generator no longer produces. | **I-232** | **`IPL 190` at 16M (i239, w109) abended CP with PRG005 in DMKUNT+E2, the IDA path of the CCW untranslate.** `L R5,RCWADDR` — command code in byte 0 — then `L R14,0(,R5)` at IDASET; the `LA R2,0(,R5)` strip beside it was swept, but the IDA path uses R5 itself (the I-226 class again). `grep 'L  *R.*,RCWADDR'` over the nucleus then listed every such load without a strip of its own: DMKUNT 00222450 and 00378000 (UNREL5), DMKTRK 00360000 and 00507000, DMKVCA 00361000, 01171000 and 01604000 (virtual CTCA, fixed for completeness). Each gets `N Rx,XRIGHT24` after the load (XA0048DK). The 15M case was CMS's own `DMSINI260T VIRTUAL MACHINE SIZE TOO SMALL` (DIAG X'60' correct; CMS loads high), not a CP fault. | **fixed** (i240) — verified w109/w110: `IPL 190` at 16M reaches CMS, QUERY DISK answers |
| **I-233** | **PRG005 in DMKPTR RSPGLOOP when AUTOLOG1 logged off (i240, w109).** `ICM R2,B'0111',PAGSHR+1` then `ICM R2,B'1111',SHRSEGCT-SHRTABLE(R2)`: ICM with mask 7 loads a 3-byte pointer and **leaves byte 0 of the register as it was**; AMODE 24 never looked at it, AMODE 31 addresses storage with it. The sixth idiom the sweep cannot see, and the first that is not about a *stored* word but about a *register's* history. Inventory: strips.py kind `icm3`, 100 sites in nucleus modules; 59 are not provably clean (no SR/SLR/XR of the register, no LA/LH/IC/SRL into it, in the preceding eight instructions) and get `SR Rx,Rx` in front of the ICM (`icm3cards()` in build.py, XA0048DK, 29 modules). ICM sets the condition code the code then tests, so the SR changes nothing it reads. Five exceptions are listed by hand: DMKPER 00628000, DMKRSE 00508000, DMKVER 00571000 (clean by a path the rule cannot see) and DMKCPS 01358000, DMKVDE 00813000 (`SLL R3,24` first — they build DMKFRET's `count || address` on purpose; I-230 strips it at FRET). The 457 `STCM Rx,7` sites are the mirror image and harmless: they store three bytes. | **fixed** (i241) — verified w109/w110/w111 13:15–13:22: AUTOLOG1 logs off, full 32M run and GCCLIB regression clean |
| **I-234** | **M2 step 3, first cut: CMS could not IPL (w114 — `IPL CMS` fell straight into CP READ, a loop of `LPSW 0` / `LPSW` wait).** The mode test `TM VMPSW+4,X'80'` is right only for an EC-mode PSW; CMS runs in **BC mode**, where bits 32-33 of the PSW are the ILC, so every CMS PSW with ILC 2 or 3 looked like a 31-bit guest, its operand addresses kept the junk byte 0 of `BALR`-set base registers (R12 = `60F8C132`), and the LPSW operand was fetched from `60F8BC58`. Fixed by making the mode a maintained VMBLOK flag, `VMAM31` (VMFSTAT X'01', EQU), set and cleared by the three places CP accepts a guest PSW — DMKDSP PSWCKSUB (off, then on for an EC PSW with bit 32), DMKPRV's PRLPSWEC fast path, DMKSVC's fast reflection — and tested with one `TM VMFSTAT,VMAM31` in GADR31, TRL31, DMKPSARX and DMKPTRAN. | **fixed** (i244) — verified w114/w115/w116 18:04–18:10: the G31 guest runs in AMODE 31 (`AMODE 31` stored at 1FF0000 by the guest, its SVC old PSW `00080000 8000041E` with bit 32 on, `HI31` at 1000000, wait `000A0000 00000031`); IPL CMS, the 32M run and the GCCLIB regression unchanged |
| **I-235** | **CP abends when a user who IPLed the saved system CMS IPLs something else (w117, c1).** `LOGON MAINT` (directory `IPL CMS`, shared by frame per XA0045DK) then `CP IPL 190` → `PRG005`; `IPL 00C CLEAR` of VMFLOAD's deck from the same state → `PTR018` (`VMPAGES` going negative in DMKPTR's steal/reset path). Leaving a named system is M3 increment 2 — `SHRUSECT`/`VMSHRSYS` bookkeeping, dropping the copies, freeing the models — listed as not done in `33-FRAME-SHARING.md`; DMKPGS's release still follows VM/370's assumption that the shared segment's page table *is* the SHRTABLE's. Every earlier test (w84–w116) logged on `NOIPL` and never left a shared system, so the gap was never exercised. Found on the first `cmswrite`. Re-examined after I-236: the `IPL 190` PRG005 may be the same flag byte, to be re-tested on i245. | **fixed for the device-IPL and DEF STOR paths** (XA0054DK, 9 Oct, M7.8): a full release in DMKDEH/DMKCFG frees the VMABLOKs and clears VMSHRSYS/VMSHR; a device IPL by a user in a named system is forced CLEAR. Model tables (FRAME-SHARING), decided 11 Oct (9.2): **kept resident by design** -- one locked model per shared 64 KB group, about 60 frames for CMS + GCCLIB, below 1% of the low frames; freeing them when the last user leaves would need every holder's copied PTEs found and dropped first, the same machinery as the open invalidation of other sharers' copies after DMKVMASH, which is deferred to the CP work of M10 |
| **I-236** | **PRG005 in DMKPTRFD on the first paging workload (`IPL 00C` of VMFLOAD's CMS deck into a 16 MB MAINT; w121 pgmtrace: `NI 0(R5),X'3A'` with R5 = `20FD94F8`).** `CORSWPNT` is a fullword whose byte 0 is `CORFLAG` (`ORG CORSWPNT / CORFLAG DS 1X`). CP reads the pointer with `ICM Rx,B'0111',CORSWPNT+1` nearly everywhere, and with a plain `L` at thirteen sites — harmless in AMODE 24. A frame on the flush list has `CORFLUSH = X'20'`, so the steal path's `L R5,CORSWPNT` (DMKPTR 01206000) became an address above 16 MB and the page-out completion took an addressing exception. The seventh sweep-invisible idiom: a *flag-bearing pointer word loaded whole*. Found by `grep "L  *R[0-9]*,CORSWPNT"`; `CORFPNT/CORBPNT/CORPGPNT` carry no flags. Sites: DMKATS 00809000, DMKCCW 00971000/03582000, DMKCDS 00890000, DMKDGD 00516000, DMKMCH 00618000, DMKPTR 00371000/00826000/01206000, DMKRPA 00284000, DMKUDU 00274000, DMKVMA 00280100/00456000 (the last between an `LTR` and its `BNZ`, so the `N` moves ahead of the `LTR`). Deck XA0050DK (`CORSWMODS` in build.py). Every test before this one ran in a 16 MB real machine with guests small enough never to page. | **fixed** (i245) — verified i248/w160: the CMS nucleus load pages and completes |
| **I-237** | **PTR018 on the first paging workload, after I-236 (c1 20:16, w123): `VMPAGES` of the SYSTEM VMBLOK was 1 after initialisation instead of ~520 (CE original, measured on the pristine pack: `X'20A'`), so the first steal of a CP pageable page took it negative.** Found by an instruction trace of the dynamic area (w141): XA0045DK's `SHRTBLD` counted the frames it moved to SYSTEM with `LA R0,1(,R0)` — and **register 0 as a base is no base**, so the instruction loads 1 and the count was *set* to 1 instead of incremented. DMKATS XA0036DK had the same idiom twice (`LA R0,1(,R0)` after `N R0,=A(SEGPTLF)`). All three are `AL Rx,F1` now, and `mkdeck.verify` rejects any generated card with `(,R0)`/`(Rx,R0)` as a base or index, which the assembler cannot. Visible only once CP pages: no earlier test had a 16 MB guest load a nucleus. | **fixed** (i246) — SYSTEM's count stays ~520 (CE: X'20A'); verified w160 |
| **I-238** | **PRG005 instruction fetch at `FF03EB84` after a paging I/O error (w122, i246).** DMKPAG reports a failed page I/O by storing X'FF' into byte 0 of the CPEXBLOK's exit address (`STC R9,CPEXADD`, R9 = -1); DMKDSP's unstack does `LTR R15,R15` / `BR R15`, so the flag reaches the exit (DMKPTRFD) as the condition code — and in AMODE 24 the BR ignored the byte. Fixed in DMKDSP (XA0049DK 01943000): `SLL R15,8` / `SRL R15,8` between the LTR and the BR — shifts set no condition code, so the contract holds. | **fixed** (i247) — verified w160 |
| **I-239** | **CE packaging: VM50-4's allocation record says TEMP for cylinders 1–100, but the whole volume is formatted with 800-byte CMS records.** `SYSOWN (VM50-4,PAGE)` makes it a preferred paging volume, so the first paging write CP ever issued went to `6A4` cyl 100 rec 2 and ended in unit check + incorrect length (`Sense=NRF`, w143 device trace); on CE itself the same would happen the first time it pages, which a 16 MB machine with CE's small users never does. VM50-1 (20–79), VM50-2 (466–509) and VM50-3 (101–200) are 4 KB-formatted TEMP/PAGE and fine. Fixed on the working pack by changing the 100 bytes of cylinder-0 record 4 from X'00' (TEMP) to X'02' (TDSK) — `cckd2ckd`, patch, `ckd2cckd`; the original kept as `disks/vm50-4.cckd.ce-orig`; the shadows do not cover cylinder 0, verified with `cckddiag`. **To report upstream.** | **fixed on our pack** 5 Oct 21:50 |
| **I-240** | **A released page's PTE became `00000001` — real frame 0, valid (w159: MAINT's tables at the disabled wait).** Two DMKPTR sites that return a frame (01134000 FREEXIT, 01683000 PTR11A) kept the original's *"MVI 0(R1),0 / NI 1(R1),PAGINVAL+PAGREF"* intent as `NI +3,PAGREF / XC 0(3) / NI +2,PAGINV` — but the ESA/390 I bit is in byte 2, inside the three bytes the XC clears, so the invalid bit went with the address and the entry mapped the guest's page to **CP's own page 0**. The CMS loader relocating itself then wrote its new PSWs through that mapping into CP's PSA (SVC new byte 5 → `FE`, program new byte 1 → wait bit, both seen in w146/w153), and its next privileged instruction loaded a wait PSW with DMKPRG's address (`000A0000 80002028`). Visible only once pages are released and re-touched under load — the first paging workload. XC two bytes, NI the nibble: both sites. DMKMCH's copy of the idiom ends in `OI PAGINV` and was right. Found by dumping the top 256 KB at the wait and walking the 16 segment tables offline. | **fixed** (i248) — verified w160: `cmswrite 290` completes, `IPL 290` boots the rebuilt nucleus |
| **I-241** | **An EC-mode guest never finds its IPL device: `I-47`'s DMKVMI card wrote the address into `SYSIPLDV` — in the *guest's* storage.** DMKVMI is the IPL simulator that `DMKCFP` copies into the virtual machine at X'20000'; its last act is to leave the IPL device address where a real S/370 IPL would: `IPLPSW+2` for a BC-mode IPL PSW, `INTTIO` (X'BA') for an EC-mode one. `I-47` read that `INTTIO` as CP's own PSA field and replaced it with `SYSIPLDV` — correct for every nucleus-resident site, wrong here, because this low core is the guest's. BC-mode CMS never noticed (it reads `IPLPSW+2`). The first EC-mode CMS (M5b) read X'BA' and got `0300`: the record-ID byte of its own IPL text's SEARCH argument, which the IPL records read into X'68'–X'C1'. DIAG X'20' to device 300 → `DMSINI902T` for ever (w165, w168, w170: `IPL 290 STOP` showed `BA = 0300` before DMSINI's first instruction). Found with CP's own TRACE PROG (the wait PSW `000E0000 40C9D5C9` — CL4' INI' in bytes 4–7 — is a specification exception in EC mode, which turned the wait into a restart loop; fixed on the CMS side too, DMSINI 00192000 / DMSCRD 00392000). The card now keeps `STH R13,INTTIO` with a comment saying whose storage it is. | **fixed** (i249) — w169: `IPL 290` under ECMODE boots, `TRACE SVC` shows EC reflection |
| **I-242** | **GCCLIB and BREXX re-enable interrupts with a hard-coded BC mask.** `CMSSETNU`/`CMSSETFL` in GCCLIB's CMSSYS (and BREXX's HOSTCMD) do `IPK / SPKA 0 / SSM *+1 … SPKA 0(R2) / SSM =X'FF'`. X'FF' is "all channels + external" in BC mode and a specification exception in EC mode (bits 0, 2–4 must be zero), so the EC-mode CMS died in the PROFILE EXEC (`03EC… F3A04A`). Fix: `STNSM <save>,X'00'` … `SSM <save>` — save and restore the caller's real mask, correct in both modes. Source: GCCCMS 891 `CMSSYS ASSEMBLE` (original kept as `CMSSYS ASMORIG`), diff in `arch/31bit/gcclib/` for upstream. Installed: the 19E runtime (GCCLIB TEXT 07/05/22) is newer than the GCCCMS sources (02/17/21) — a rebuild from those sources would downgrade it and did not even load — so the shipped GCCLIB TEXT/TXTLIB (5 sites) and BREXX TEXT (1 site, saving into its own X'FF' literal) were patched in place: same-length base/displacement instructions, no RLD change. Originals on 19E as `*ORIG`. | **fixed** (w190, w192); report upstream (Adrian) |
| **I-243** | **STNSM is an operation exception in a virtual machine without ECMODE** — VM/370 simulates a S/360 for non-370 machines (DMKPRV `PSTSSM`: `TM VMPSTAT,VMV370R / BZ OPEREXCP`), so the I-242 fix broke BC-mode CMS for CMSUSER (`F3A03E PROG 0001`). The EC-mode CMS needs ECMODE anyway (M5b's open "directory option" item): `OPTION ECMODE` added to the 11 logon-able users that lacked it (ASSIST AUTOLOG1 CMSBATCH CMSUSER CMS67 CMS67M KICKS MECAFF OPERATOR PASCAL WAKEUP), `DIRECT USER DIRECT A`, original kept as `USER DIRORIG`; diff in `arch/31bit/directory/`. | **fixed** (w192: IPL 190 BC + REXX, IPL 290 EC + REXX, same user) |
| **I-244** | **A guest that changes AMODE by itself is invisible to CP.** VMAM31 (the guest's addressing mode, which GADR31 and TRL31 use to decode every guest address in a simulated instruction) was recomputed only by the PSW gatekeepers -- LPSW and interrupt reflection. A program that switches with BSM/BASSM runs natively and CP never sees it, so its next simulated privileged instruction is decoded as 24-bit: HIGHSTOR's SSK on X'01001000' would have set the key of X'00001000'. Fix in DMKPRG at 00290000, where an EC-mode guest's program old PSW address word is copied into VMPSW+4 on every interception: take the mode from the same word. First CP module built and written under our own ESA/390 CP (`cpstage`/`cpwrite`, no pack restore). | **fixed** (i250; w202: 40 MB keyed and used above 16 MB in AMODE 31) |
| **I-245** | **CE's SVC 120 (GETMAIN/FREEMAIN RU) was an S/380 placeholder**: DMSSVT returned the fixed address X'04100000' for every request and did nothing on FREEMAIN, relying on S/380 Hercules to back the storage. GCC380 (PDPCLIB built for S/380: AMODE 31, BSM to 24 around CMS calls) asks once for a 60,000,008-byte heap (R0=X'03938708', R15=X'72'); FREEMAIN RU is R15=X'03' with R1 = the address -- and is also used for storage that came from an ordinary GETMAIN below the line (w209: X'002DA2F8', X'0002F418'). Now: FREEMAIN above 16 MB -> HIGHSTOR RELEASE; GETMAIN from an AMODE 31 caller -> HIGHSTOR OBTAIN (LOC=ANY); everything else, and a GETMAIN HIGHSTOR cannot satisfy, goes to DMSSMN10 exactly as the SVC 10 dispatch would (so an impossible request abends instead of returning address 0, which is what w208 did in a 64 MB machine). | **fixed** (DMSSVT XA0051DK; w209/w210) |
| **I-246** | **`CP Q V STOR` shows five digits**: a 128 MB machine reports `31072K` (131072K truncated). `DEF STOR` echoes the right value. Cosmetic; the edit mask in CP's QUERY VIRTUAL STORAGE was sized for VM/370's 16 MB. | **open** — cosmetic |
| **I-247** | **DMSEXS under the EC-mode CMS: specification exception (w214).** The `DMSEXS` macro (system-key execution for COPYFILE, EDIT, LKED, MOVEFILE, ZAP, the library and tape commands) does `BAL 1,x` / `SVC 203`; DMSITSXS builds `RET` from R1 and `LPSW RET`. In AMODE 24 the BAL puts ILC/CC/program mask in R1's byte 0 — ignored in a BC PSW, but EC PSW bits 32–39 must be zero, so `COPYFILE` died with `DMSITP144T SPECIFICATION EXCEPTION ... IN ROUTINE DMSEXS`. No EC-mode test had used a DMSEXS command before. DMSITS 01293000: `MVI RET+4,0` after the store. | **fixed** — w215: COPYFILE of 60 files to D |
| **I-248** | **SVC 120 sent LOC=BELOW requests above the line.** M5d's DMSSVT routed every GETMAIN RU from an AMODE 31 caller to HIGHSTOR; PDPCLIB-S380 asks for its DCBs and I/O buffers with LOC=BELOW. Now only requests with the LOC=ANY (virtual) flag X'20' in R15 byte 3 go high; the rest go to DMSSMN as an SVC 10 GETMAIN would. | **fixed** — did not by itself stop the GCC380 overlay (w223) |
| **I-249** | **DMSSVT's work area came out 4 bytes short.** TEMPSPC (per-call OS-simulation work, from DMSFREE) is sized `TEMPLNT EQU (TEMPSEND-TEMPSPC)/8` doublewords — rounding *down*. M5d added 36 bytes (S120R0..S120PL), so the area lost its last 4 bytes and the SVC 120 path stored S120PL+20 into the next DMSFREE block. `DS 0D` before TEMPSEND. **This was the cause of the DMSFRE 167T/164T chain overlays** (`CHAIN HEADER AT 003710`, element at X'EE3000' holding `002DA2F8 00000028`) seen with GCC380 from w222 on and once with GCC370 (w236). The earlier reading — GCC380's S/380 PDPCLIB passing addresses above the line to OS simulation (BLDL) — was wrong: with the area rounded, HELLO31 compiles under GCC380 with SYSTERM defined and its heap above 16 MB. | **fixed** — w238 (GCC370), w240 (GCC380) |
| **I-250** | **ESA/390 has no interval timer; VM/370 CP time-slices with it.** CP keeps `TIMER` (real X'50'), `QUANTUM` and `QUANTUMR` in the PSA; S/370 hardware decremented X'50' and its X'0080' external interrupt ended the slice, and DMKDSP's UPVIRT advances each BC guest's virtual X'50' timer from the real one's movement. Under ESA/390 Hercules leaves X'50' alone, so quanta never ran out and virtual interval timers stood still. Found when CMSUSER got no CPU for minutes while **CPWATCH** looped (w240/w248): CPWATCH is a stand-alone S/370 CP monitor (`CP IPL 099` of its own deck, `DIAG X'04'` reads of CP storage), directory **priority 5** against CMSUSER's 50, and it never stopped being runnable. **M4c (XA0052DK):** DMKSCHTE emulates the interval timer from the TOD clock at every FLIH entry that snapshots it (DMKSVC, DMKPRG, DMKIOT, DMKPSA external; not DMKMCH) — `units = (TOD*3 + rem)/160000`, the remainder carried because CPWATCH enters CP every few microseconds and one unit is 13 µs (the first version, without it, never moved TIMER); a 10 ms clock-comparator TRQBLOK (DMKSCHTK, started by DMKSCHTI's first sample via DMKSCHTS — DMKCPI had no room, CPIATABL fell off its base) guarantees entries while a guest computes. Verified: X'50' now moves (w252), CP stable, RXVMINTP compile and shutdown clean. **Not** a fix for CPWATCH starving CMSUSER: a runnable priority-5 user is preferred by design. | **M4c in** — CPWATCH's own loop open (I-251) |
| **I-251** | **CPWATCH loops under VM/370plus** (BC PSW at X'A72'..X'A9A', `DIAG X'04'` storage reads, VTIME ~10% of elapsed, TTIME far more; RDR/PCH 618 each). A stand-alone monitor written for VM/370's S/370 CP layout; it probably follows a structure that moved, or waits on a timer it no longer gets. At priority 5 it starves every default-priority user. Workaround: test runs `FORCE CPWATCH`. | **worked around** 7 Oct 00:03 (w254): AUTOLOG1's PROFILE EXEC (AUTOLOG1 191) has `*P AUTOLOG CPWATCH CPWATCH` and `*P SET PRIOR CPWATCH 5` — the first letter only, so restoring is one character each. Porting CPWATCH (CPWSYS/CPWDSP ASSEMBLE on its 191) stays open. **11 Oct (4c), the mechanism:** CPWATCH's main loop is `SETIME n` (SVCTIMES: X'50' = n*76800) ... collect with DIAG 4 ... `WAIT` (SVCWAIT waits only if X'50' is still positive). Its virtual X'50' was negative from the first look after logon (-51323, then decreasing at 76800/s): every snapshot takes longer than its interval, so it never waits -- a busy loop by design, not a CP fault in the timer (UPVIRT moves X'50' at the right rate). The snapshot walks RDEVBLOKs with the stride `RDEVSIZE*8` of the DMKMAC it was assembled with (2005); our RDEVBLOK is not that size, and the device count is far above CE's. Sources now in `arch/31bit/cpwatch/orig` (punched from CPWATCH 191). Next: rebuild with CPWR6 CNTRL against VM/370plus's DMKMAC and time one snapshot |
| **I-252** | **GCC380 abends 80A (`DMSSMN109S VIRTUAL STORAGE CAPACITY EXCEEDED`) after writing its output** — on larger sources in a 128 MB machine (CMSIO, M5G2) and on every source in a 256 MB machine (w263, w264). ADSTOP at DMSSMN's 109S path: the caller is AMODE 31 at X'021462' (OLDPSW `03EC000A 80021462`), SVC 10 with R0=X'001FC3AC', R1=X'02D203C0'. The code at X'021436' is the FREEPOOL expansion after CLOSE (`L R15,20(R1)` = DCBBUFCB, `OI 23(R1),1`, length BUFNO*BUFL+8, SVC 10): PDPCLIB frees a buffer pool whose BUFCB it never set, so R1 is a stale pointer into its heap above 16 MB. DMSSMN reads a nonzero byte 0 of R1 as GETMAIN (the 24-bit convention) and fails a 2 MB GETMAIN. Fix: from an AMODE 31 caller, SVC 10 is GETMAIN only if R1 bit 0 is on (the XA rule); a FREEMAIN above the line is not DMSSMN's storage and is ignored (DMSSMN XA0051DK). Workaround until then: GCCLIB31 is compiled with GCC370, whose output is the same. | **fixed** (DMSSMN XA0051DK; w265) |

| **I-263** | **CP never saved an ESA/390 guest's access registers.** One Linux guest owned the CPU's set; with two, they shared it, and Debian 2's init ran with Debian 1's A0 (its thread pointer, 7Dxxx720): segment-translation exceptions on TLS addresses, SIGSEGV, `Attempted to kill init`. Rare under the old take-when-not-waiting rule; frequent once the lock is released explicitly (ca8f03c). | **fixed** — XA0054DK: VMEXWAIT cleared on the retry path |
| **I-267** | **Warm start never worked: every `SHUTDOWN` ended in WAIT 17 and every IPL said `NO WARM START DATA`.** DMKDMP (and DMKCKP's `SHUTDOWN REIPL` path) load DMKCKP's bootstrap with a read-IPL channel program of their own and enter it; the bootstrap (I-88) takes the IPL device from the subsystem id at X'B8', which only a real IPL leaves. After CP's own read it still named the last device to interrupt (06B1), so the checkpoint looked for DMKSAV on the wrong pack and gave up after 100 retries. | **fixed** 11 Oct — DMKDMP XA0055DK and DMKCKP XA0015DK put the IPL device's subsystem id (cached by XAIOFIND) at X'B8' before entering the bootstrap. `SHUTDOWN` now ends in `DMKCKP960I System WARM START data saved` / WAIT 8, and the next IPL warm starts by itself with the spool intact |
| **I-268** | **The I-233 `SR Rx,Rx` in front of `ICM Rx,7,field` zeroed the ICM's own base** when the field is addressed through Rx (`ICM R4,7,ACNTNEXT+1` under `USING ACNTBLOK,R4`; `ICM R7,7,1(R7)`): the ICM then read location 9. DMKCKP's accounting-chain walk looped on X'800' and filled the warm start area (`DMKCKP911W ... WARM START area full`, WAIT 7) -- the step after I-267. 22 sites in 15 modules (DMKCFS, DMKCKP, DMKDRD, DMKHVC, DMKIOG, DMKPER, DMKPTR, DMKRNH, DMKTAP, DMKTRK, DMKUSO, DMKVIO, DMKVSC), some of them reached only by rare paths. | **fixed** 11 Oct — `icm3base()` in build.py finds them (explicit base, or the nearest USING/DROP for Rx); those sites get `SLL Rx,8 / SRL Rx,8` *after* the ICM instead (logical shifts keep the condition code). Over-detection is harmless. Verified: shutdown, warm start, m5g-native at 256 MB |
| **I-269** | **`ATTACH` of a CTC at an address next to another CTC (600 as 600, then 601 as 601) took CP down with PRG005.** The second ATTACH fails (`... ALREADY DEFINED`), and building that message used DMKVDA's work-area address from SAVEWRK5, whose byte 0 holds the DMKVDC flags (`MVC SAVEWRK5(FLAGLEN),SAVEWRK9`): 20F5DF18. The I-126 sweep had masked eleven reloads of SAVEWRK5; three were left. | **fixed** 11 Oct — XA0055DK: `N Rx,XRIGHT24` after the other three. Now `CTCA 603 NOT ATTACHED; OPERATOR 603 ALREADY DEFINED`. (The automatic restart after the dump warm-started, thanks to I-267/I-268) |
| **I-270** | **`CP D` (display) after a CMS disabled wait gave FRE013.** Seen once during the M6 EDF work (11 Oct): CMS stopped in a disabled wait, and the next `CP D` storage display from the console abended CP with FRE013 (a free-storage error). Not reproduced since. | **open** -- needs a reproduction: a CMS disabled wait (e.g. a forced program check in a nucleus extension), then `CP D` of the PSA and of a virtual range above 16 MB |
| **I-271** | **A MODULE on an EDF (4K) disk crashes CMS when run:** `DMSITS136T SVC CALL FROM EDA0DA ILLEGALLY RE-ENTERS INTSVC. Probably DMSEFS's READ path issues an SVC (DMSFREE) while LOADMOD runs inside INTSVC. | **fixed** 12 Oct -- DMSEFS keeps a spare 8K buffer pair in the EDFCB (allocated at ACCESS), so OPENST and CLOSE need no DMSFREE/DMSFRET for the first open file. tc17: the PRIMES MODULE on the EDF disk runs |
| **I-272** | **CP hung at the LOGOFF of a 256 MB virtual machine that had been paging hard** (test tc19, 12 Oct, Hercules MAINSIZE 16). After it CP sat in an enabled wait (PSW 030E0000 00000000) with SIO count frozen, no I/O queued, and answered neither console 0009 nor a new terminal. Recovered with the Hercules `restart` command (DMKDMP908I SYSTEM FAILURE CODE PSA002, dump to the reader, warm start), then `CP SHUTDOWN` from 0009. Suspects: LOGOFF waiting for a page I/O completion that never comes, or a free-storage/page-slot release deadlock with a large VM. (The dump went to the spool and was lost to the next run's cold start.) | **open** -- reproduce with MAINSIZE 16, DEF STOR 256M, a paging job, then LOGOFF; with MAINSIZE 256 it did not occur (tc20, tc21) |
| **I-273** | **CMS file dates were 1970 in C programs** -- cmsrt's stat/fstat left st_mtime 0, so zip stored 01-01-1970 (tc64). | **fixed** (tc70): cmsrt reads the FST with STATE: DMSSTT leaves the address of its STATEFST copy in the plist at +28 (not in R1 -- tc69 showed R1 is restored by SVC 202); date MMDD/HHMM packed at +16/+18, year EBCDIC at +38 |
