| **I-276** | **A virtual reader read (SSCH, CCW READ) by an ESA/390 guest never completes** | 13 Oct, 9.25. Linux (vmur in VM/370 mode: one CCW READ X'02', 80 bytes, SLI, per card) read every card, then hung in D state on the read after the last one; the file was gone from the reader. **Cause:** at end of file DMKVSP ends the SIO at its first CCW with unit exception alone (no CE, no DE; S/370's CC 1, CSW stored), which XA0054DK turns into an I/O interruption. DMKVCS's BLDSCSW then set *primary* status only (secondary only with device end), and Linux's cio treats primary-without-secondary as intermediate status: no handler call, it waits for a device end that never comes. Found with the vmur s390dbf view (two reads `dstat=0c`, the third `ccw_device_start returned 0` and nothing else) and the subchannel entry in the DMKVCS table (CSW `01000050` = UE, residual 80, collected). **Fix:** BLDSCSW sets secondary status as well when the status has neither CE nor DE. pd14: `cat /dev/vmrdr-0.0.000c` reads the reader to the end (1,581,760 bytes = 19,772 cards over two files) and returns. | **fixed** |
| **I-277** | **A REXX EXEC called with 8 or more arguments ends CMS when the GCCLIB segment is loaded** (`DMSITP143T ADDRESSING EXCEPTION OCCURRED AT F2ACD0 IN SYSTEM ROUTINE EXEC`, or BREXX's `ABNORMAL TERMINATION ... DLMalloc aborted`, or garbage like `Error 58 running " V "`) | 13 Oct, found by LXCALL/RXPIP (9.25/9.26). `T16 a b c d e f g` works, `T16 a b c d e f g h` fails. **Not ours: stock VM/370 CE 1.1.2 (CE-ORIG, S/370, OPERATOR 2 MB, `IPL CMS`) fails the same way at the same address** (run ce1). Machines too big for the GCCLIB segment (16 MB: "Segment GCCLIB is not loaded") are fine, under the shared CMS, IPL 190 and IPL 290 alike. F2ACD0 is inside GCCLIB (segments 242-243, X'F20000'). Workaround in our EXECs: LXCALL takes its options from `LXCALL OPTIONS A`, so RXPIP calls it with at most 4 arguments. To report to the CE maintainers (GCCLIB/BREXX). 13 Oct, later: it does not reach users with the directory default (CMSUSER 16M: no GCCLIB segment); OPERATOR (2M) is affected. CE's GCCLIB CMSRUNTM allocates 520 bytes for argv (upstream 8babe46 has 32 bytes -- I-282), so the argv block is not the cause; the abend is inside dlmalloc (GCCLIB+X'ACD0' = MALLOC@@+X'9F0'), so something corrupts the heap first. | **FIXED** 13 Oct 13:28 (run i277). **Cause: CE's saved segment GCCLIB is stale.** It was saved from an older GCCLIB whose CMSRUNTM allocates 32 bytes for argv (8 pointers, I-282), while GCCLIB TEXT on 19E allocates 520. A machine of 16 MB or more never loads the segment and uses the TEXT, which is why only small machines failed. The segment pages on VM50-1 (cyl 9, pages 2-30) showed `00000020` at F20C84 where the TEXT has `00000208`. **Fix:** MAINT `DEF STOR 16M`, `IPL 190`, first command `ACCESS (NOPROF` (no profile, so BREXX is not loaded into the high free storage at F24000 that LOAD would overwrite: DMSFRE164T), `ACCESS 093 F`, `GCCLIBGN F20000` (LOAD CLEAR, SETKEY 13, SAVESYS). The new pages match the TEXT relocated to F20000 on the PC byte for byte (apart from our M5b CMSSYS patch), keys D4 as before. CMSUSER at 8M with the segment loaded: an EXEC with 11 and 12 arguments runs. To report upstream: re-run GCCLIBGN after NEWBREXX. |
| **I-278** | **A READ of an empty VM/370 reader by the ESA/390 guest lost the first card of the next file** | 13 Oct, pd18. vmspoold polled every 5 s; for a class D user QUERY FILES counts the whole system, so vmur's empty-reader check passed and each poll ended in unit check (intervention required). The next request then arrived without its first card (the request card), and was set aside as unknown. vmspoold now reads only when `QUERY READER ALL userid` lists a file of its own (as SQLSERV does): no lost cards since. The CP side (DMKVSP after intervention required) is not yet understood. | mitigated |
| **I-279** | **CP `PRG005` system failure, once** | 13 Oct 06:38, pd17, while OPERATOR (2 MB) had just hit `DMSSMN109S VIRTUAL STORAGE CAPACITY EXCEEDED` in RXPIP and Debian was building. The dump was 4 records long and VMFDUMP did not see it. Not reproduced in pd18/pd19 (8 MB OPERATOR, same work). | open |
| **I-280** | **vmur (VM/370 mode) ended a reader file after the first read() of a large file** | 13 Oct, pd19: BOOKIE GUIDE arrived with only its first 400 cards (one 32,000-byte read). vmur asks QUERY FILES before every read(), and the file being read is no longer counted, so the second read() returned 0 (end of file) and the rest of the file was lost when the device was closed. vmspoold now reads a whole file with one read() (up to 4 MB). Kernel #10 (linux57, the disk IPL now) asks QUERY FILES only at the start of a file (offset 0), and a unit check after a file is the end of the reader, not an I/O error. pd21: `cat` of a 3,000-card file in two reads gives all 240,000 bytes and rc 0. vmspoold also looks only at class A files (the monitor's MONDATA files, class M, cannot be read by a class A reader). | **fixed** |
| **I-281** | **Upload pipeline: a UTF-8 line shifted every following card** | 13 Oct: `awk printf %-80.80s` counts bytes, iconv then made a multi-byte character one byte, so the line was short and all later cards were misaligned. Convert UTF-8 to Latin-1 before padding (`iconv -f UTF-8 -t ISO-8859-1 | awk ... | iconv -t IBM1047`); mkbookie.js writes Latin-1 only. | fixed |
| **I-282** | **GCCLIB (upstream cms-370-gcclib 8babe46, and our GCCLIB31) allocates `MAXEPLISTARGS` = 32 bytes for argv -- 8 pointers -- and never checks the count.** A C program called with a 9th argument writes past the block and corrupts dlmalloc. CE's own GCCLIB binary allocates 520 bytes (found while chasing I-277), so CE is not affected. | **fixed** (mk31.py edit: `(ARGBUFFERLEN/2 + 2) * sizeof(char *)`); GCCLIB31 TXTLIB rebuilt on CMSUSER 195 by GCLB31 (run g31, 13 Oct 13:15, `tests/runs/gcclib31-args.json`): `M5G2 a b c d e f g h i j k l` prints all 13 argv entries. To report upstream. |
| **I-283** | **BREXX ended BC-mode machines with `DMSITP141T OPERATION EXCEPTION ... IN ROUTINE DMSREX`** (found by NICOF's TCPIPPXY, 3M, no `OPTION ECMODE`; the address, F35E36, is GCCLIB's CMSSYS). Since M5b, GCCLIB's CMSSYS masks with STNSM/SSM (mode-independent), and DMKPRV reflected STNSM/STOSM from a BC-mode machine as an operation exception (PSTSSM: "CF 370 PRINCIPLES OF OPERATION"). This was latent for BC-mode machines of 16M or more (GCCLIB TEXT) and became visible for small ones when the GCCLIB segment was re-saved (I-277). | **fixed** (XA0054DK, DMKPRV 00809000: STNSM/STOSM simulated in BC mode too; in BC mode the system mask is also the PSW's byte 0). Run bc, 13 Oct 15:05: CMSUSER with `SET ECMODE OFF`, 8M, GCCLIB segment, a REXX EXEC with 11 arguments runs. NICOF users also got `OPTION ECMODE`. |
| **I-284** | **Run http1 (13 Oct): CP's IPL ended in `HHCCP017I Machine check due to host error: User defined signal 1` (three times) and disabled wait 007; Hercules then hung in its own shutdown (all threads in futex waits, the HTTP console silent).** SIGUSR1 comes from Hercules 3.13's watchdog (impl.c): a started CPU whose instruction count did not move for 20 s. The NICOF outside proxy (Java) had been started 60 s after Hercules, i.e. during the IPL, and connects two 3270 sessions by LU name (groups NICOFPXY, TCPIPPXY); in ncf3 and kt25 it was started after the IPL and nothing happened. Suspected: a console.c lock held while the CPU thread waited. | open -- mitigation: start `nicof.sh` only after `AUTOLOG1 DONE`; the hung Hercules waits for the owner's OK to be ended |
| **I-285** | **NICOF socket programs wait forever under IPL 290** (HTTPD: `nicofclt_init` returned, the first socket call never came back); under IPL CMS they work. | 13 Oct, run i285. CP TRACE SVC EXT showed the VMCF answer (EXT 4001) arriving during the WAIT at F923F6, then nothing: no POST. NICOF's C handler takes the interrupt code from bytes 2-3 of the external old PSW in DMSITE's user area (+96). In the EC-mode CMS that PSW is an EC PSW, whose bytes 2-3 are zero; the code is at X'86'. So the handler never saw X'4001'. **Fix** (INTRAPI EXTHNDLR, arch/31bit/nicof/cegcc): hand the C handler a copy of the user area and, when the old PSW is EC (byte 1 X'08'), put X'86' into the copy's PSW bytes 2-3. Verified: HTTPD under IPL 290 at 16M and 64M and under IPL CMS. (The disabled wait after HX seen here is not NICOF's: I-286.) | **fixed** |
| **I-286** | **HX under the EC-mode CMS (IPL 290) ends in a disabled wait** (`DMSITP144T OPERATION EXCEPTION OCCURRED AT 0009EA IN ROUTINE -2 WHILE 'UFDBUSY' = 02, RE-IPL CMS`, wait PSW `00020000 60F8B97C`) | 13 Oct, runs i285b/i286. Also without NICOF: a REXX `do forever`, attention, HX, then any line at DMSABN's `CMS` prompt. Under IPL CMS the same steps work. CP TRACE BRANCH: in abend recovery DMSABN calls DMSITSR; its `LM BR,BR2,=A(DMSITS,DMSITS+4096)` took the literal through R6 (BR2, the second base, still in USING), because our EC cards grew DMSITS and moved the literal pool past DMSITSR's own `USING *,R15` reach. From DMSABN R6 is anything: BR (R5) came back 0, DMSFRET's error exit `B X'9E8'(R5)` went to low core. **Fix** (DMSITS XA0051DK): `LM BR,BR2,ITSRBAS-DMSITSR(R15)` with the two addresses in a DC right after DMSITSR's return. Verified i286: HX of a REXX loop and of HTTPD31 (GCC 13, NICOF), then `Q DISK` and NCTEST. | **fixed** |
| **I-287** | **CP stops dispatching after two Linux guests are coupled by virtual CTCAs** (9.12 experiment, run vc1): CP sits in its enabled wait, the 0009 console and 3270 logons take input but nothing is ever answered, the guests freeze | 14 Oct, run vc1. MAINT (Debian, real CTC 620/630) and CMSUSER (ramdisk Linux) each DEFINE CTCA 640 and 650 (641 is refused: one CTCA per virtual control unit), COUPLE 640 to the other's 650 and 650 to 640. Linux sees the devices as 3088-08 (650, defined after boot, needed `echo 0.0.00xx > /sys/bus/css/drivers_probe`: no channel report for a new device), ctcm groups them (`ctc1: setup OK`), then both sides log `The communication peer is busy` and ping gets no answer. A few minutes later CP stopped running anything: Hercules showed the CPU in CP's wait PSW, no I/O interrupt pending, the 3270 logo screen still came up, but no command got a reply (not even OPERATOR's SHUTDOWN on a 3270). Recovered with the Hercules RESTART key: `DMKDMP908I SYSTEM FAILURE; CODE PSA002`, CP re-IPLed, then the normal shutdown; every pack checked clean. To analyse: DMKVCA's extended-mode handshake with Linux ctcm, and what DMKVCA leaves on the dispatcher's queues. **Analysis 14 Oct, 02:00:** (1) DMKVCS holds its lock (VCSLOCK) over the SSCH it hands to DMKVSI; for a virtual CTCA, DMKVCA then waits for the other side (VCADSPCH, the user in IOWAIT, not in a wait PSW, so the lock is never stale), and the other guest's SSCH is backed up and retried for ever: the two adapters can never meet. (2) DMKVCA's cc 1 (attention+busy when both sides start, busy, unit check) comes back through DMKVIO SETCC1, not DMKVSJ TESTEC, so DMKVCSSE never sees it: the guest gets cc 1 for its SSCH, which Linux reports as -EBUSY, `The communication peer is busy`. (3) Still open: HSCH/CSCH of a virtual CTCA only queue the halt status and never reach DMKVCA (DMKVCASH), so an adapter halted in mid-transfer stays busy in DMKVCA. **Fix written (XA0054DK, untested):** VCSSSCH releases the lock before a virtual CTCA's SSCH goes to DMKVSI; DMKVIO SETCC1 sends an ESA/390 guest's CTCA cc 1 to the new DMKVCSC1, which queues the CSW as pending status (the SSCC1Q path, now the subroutine QSTAT), and the SSCH gets cc 0. **Runs vc2-vc7, 14 Oct 02:50-05:45** -- five more causes found and fixed, the link itself not yet up: (4) vc2 hung CP at MAINSIZE 16: **real-storage exhaustion** (DMKPTRFN 0, frame requests queued, nothing paging out, every user in page wait) -- the same as I-272; MAINSIZE 256 (the kit's) for these tests. (5) **DMKVCA checked real data addresses against DMKSYSRM (16 MB) and IDAW high bytes** -- every guest buffer in a frame above 16 MB (M4b.3) was a channel program check (ctcm `cs=20`, "A check occurred on the subchannel"): now DMKPGTXT and 31-bit IDAWs (XA0055DK). (6)-(7) **The scheduler never let a CTCA guest go idle**: VCSSSCH makes channels block multiplexers, and an SIO on a BMX channel flags its CU active (DMKVSI GETIOB) and the rescan in DMKVIO/DMKVSJ keeps VMIOACTV set while any CU or busy device is active -- a CTCA (virtual or the dedicated 3088 to the PC) always has a READ waiting, so the Linux guest held its queue slot (80 % storage), even halted (no DISABLED WAIT message), and the other guest starved in the eligible list: the special class is now treated like a terminal (XA0054DK). vc7: both guests alternate through the queue, VMIOACTV 0, Debian halts with the DISABLED WAIT message. **Still open:** ctcm on Debian says `setup OK` but never `Connected`; ping fails. The ramdisk guest ends in EXWAIT on a TPI whose TIO waits in a VM/370 "TIO busy loop" on the busy CTCA (TSCH/TPI must never block); possibly the VCHCEPND left set on the VCHBLOK by SSCC1Q/QSTAT (DMKDSP clears only the VCUBLOK's for a shared CU). HSCH/CSCH still never reach DMKVCA. | open (5 causes fixed) |
| **I-288** | **A disconnected Linux guest freezes** (kit test kt28, 14 Oct): when MAINT's terminal (the 1052 line on port 3270) dropped while Debian ran, MAINT went DSC and stopped -- RSTAT X'80' (VMCFWAIT, console function wait), no ssh, the spool bridge frozen (a PGSQL request waited in its reader). CP also marked the line `CONS 00A OFFLINE`; `VARY ONLINE 00A`, `ENABLE 00A`, `LOGON MAINT` (RECONNECT) and the guest ran on, the queued PGSQL answered. Linux's 3215 console read (getty on ttyS0) is simulated as a CMS-style synchronous console read that waits for a terminal. Fix: a disconnected ESA/390 guest's console read should stay pending (no CFWAIT), as z/VM does; workaround: keep the Linux console connected, or SET RUN ON / use the ramdisk-free console. **Fixed 14 Oct (i288v):** VM/370 stops a user whose line drops (DMKQCNTO: VMCFWAIT, logoff after 15 minutes; DMKCFM: CP READ, line break leaves VMCFWAIT). For an ESA/390 guest now: no VMCFWAIT, no 15-minute timer, CP mode's line break resumes the machine (XA0054DK, DMKQCN and DMKCFM); CMS users keep VM/370's rule. Verified: terminal killed under a running Debian -> MAINT DSC and running (RSTAT 08), ssh works, a console write works, CMSUSER PGSQL answered through the spool bridge; reconnect: VARY ONLINE 00A, ENABLE 00A with the client connected, LOGON MAINT, BEGIN. | **fixed** |
| **I-289** | **XL programs: fstat() of a CMS file passed CMS a plist above 16 MB** (9.35, JamVM on CMS, 14 Oct): cmsrt's `cms_mtime` built its STATE plist in a static, which an XL program (image above 16 MB) has above 16 MB; SVC 202 in AMODE 24 saw a truncated address and CMS printed `Invalid character ' ' in fileID '         MODULE'.` on every fstat, and st_mtime was 0. Fixed: the plist is in the low block (`L->dirpl`). XL programs built before 14 Oct (PY, CC1, CC1PLUS, ELF2CMS, XBASEC) still have it until they are relinked. | **fixed** |
| **I-290** | **CP abend PGS001 at LOGOFF after a large paging CMS user** (9.35, kt29 jv10, 14 Oct): CMSUSER (DEF STOR 256M, EC-mode CMS from 290) ran NetRexxC under JamVM (64 MB Java heap, about 200 MB of HIGHSTOR touched, real storage 15.7 MB, so heavy paging); `CP LOGOFF` ended in `DMKDMP908I SYSTEM FAILURE; CODE PGS001` and CP restarted itself (warm). PGS001 = DMKPGS PGOUTU: the owner's VMPAGES (resident page count) would go negative while the logoff releases its frames -- a frame owned by the user that was never counted, or one counted down twice. Earlier logoffs of 256M users (Python, tc63; JamVM hello) were clean. Reproduced with a plain C program (TOUCH, XL): touching 120 MB above 16 MB and logging off is clean, 140 MB gives PGS001, 200 MB PTR018 while paging. Cause: M4B.3 gives CP real frames up to 160 MB, so one user can hold about 36,000 resident frames, and VMPAGES is a halfword that LH reads signed: past 32767 it is negative and the next decrement abends. **Fixed 14 Oct (i290):** frames up to 128 MB (CPIBFT), so no count reaches 32768; the paging store above them ends at X'0FFFF000' (CPIBXT), at most 32767 slots, as VMPDRUM is a halfword too (DMKCPIB, XA0053DK). Verified on the dev system, MAINSIZE 256: TOUCH 140 and TOUCH 200, LOGOFF clean, SHUTDOWN clean. Needs kit build 16. | **fixed** |
| **I-291** | **A dedicated LCS (Hercules 3088-60) cannot run under CP** (9.12b step 1, runs lc2/lc3, 14 Oct): kernel #11 has the LCS driver. (1) CP answered SENSE ID for every CTCA from its own table (3088-08), so Linux bound ctcm to the LCS: **fixed** -- a dedicated device answers SENSE ID itself (DMKVCS SIDCHK, XA0054DK); now 640/650 show 3088/60 and bind to lcs. (2) Setting it online ends in `The LCS device stopped because of an error, dstat=0x0, cstat=0x20` (channel program check): Linux's LCS driver runs its read and write rings with CCW **suspend** flags, PCI and RSCH, changing the suspend flags in guest storage between resumes. CP translates a dedicated device's channel program into a real copy once (DMKCCW/DMKVCS) and supports neither suspend nor RSCH. (3) Linux 4.0's lcs error path then oopses in dev_err (lcs_new_device). Conclusion: the dedicated-LCS step needs suspend/resume in CP's translation, which a CP-simulated LCS (the Guest LAN target) gets for free, because CP reads the guest's own CCWs. | open |
| **I-292** | **Guest LAN: CP fails when two members exchange frames** (9.12b step C, run lb4, 14 Oct). MAINT Debian (kernel #11, LCS 640/641, MAC 02:f7:6c:88:06:40, the LANSTAT reply now 64 bytes) and CMSUSER's ramdisk Linux (kernel #11 from the reader, LCS 650/651) both bring up eth0. The first ping from CMSUSER (10.3.0.3 -> 10.3.0.2) takes CP down: CP re-IPLs itself and then loops in DMKCPI MOUNTALL on `HIO` cc=2 for the busy 3215 0009 (a second bug); a Hercules `restart` gives wait PSW 000E0000 00000004. The suspects are the cross-member path: VLSWITCH -> VLENQ on another member, VLKICK's CPEXBLOK, and DMKVLNPX pumping (VLPUT/TRANS) into a user that is not running. Next: find the abend code from the dump file after a warm start, and add checks. **Cause (lb5, breakpoint at DMKCPI+8):** `DMKDMP908I SYSTEM FAILURE; CODE FRE013` -- DMKDSP unstacked a CPEXBLOK whose address was 0: the dispatcher's CPEXBLOK chain was broken. The kick of another member stacked a CPEXBLOK (DMKVLNPX) that pumped the other user's read side with TRANS and static work fields while that user could itself be in DMKVCSEX (the lock let the same user in), so queue pointers and statics were used twice. **Fix:** no cross-user work at all -- VLKICK gives the other member's running read side a PCI interruption (CPA unchanged, so Linux takes no buffer) through DMKVCSC1 and DMKSCHDL, as DMKVCA wakes its partner; the member's own TSCH (or RSCH) pumps the frames in its own context. lb6: two guests ping each other. The re-IPL loop on 0009 after the dump did not recur. | fixed |
| **I-293** | **Debian's poweroff hangs after `Will now halt` when eth0 is on the guest LAN** (lb9, lb10; 15 Oct). Without LCS devices (lb12) the same Debian (kernel #11) halts; the ramdisk system with eth0 up, or after `ifconfig eth0 down`, powers off (lb6, lb11); `ifconfig eth0 down` on Debian takes 1.9 s. What differs is Debian's `/etc/init.d/halt`: `halt -d -f -i -p -h`, where `-i` takes every interface down after the rest of the system has stopped. **Workaround** on the Debian disk and in vm370plus-post.sh: `NETDOWN=no` in `/etc/default/halt` -- poweroff then ends in the disabled wait (lb13). Cause not yet found. | open (worked around) |
| **I-294** | **READCARD ends in an operation exception under `IPL 290`** (bi2, 15 Oct): `DMSITP141T OPERATION EXCEPTION OCCURRED AT 0844C2 IN ROUTINE READCARD` and `3594 ... DOUBLEWORDS OF SYSTEM STORAGE WERE NOT RECOVERED`, every time. Under `IPL CMS` READCARD works. Workaround: read cards under the classic CMS. | open |
| **I-295** | **Under `IPL 290` (EC-mode CMS) AUTOLOG's first command goes to CP** (bi2, 15 Oct). `CP AUTOLOG BORDER BORDER EXEC BORDER` with the directory's `IPL 290`: the virtual machine is in a CP read after the IPL (the same thing as the line-console "Enter after logon" seen in the test harness), so the first-VREAD data is taken as a CP command; `CP MSG OPERATOR ...` as first command works, `EXEC BORDER` is lost and CMS waits. Under `IPL CMS` the first command reaches CMS (CMSBATCH, WAKEUP). BORDER uses `IPL CMS` and a PROFILE EXEC. | open |

| **fixed** 11 Oct — the cause: DMKVSP's page-crossing move took the next page's address from `L R1,VSPCCW` (the CCW word, op code in byte 0) and `LA R1,1(R15,R1)` kept that byte in AMODE 31; TRANS masks to 24 bits only for a 24-bit guest, so for Linux the part of every card past a 4 KB boundary went 32 MB too high and the kernel image had holes (operation exception at X'10000', then the loop through an unset program new PSW). Found by comparing guest storage after the IPL loader with the kernel image. XA0055DK: `N R1,XRIGHT24`. Verified: `LINUX READER` boots to the login prompt. (`INVALID OPTION - KEEP` is the kernel's own `CHANGE RDR ALL KEEP NOHOLD`; harmless) | 1 |

| **worked around** 7 Oct 00:03 (w254): AUTOLOG1's PROFILE EXEC (AUTOLOG1 191) has `*P AUTOLOG CPWATCH CPWATCH` and `*P SET PRIOR CPWATCH 5` — the first letter only, so restoring is one character each. Porting CPWATCH (CPWSYS/CPWDSP ASSEMBLE on its 191) stays open. **11 Oct (4c), the mechanism:** CPWATCH's main loop is `SETIME n` (SVCTIMES: X'50' = n*76800) ... collect with DIAG 4 ... `WAIT` (SVCWAIT waits only if X'50' is still positive). Its virtual X'50' was negative from the first look after logon (-51323, then decreasing at 76800/s): every snapshot takes longer than its interval, so it never waits -- a busy loop by design, not a CP fault in the timer (UPVIRT moves X'50' at the right rate). The snapshot walks RDEVBLOKs with the stride `RDEVSIZE*8` of the DMKMAC it was assembled with (2005); our RDEVBLOK is not that size, and the device count is far above CE's. Sources now in `arch/31bit/cpwatch/orig` (punched from CPWATCH 191). Next: rebuild with CPWR6 CNTRL against VM/370plus's DMKMAC and time one snapshot | 1 |
| CP side **verified** 07:56 (w41): `DEF STOR 32M` → `STORAGE EXCEEDS ALLOWED MAXIMUM`, the directory gate. Raised to 256 MB and `DIRECT MODULE` regenerated with LOAD/GENMOD in the build (i206build). | 1 |

**Six of the twenty-three closed entries are retracted claims of my own** —
I-21, I-22, I-23, I-24, I-25 and I-27. Four were wrong in the project's favour,
I-23 was wrong against it, and I-27 was wrong in the flattering direction, which
is the one to watch. That ratio is itself worth watching: a review that only ever
finds good news is not reviewing.

The counts above are generated from the table by `tools/tally.py`, not
maintained. They had drifted to `fixed 68, closed 11, open 9, fix known 3` —
wrong by 24 entries — which is the same failure as every stale-artifact entry in
the table: a figure that reads as current because it is written down. `tally.py
--check` fails if the file needs rewriting.
